\Delta_{j}(q)\in\Delta(T^{o},q)
we have

\begin{array}[]{rcl}&&\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q);G_{l})+\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{l}}P,\Delta_{j}(q);G_{r})\\
&=&\hskip-5.69054pt\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q);G_{r})+\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{r}}P,\Delta_{j}(q);G_{l}).\end{array}

Proof.

It follows by Lemma
5.2
and Definition
5.11
.

\square

Lemma 5.13
.

Suppose that
P
can twist on a tile
G_{l}
such that
\mu_{G_{l}}P>P
. Then for any
\Delta_{i}(p)\in\Delta(T^{o},p)
,
\Delta_{j}(q)\in\Delta(T^{o},q)
we have

(1)

\begin{array}[]{rcl}&&\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q);G_{l})+\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{l}}P,\underline{\Delta_{j}(q)})\\
&=&\hskip-5.69054pt\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\underline{\Delta_{j}(q)})+\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j+1}(q);G_{l}).\end{array}

(2)

\begin{array}[]{rcl}&&\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q);G_{l})+\Omega(\underline{\Delta_{i}(p)},\mu_{G_{l}}P,\Delta_{j}(q))\\
&=&\hskip-5.69054pt\Omega(\underline{\Delta_{i}(p)},P,\Delta_{j}(q))+\Omega(\Delta_{i+1}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q);G_{l}).\end{array}

Proof.

We shall only prove (1) as the proof of (2) is similar.

By definition, we have

\begin{array}[]{rcl}&&\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q);G_{l})+\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{l}}P,\underline{\Delta_{j}(q)})\\
&=&\hskip-5.69054pt\Omega(P;G_{l})+d(\tau_{i_{l}})[m(\Delta_{j}(q);\tau_{i_{l}})-m(\Delta_{i}(p);\tau_{i_{l}})]\\
&-&\hskip-5.69054ptd(\tau_{j}(q))[m(\mu_{G_{l}}P;\tau_{j}(q))-n(G_{T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}};\tau_{j}(q))+m(\Delta_{i}(p);\tau_{j}(q))]\end{array}

and

\begin{array}[]{rcl}&&\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\underline{\Delta_{j}(q)})+\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j+1}(q);G_{l})\\
&=&\hskip-5.69054pt-d(\tau_{j}(q))[m(P;\tau_{j}(q))-n(G_{T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}};\tau_{j}(q))+m(\Delta_{i}(p);\tau_{j}(q))]\\
&+&\hskip-5.69054pt\Omega(P;G_{l})+d(\tau_{i_{l}})[m(\Delta_{j+1}(q);\tau_{i_{l}})-m(\Delta_{i}(p);\tau_{i_{l}})].\end{array}

We first consider the case that
b^{T^{o}}_{\tau_{i_{l}},\tau_{j}(q)}=0
. Then we have

m(P;\tau_{j}(q))=m(\mu_{G_{l}}P;\tau_{j}(q)),\hskip 14.22636ptm(\Delta_{j}(q);\tau_{i_{l}})=m(\Delta_{j+1}(q);\tau_{i_{l}}).

Thus the result follows in this case.

We then consider the case that
b^{T^{o}}_{\tau_{i_{l}},\tau_{j}(q)}\neq 0
. We may assume that
b^{T^{o}}_{\tau_{i_{l}},\tau_{j}(q)}>0
. As
\mu_{G_{l}}P>P
, the edge labeled
\tau_{j}(q)
of
G_{i_{l}}
is in
P
. Therefore, we have

m(P;\tau_{j}(q))=m(\mu_{G_{l}}P;\tau_{j}(q))-b^{T^{o}}_{\tau_{j}(q),\tau_{i_{l}}},\hskip 14.22636ptm(\Delta_{j}(q);\tau_{i_{l}})=m(\Delta_{j+1}(q);\tau_{i_{l}})-b^{T^{o}}_{\tau_{i_{l}},\tau_{j}(q)}.

Then the result follows by
d(\tau_{i_{l}})b^{T^{o}}_{\tau_{i_{l}},\tau_{j}(q)}=-d(\tau_{j}(q))b^{T^{o}}_{\tau_{j}(q),\tau_{i_{l}}}
, as
B(T^{o})
is skew-symmetrizable.

The proof is complete.

\square

Lemma 5.14
.

For any
(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))\in\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)})
we have

\begin{array}[]{rcl}&&\Omega(\underline{\Delta_{i}(p)},P,\Delta_{j}(q))+\Omega(\Delta_{i+1}(p),P,\underline{\Delta_{j}(q)})\\
&=&\hskip-5.69054pt\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\underline{\Delta_{j}(q)})+\Omega(\underline{\Delta_{i}(p)},P,\Delta_{j+1}(q)).\end{array}

Proof.

By definition, we have

\begin{array}[]{rcl}&&\Omega(\underline{\Delta_{i}(p)},P,\Delta_{j}(q))+\Omega(\Delta_{i+1}(p),P,\underline{\Delta_{j}(q)})\\
&=&\hskip-5.69054ptd(\tau_{i}(p))[m(P;\tau_{i}(p))-n(G_{T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}};\tau_{i}(p))+m(\Delta_{j}(q);\tau_{i}(p))]\\
&-&\hskip-5.69054ptd(\tau_{j}(q))[m(P;\tau_{j}(q))-n(G_{T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}};\tau_{j}(q))+m(\Delta_{i+1}(p);\tau_{j}(q))],\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{rcl}&&\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\underline{\Delta_{j}(q)})+\Omega(\underline{\Delta_{i}(p)},P,\Delta_{j+1}(q))\\
&=&\hskip-5.69054pt-d(\tau_{j}(q))[m(P;\tau_{j}(p))-n(G_{T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}};\tau_{j}(q))+m(\Delta_{i}(p);\tau_{j}(q))]\\
&+&\hskip-5.69054ptd(\tau_{i}(p))[m(P;\tau_{i}(p))-n(G_{T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}};\tau_{i}(p))+m(\Delta_{j+1}(q);\tau_{i}(p))],\end{array}

We first consider the case that
b^{T^{o}}_{\tau_{i}(p),\tau_{j}(q)}=0
. Then we have

m(\Delta_{i}(p);\tau_{j}(q))=m(\Delta_{i+1}(p);\tau_{j}(q)),\hskip 14.22636ptm(\Delta_{j}(q);\tau_{i}(p))=m(\Delta_{j+1}(q);\tau_{i}(p)).

Thus the result follows in this case.

We then consider the case that
b^{T^{o}}_{\tau_{i}(p),\tau_{j}(q)}\neq 0
. We may assume that
b^{T^{o}}_{\tau_{i}(p),\tau_{j}(q)}>0
. Then we have

m(\Delta_{i}(p);\tau_{j}(q))=m(\Delta_{i+1}(p);\tau_{j}(q))-b^{T^{o}}_{\tau_{j}(q),\tau_{i}(p)},\hskip 5.69054ptm(\Delta_{j}(q);\tau_{i}(p))=m(\Delta_{j+1}(q);\tau_{i}(p))-b^{T^{o}}_{\tau_{i}(p),\tau_{j}(q)}.

Then the result follows by
d(\tau_{i}(p))b^{T^{o}}_{\tau_{i}(p),\tau_{j}(q)}=-d(\tau_{j}(q))b^{T^{o}}_{\tau_{j}(q),\tau_{i}(p)}
, as
B(T^{o})
is skew-symmetrizable.

The proof is complete.

\square

Proposition 5.15
.

With the foregoing notation. Suppose that
p
and
q
are punctures and
T
contains no arcs tagged notched at
p
or
q
. There is a unique map

w:\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)})\to\mathbb{Z}

satisfies the following conditions:

(1) (Initial condition)
w({\bf P}_{-})=0
, where
{\bf P}_{-}
is the minimum element in
\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)})
,

(2) (Recurrence condition)

(a) For any
(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))
such that
(\Delta_{i+1}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))
covers
(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))
,

w(\Delta_{i+1}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))-w(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))=\Omega(\underline{\Delta_{i}(p)},P,\Delta_{j}(q)).

(b) For any
(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))
such that
(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j+1}(q))
covers
(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))
,

w(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j+1}(q))-w(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))=\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\underline{\Delta_{j}(q)}).

(c) For any
(\Delta_{i}(p),Q,\Delta_{j}(q)),(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))\in\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)})
such that
(\Delta_{i}(p),Q,\Delta_{j}(q))
covers
(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))
, in particular,
Q>P
are related by a twist on some tile
G_{l}
,

w(\Delta_{i}(p),Q,\Delta_{j}(q))-w(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))=\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q);G_{l}).

Proof.

The uniqueness holds as the Hasse graph of
\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)})
is connected (by Lemma
4.13
). We now show the existence. For any
\textbf{P}=(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))\in\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)})
, for any two chains
\textbf{P}_{0}=\textbf{P}_{-}<\textbf{P}_{1}<\cdots<\textbf{P}_{a}=\textbf{P}
and
\textbf{P}^{\prime}_{0}=\textbf{P}_{-}<\textbf{P}^{\prime}_{1}<\cdots<\textbf{P}^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}=\textbf{P}
such that
\textbf{P}_{i+1}
covers
\textbf{P}_{i}
and
\textbf{P}^{\prime}_{i+1}
covers
\textbf{P}^{\prime}_{i}
for all
i
, it suffices to show that
w_{1}(\textbf{P})=w_{2}(\textbf{P})
, where
w_{1}(\textbf{P})
(resp.
w_{2}(\textbf{P})
) is determined by the chain
\textbf{P}_{0}=\textbf{P}_{-}<\textbf{P}_{1}<\cdots<\textbf{P}_{a}=\textbf{P}
(resp.
\textbf{P}^{\prime}_{0}=\textbf{P}_{-}<\textbf{P}^{\prime}_{1}<\cdots<\textbf{P}^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}=\textbf{P}
).

If
\widetilde{\beta}\notin T^{o}
, denote

\Psi(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))=\begin{cases}\Psi(P,\Delta_{j}(q)),&\mbox{if $E_{1}(p)\in P$ and $i=1$},\\
\Psi(P,\Delta_{j}(q))\vartheta_{1}\vartheta_{2}\cdots\vartheta_{s-1}\vartheta_{s},&\mbox{if $E_{2}(p)\in P$ and $i=1$},\\
\Psi(P,\Delta_{j}(q))\vartheta_{1}\vartheta_{2}\cdots\vartheta_{i-1},&\mbox{otherwise},\end{cases}

where
E_{1}(p)
and
E_{2}(p)
are the edges of
G_{1}
determined by (
8
) in Section
4
and
\Psi(P,\Delta_{j}(q))
is given in the proof of Proposition
5.8
.

If
\widetilde{\beta}\in T^{o}
, denote

(13)

\Psi(\Delta_{i}(p),P_{\widetilde{\beta}},\Delta_{j}(q))=\begin{cases}1,&\mbox{if $i=1$ and $j=t$},\\
\vartheta_{1}\vartheta_{2}\cdots\vartheta_{s}\kappa_{1}\cdots\kappa_{t},&\mbox{if $i=s$ and $j=1$},\\
\vartheta_{1}\vartheta_{2}\cdots\vartheta_{i-1}\kappa_{1}\cdots\kappa_{j-1}\kappa_{t},&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Thus,
\Psi(\Delta,\mu_{G_{l}}Q,\Delta^{\prime})=\Psi(\Delta,Q,\Delta^{\prime})\chi_{l}
if
(\Delta,\mu_{G_{l}}Q,\Delta^{\prime})
covers
(\Delta,Q,\Delta^{\prime})
,
\Psi(\Delta,Q,\Delta_{l+1}(q))=\Psi(\Delta,Q,\Delta_{l}(q))\kappa_{l}
if
(\Delta,Q,\Delta_{l+1}(q))
covers
(\Delta,Q,\Delta_{l}(q))
, and
\Psi(\Delta_{l+1}(p),Q,\Delta^{\prime})=\Psi(\Delta_{l}(p),Q,\Delta^{\prime})\vartheta_{l}
if
(\Delta_{l+1}(p),Q,\Delta^{\prime})
covers
(\Delta_{l}(p),Q,\Delta^{\prime})
. It follows that
a=a^{\prime}
equals the exponent of
\Psi(\textbf{P})
. We say that
a=a^{\prime}
is the distance between
\textbf{P}_{-}
and
P
. We prove that
w_{1}(\textbf{P})=w_{2}(\textbf{P})
by induction on the distance
a
.

In case
a=0
we have
\textbf{P}=\textbf{P}_{-}
. It follows that
w_{1}(\textbf{P})=w_{2}(\textbf{P})=0
in this case. Assume that
w_{1}(\textbf{P})=w_{2}(\textbf{P})
holds for any case that the distance is less than
a
.

If
\textbf{P}_{a-1}=\textbf{P}^{\prime}_{a-1}
then
w_{1}(\textbf{P})-w_{1}(\textbf{P}_{a-1})=w_{2}(\textbf{P})-w_{2}(\textbf{P}_{a-1}).

Thus by the hypothesis we have

w_{1}(\textbf{P})=w_{2}(\textbf{P}).

We now consider the case that
\textbf{P}_{a-1}\neq\textbf{P}^{\prime}_{a-1}
. There are four possibilities:

(1)

\textbf{P}_{a-1}=(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{l}}P,\Delta_{j}(q))
and
\textbf{P}^{\prime}_{a-1}=(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{r}}P,\Delta_{j}(q))
for some
G_{l}
and
G_{r}
such that
P
can twist on
G_{l}
and
G_{r}
;

(2)

\textbf{P}_{a-1}=(\Delta_{i-1}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))
and
\textbf{P}^{\prime}_{a-1}=(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{l}}P,\Delta_{j}(q))
for some
G_{l}
such that
P
can twist on
G_{l}
;

(3)

\textbf{P}_{a-1}=(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j-1}(q))
and
\textbf{P}^{\prime}_{a-1}=(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{l}}P,\Delta_{j}(q))
for some
G_{l}
such that
P
can twist on
G_{l}
;

(4)

\textbf{P}_{a-1}=(\Delta_{i-1}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))
and
\textbf{P}^{\prime}_{a-1}=(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j-1}(q))
.

We shall only prove cases (1) (3) and (4) as cases (2) and (3) are dual.

For the case (1), we have
|l-r|>1
and
\textbf{P}_{a-1}
covers
(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{r}}\mu_{G_{l}}P,\Delta_{j}(q))
and
\textbf{P}^{\prime}_{a-1}
covers
(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{l}}\mu_{G_{r}}P,\Delta_{j}(q))
. Thus we have

\begin{array}[]{rcl}w_{1}(\textbf{P})&=&w_{1}(\textbf{P}_{a-1})+\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{l}}P,\Delta_{j}(q);G_{l})\\
&=&\hskip-5.69054pt\left(w_{1}(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{r}}\mu_{G_{l}}P,\Delta_{j}(q))+\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{r}}\mu_{G_{l}}P,\Delta_{j}(q);G_{r})\right)\\
&+&\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{l}}P,\Delta_{j}(q);G_{l})\\
&=&\hskip-5.69054ptw_{2}(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{l}}\mu_{G_{r}}P,\Delta_{j}(q))+(\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{l}}\mu_{G_{r}}P,\Delta_{j}(q);G_{l})\\
&+&\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{r}}P,\Delta_{j}(q);G_{r}))\\
&=&\hskip-5.69054ptw_{2}(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{r}}P,\Delta_{j}(q))+\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{r}}P,\Delta_{j}(q);G_{r}))\\
&=&\hskip-5.69054ptw_{2}(\textbf{P}^{\prime}_{a-1})+\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{r}}P,\Delta_{j}(q);G_{r}))\\
&=&\hskip-5.69054ptw_{2}(\textbf{P}),\end{array}

where the third equality follows by the induction hypothesis and Lemma
5.12
.

For the case (3), as
\textbf{P}=(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))
covers
\textbf{P}_{a-1}=(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{l}}P,\Delta_{j}(q))
, we have
E_{2}(q)\in P
and
j\neq 1
.
Thus,
E_{1}(q)\in\mu_{G_{l}}P
and
\textbf{P}_{a-1}=(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{l}}P,\Delta_{j}(q))
covers
(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{l}}P,\Delta_{j-1}(q))
by Lemma
4.12
. We also have that
l=c
and
j-1=1
can not hold simultaneously, since otherwise
\textbf{P}=(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))<\textbf{P}^{\prime}_{a-1}=(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j-1}(q))
.
Thus by Lemma
4.11
,
\textbf{P}^{\prime}_{a-1}=(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j-1}(q))
covers
(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{l}}P,\Delta_{j-1}(q))
.

Therefore,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}w_{1}(\textbf{P})&=&\hskip-5.69054ptw_{1}(\textbf{P}_{a-1})+\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{l}}P,\Delta_{j}(q);G_{l})\\
&=&\hskip-5.69054ptw_{1}(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{l}}P,\Delta_{j-1}(q))+\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{l}}P,\underline{\Delta_{j-1}(q)})\\
&+&\hskip-5.69054pt\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{l}}P,\Delta_{j}(q);G_{l})\\
&=&\hskip-5.69054ptw_{2}(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{l}}P,\Delta_{j-1}(q))+\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{l}}P,\Delta_{j-1}(q);G_{l})\\
&+&\hskip-5.69054pt\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\underline{\Delta_{j-1}(q)})\\
&=&\hskip-5.69054ptw_{2}(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j-1}(q))+\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\underline{\Delta_{j-1}(q)})\\
&=&\hskip-5.69054ptw_{2}(\textbf{P}^{\prime}_{a-1})+\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\underline{\Delta_{j-1}(q)})\\
&=&\hskip-5.69054ptw_{2}(\textbf{P}),\end{array}

where the third equality follows by induction hypothesis and Lemma
5.13
.

For the case (4), as
P
covers
\textbf{P}_{a-1}=(\Delta_{i-1}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))
and
\textbf{P}^{\prime}_{a-1}=(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j-1}(q))
, we have
\textbf{P}_{a-1}=(\Delta_{i-1}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))
and
\textbf{P}^{\prime}_{a-1}=(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j-1}(q))
covers
(\Delta_{i-1}(p),P,\Delta_{j-1}(q))
by Lemma
4.12
.

Therefore,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}w_{1}(\textbf{P})&=&\hskip-5.69054ptw_{1}(\textbf{P}_{a-1})+\Omega(\underline{\Delta_{i-1}(p)},\mu_{G_{l}}P,\Delta_{j}(q))\\
&=&\hskip-5.69054ptw_{1}(\Delta_{i-1}(p),P,\Delta_{j-1}(q))+\Omega(\Delta_{i-1}(p),P,\underline{\Delta_{j-1}(q)})\\
&+&\hskip-5.69054pt\Omega(\underline{\Delta_{i-1}(p)},\mu_{G_{l}}P,\Delta_{j}(q))\\
&=&\hskip-5.69054ptw_{2}(\Delta_{i-1}(p),P,\Delta_{j-1}(q))+\Omega(\underline{\Delta_{i-1}(p)},P,\Delta_{j-1}(q))\\
&+&\hskip-5.69054pt\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{l}}P,\underline{\Delta_{j-1}(q)})\\
&=&\hskip-5.69054ptw_{2}(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j-1}(q))+\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{l}}P,\underline{\Delta_{j-1}(q)})\\
&=&\hskip-5.69054ptw_{2}(\textbf{P}^{\prime}_{a-1})+\Omega(\Delta_{i}(p),\mu_{G_{l}}P,\underline{\Delta_{j-1}(q)})\\
&=&\hskip-5.69054ptw_{2}(\textbf{P}),\end{array}

where the third equality follows by induction hypothesis and Lemma
5.14
.

The proof is complete.

\square

6.
Expansion formulas and the positivity

Let
\Sigma
be an orbifold and
\mathcal{A}_{v}(\Sigma)
be a quantum cluster algebra arising from
\Sigma
. Let
\beta
be an arc in
\Sigma
and
T^{o}
be an ideal triangulation. Let
\widetilde{\beta}
be the curve associated with
\beta
given by (
4
). Let
T
be the corresponding tagged triangulation. Assume that
\widetilde{\beta}
starts from
p
and ends at
q
. In this section using the lattices
\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta})
,
\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(q)})
and
\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)})
, respectively in Section
4
, we give expansion formulas for quantum cluster variables
\widetilde{\beta}
,
\widetilde{\beta}^{(q)}
and
\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)}
, respectively. As a corollary, we obtain the positivity for quantum cluster algebras from orbifolds.

6.1.
Expansion formula for ordinary arcs.

Definition 6.1
.

[
34
]
Suppose that the diagonal of
G_{T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}}
are labeled
\tau_{i_{1}},\cdots,\tau_{i_{c}}
. Let
P
be a perfect matching of
G_{T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}}
with edges labeled by
\tau_{k_{1}},\cdots,\tau_{k_{r}}
. Suppose that
P\ominus P_{-}
encloses the union of tiles
\bigcup_{j\in J}G_{j}
for some
J\subseteq\{1,\cdots,c\}
.

(1)

The
weight

x^{T}(P)
of
P
is defined to be

x^{T}(P):=\frac{x_{\tau_{k_{1}}}\cdots x_{\tau_{k_{r}}}}{x_{\tau_{i_{1}}}\cdots x_{\tau_{i_{c}}}},

(2)

The
height monomial

h^{T}(P)
of
P
is defined to be

h^{T}(P):=\prod_{j\in J}h^{T}_{i_{j}},

(3)

The
specialized height monomial

y^{T}(P)
of
P
is defined to be

y^{T}(P):=\Phi(h^{T}(P)),

where
\Phi
is given by

(14)

\Phi(h^{T}_{\tau_{i}})=\begin{cases}y^{T}_{\tau_{i}},&\mbox{if $\tau_{i}$ is not a side of a self-folded triangle},\\
\frac{y^{T}_{r}\vskip 5.69054pt}{\vskip 5.69054pty^{T}_{r^{(p)}}},&\mbox{if $\tau_{i}$ is a radius $r$ to puncture $p$ in a self-folded triangle},\\
y^{T}_{r^{(p)}},&\mbox{if $\tau_{i}$ is a loop in a self-folded triangle with radius $r$ to a puncture $p$.}\end{cases}

(4)

The
quantum weight

X^{T}(P)
of
P
is defined to be the element
X^{a(P)}
such that

X^{a(P)}|_{v=1}=\frac{x^{T}(P)y^{T}(P)}{\bigoplus_{R\in\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}})}y^{T}(R)}.

In particular, if
\widetilde{\beta}\in T^{o}
we have
x^{T}(P_{\widetilde{\beta}})=x_{\widetilde{\beta}},y^{T}(P_{\widetilde{\beta}})=1
, where
P_{\widetilde{\beta}}
is the unique perfect matching of
G_{T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}}
.

For an arc
\gamma
incident to a puncture
p
, we let the weight
x_{l_{q}(\gamma)}=x_{\gamma}x_{\gamma^{(q)}}
.

Remark 6.2
.

(1)

Note that the weight of a perfect matching slightly differs from the original definition in
[
34
]
, here we quotient the crossing monomial.

(2)

For convenience, we denote

y^{T^{o}}_{\tau_{i}}=\begin{cases}y^{T}_{\tau_{i}},&\mbox{if $\tau_{i}$ is not a side of a self-folded triangle},\\
\frac{y^{T}_{r}\vskip 5.69054pt}{\vskip 5.69054pty^{T}_{r^{(p)}}},&\mbox{if $\tau_{i}$ is a radius $r$ to puncture $p$ in a self-folded triangle},\\
y^{T}_{r^{(p)}},&\mbox{if $\tau_{i}$ is a loop in a self-folded triangle with radius $r$ to a puncture $p$.}\end{cases}

Then
\Phi(h^{T}_{\tau_{i}})=y^{T^{o}}_{\tau_{i}}.

For
i\in\{1,2,3,4\}
, if
\alpha_{i}
is a radius of some self-folded triangle in
T^{o}
, then
\alpha
is the loop.

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
T^{o}
be an ideal triangulation and
T
be the corresponding tagged triangulation. For any arc
\alpha\in T^{o}
which can be flipped, denote
T^{\prime o}=(T^{o}\setminus\{\alpha\})\cup\{\alpha^{\prime}\}
be flipped ideal triangulation and
T^{\prime}
the corresponding tagged triangulation. Suppose that
\alpha
is a diagonal of the quadrilateral
(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4})
in
T^{o}
and
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{3}
are in the clockwise direction of
\alpha
. Then

(1)

if
\tau\neq\alpha,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4}
, then
y^{T^{\prime o}}_{\tau}=y^{T^{o}}_{\tau}
,

(2)

y^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}=(y^{T^{o}}_{\alpha})^{-1}
,

(3)

For
i\in\{1,2,3,4\}
,

(a)

if
\alpha_{i}
is not a radius of any self-folded triangles in
T^{o}
or
T^{\prime o}
, i.e.,
\alpha_{i}\neq\alpha_{i-1},\alpha_{i+1}
, then we have

y_{\alpha_{i}}^{T^{o}}=y_{\alpha_{i}}^{T^{\prime o}}(y_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{T^{\prime o}})^{[-b^{T^{o}}_{\alpha\alpha_{i}}]_{+}}(1\oplus y_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{T^{\prime o}})^{b^{T^{o}}_{\alpha\alpha_{i}}},

(b)

if
\alpha_{i}
is a radius of some self-folded triangle in
T^{\prime o}
to some puncture
p
, then we have

y_{\alpha_{i}}^{T^{o}}=y_{\alpha_{i}}^{T^{\prime o}}y_{l_{p}(\alpha_{i})}^{T^{\prime o}},

(4)

x_{\alpha}=\frac{x_{\alpha_{2}}x_{\alpha_{4}}+x_{\alpha_{1}}x_{\alpha_{3}}y^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}{x_{\alpha^{\prime}}\cdot(1\oplus y^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}})}
.

Proof.

(1) It is clear.

(2) It is clear if
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}
are not sides of self-folded triangles. We may assume one of
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}
is a side of a self-folded triangle, without loss of generality, we may assume that
\alpha
is a loop of a self-folded triangle with the radius
\alpha_{3}=\alpha_{4}
to a puncture
p
. Then
y^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}=y^{T^{\prime}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}=(y^{T}_{\alpha_{3}^{(p)}})^{-1}=(y^{T^{o}}_{\alpha})^{-1}
.

(3) We may assume
i=1
.
For the statement (a), if
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha
are not a side of a self-folded triangle in
T^{o}
or
T^{\prime o}
, then

y^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha_{1}}=y^{T^{\prime}}_{\alpha_{1}}=y^{T}_{\alpha_{1}}(y^{T}_{\alpha})^{b^{T^{o}}_{\alpha\alpha_{1}}}(1\oplus y^{T^{o}}_{\alpha})^{-b^{T^{o}}_{\alpha\alpha_{1}}}=y^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1}}(1\oplus(y^{T}_{\alpha})^{-1})^{-b^{T^{o}}_{\alpha\alpha_{1}}}=y^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1}}(1\oplus y^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}})^{-b^{T^{o}}_{\alpha\alpha_{1}}}.

If
\alpha_{1}
is not a side of a self-folded triangle in
T^{o}
or
T^{\prime o}
but
\alpha
is a side of a self-folded triangle, then
\alpha_{2}=\alpha_{3}
is the radius to some puncture
p
, then

y^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha_{1}}=y^{T^{\prime}}_{\alpha_{1}}=y^{T}_{\alpha_{1}}(y^{T}_{\alpha_{2}^{(p)}})^{b^{T}_{\alpha_{2}^{(p)}\alpha_{1}}}(1\oplus y^{T}_{\alpha_{2}^{(p)}})^{-b^{T}_{\alpha_{2}^{(p)}\alpha_{1}}}=y^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1}}(1\oplus(y^{T^{o}}_{\alpha})^{-1})^{-b^{T^{o}}_{\alpha\alpha_{1}}}=y^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1}}(1\oplus y^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}})^{-b^{T^{o}}_{\alpha\alpha_{1}}}.

If
\alpha_{1}
is a side of a self-folded triangle in
T^{o}
, but
\alpha
is not a side of a self-folded triangle in
T^{o}
, then
\alpha_{1}
is a loop of a self-folded triangle with radius
r
to a puncture
p
. Thus we have

y^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha_{1}}=y^{T^{\prime}}_{r^{(p)}}=y^{T}_{r^{(p)}}(y^{T}_{\alpha})^{b^{T}_{\alpha r^{(p)}}}(1\oplus y^{T}_{\alpha})^{-b^{T}_{\alpha r^{(p)}}}=y^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1}}(1\oplus(y^{T^{o}}_{\alpha})^{-1})^{-b^{T^{o}}_{\alpha\alpha_{1}}}=y^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1}}(1\oplus y^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}})^{-b^{T^{o}}_{\alpha\alpha_{1}}}.

If
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha
are sides of some self-folded triangles in
T^{o}
, then
\alpha_{1},\alpha
are loops of some self-folded triangles. Assume that
\alpha_{2}=\alpha_{3}
is the radius to some puncture
p
and
\alpha_{1}
is the loop of a self-folded triangle with radius
r
to a puncture
q
. Thus we have

y^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha_{1}}=y^{T^{\prime}}_{r^{(q)}}=y^{T}_{r^{(q)}}(y^{T}_{\alpha_{2}^{(p)}})^{b^{T}_{\alpha_{2}^{(p)}r^{(p)}}}(1\oplus y^{T}_{\alpha_{2}^{(p)}})^{-b^{T}_{\alpha_{2}^{(p)}r^{(p)}}}=y^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1}}(1\oplus(y^{T^{o}}_{\alpha})^{-1})^{-b^{T^{o}}_{\alpha\alpha_{1}}}=y^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1}}(1\oplus y^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}})^{-b^{T^{o}}_{\alpha\alpha_{1}}}.

The result follows.

The statement (b) follows immediately by
y_{\alpha_{1}}^{T^{\prime}}=y_{\alpha_{1}}^{T}
.

(4) As
x_{\alpha^{\prime}}=\frac{y^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}x_{\alpha_{2}}x_{\alpha_{4}}+x_{\alpha_{1}}x_{\alpha_{3}}}{x_{\alpha}\cdot(1\oplus y^{T^{o}}_{\alpha})}
, we have

x_{\alpha}=\frac{y^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}x_{\alpha_{2}}x_{\alpha_{4}}+x_{\alpha_{1}}x_{\alpha_{3}}}{x_{\alpha^{\prime}}\cdot(1\oplus y^{T^{o}}_{\alpha})}=\frac{x_{\alpha_{2}}x_{\alpha_{4}}+(y^{T^{o}}_{\alpha})^{-1}x_{\alpha_{1}}x_{\alpha_{3}}}{x_{\alpha^{\prime}}\cdot((y^{T^{o}}_{\alpha})^{-1}\oplus 1)}=\frac{x_{\alpha_{2}}x_{\alpha_{4}}+x_{\alpha_{1}}x_{\alpha_{3}}y^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}{x_{\alpha^{\prime}}\cdot(1\oplus y^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}})}.

\square

The following lemma follows by Definition
6.1
(2) (3).

Lemma 6.4
.

Let
P,Q
be two perfect matchings. If
P<Q
are related by a twist on a tile with diagonal labeled
\tau_{i}
then

\frac{y^{T}(Q)}{y^{T}(P)}=y^{T^{o}}_{\tau_{i}}.

The following theorem is the first main result of this paper.

Theorem 6.5
.

Let
\Sigma
be an orbifold without orbifold points of weight
2
. Let
T^{o}
be an ideal triangulation of
\Sigma
and
\beta
be an arc. Then in the quantum cluster algebra
\mathcal{A}_{v}(\Sigma)
we have

X_{\beta}=\sum_{P\in\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta})}v^{w(P)}X^{T}(P),

where
w:\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta})\to\mathbb{Z}
is given by Proposition
5.3
.

6.2.
Expansion formula for one end tagged notched arcs.

Suppose that
q
is a puncture and
q\neq p
. Thus
\widetilde{\beta}=\beta
. Assume
T
contains no arc tagged notched at
q
. In this section, we give a quantum expansion formula for
\beta^{(q)}=\widetilde{\beta}^{(q)}
.

For any triangle
\Delta_{j}(q)
incident to
q
, recall that the three sides are
\tau_{j-1}(q),\tau_{j}(q)
and
\tau_{[j]}(q)
.

Definition 6.6
.

With the notation in Section
4.2
. For any
(P,\Delta_{j}(q))\in\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(q)})
with
P\in\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}})
and
j\in\{1,\cdots,t\}
,

(1)

The
weight

x^{T}(\Delta_{j}(q))
of
\Delta_{j}(q)
is defined to be

x^{T}(\Delta_{j}(q)):=x^{-1}_{\tau_{j-1}(q)}x_{\tau_{[j]}(q)}x^{-1}_{\tau_{j}(q)}.

(2)

The
weight

x^{T}(P,\Delta_{j}(q))
of
(P,\Delta_{j}(q))
is defined to be

(15)

x^{T}(P,\Delta_{j}(q)):=x^{T}(P)x^{T}(\Delta_{j}(q))=x^{T}(P)x^{-1}_{\tau_{j-1}(q)}x_{\tau_{[j]}(q)}x^{-1}_{\tau_{j}(q)}.

(3)

The
height monomial

h^{T}(P,\Delta_{j}(q))
of
(P,\Delta_{j}(q))
is defined as follows:

(a)

if
\widetilde{\beta}(=\beta)\notin T^{o}
then

(16)

h^{T}(P,\Delta_{j}(q)):=\begin{cases}h^{T}(P),&\mbox{if $E_{1}(q)\in P$ and $j=1$},\\
h^{T}(P)h^{T}_{\tau_{1}(q)}h^{T}_{\tau_{2}(q)}\cdots h^{T}_{\tau_{t-1}(q)}h^{T}_{\tau_{t}(q)},&\mbox{if $E_{2}(q)\in P$ and $j=1$},\\
h^{T}(P)h^{T}_{\tau_{1}(q)}h^{T}_{\tau_{2}(q)}\cdots h^{T}_{\tau_{j-1}(q)},&\mbox{otherwise},\end{cases}

where
E_{1}(q)
and
E_{2}(q)
are the edges of
G_{c}
determined by (
6
) in Section
4
.

(b)

if
\widetilde{\beta}(=\beta)\in T^{o}
then

h^{T}(P,\Delta_{j}(q)):=h^{T}_{\tau_{1}(q)}h^{T}_{\tau_{2}(q)}\cdots h^{T}_{\tau_{j-1}(q)}.

(4)

The
specialized height monomial

y^{T}(P,\Delta_{j}(q))
of
(P,\Delta_{j}(q))
is defined to be

(17)

y^{T}(P,\Delta_{j}(q)):=\Phi(h^{T}(P,\Delta_{j}(q))),

where
\Phi
is given by (
14
) in Definition
6.1
.

(5)

The
quantum weight

X^{T}(P,\Delta_{j}(q))
of
(P,\Delta_{j}(q))
is defined to be the element
X^{a(P,\Delta_{j}(q))}
such that

X^{a(P,\Delta_{j}(q))}|_{v=1}=\frac{x^{T}(P,\Delta_{j}(q))y^{T}(P,\Delta_{j}(q))}{\bigoplus_{(R,\Delta)\in\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(q)})}y^{T}(R,\Delta)}.

The following lemmas follow by Definition
6.6
.

Lemma 6.7
.

Assume
P>Q
are related by a twist on a tile
G
with diagonal labeled
\tau
and
(P,\Delta_{j}(q))
covers
(Q,\Delta_{j}(q))
. Then
\frac{y^{T}(P,\Delta_{j}(q))}{y^{T}(Q,\Delta_{j}(q))}=y^{T^{o}}_{\tau}

Lemma 6.8
.

If
(P,\Delta_{j+1}(q))
covers
(P,\Delta_{j}(q))
then

\frac{y^{T}(P,\Delta_{j+1}(q))}{y^{T}(P,\Delta_{j}(q))}=y^{T^{o}}_{\tau_{j}(q)}.

The following theorem is the second main result of this paper.

Theorem 6.9
.

Let
\Sigma
be an orbifold without orbifold points of weight
2
. Let
T^{o}
be an ideal triangulation of
\Sigma
and
T
be the corresponding tagged triangulation. For any
\beta
, let
\widetilde{\beta}
be the curve associated with
\beta
by (
4
). Assume that
\widetilde{\beta}
connects
p
and a puncture
q
with
q\neq p
. If
T
contains no arc tagged notched at
q
, then in the quantum cluster algebra
\mathcal{A}_{v}(\Sigma)
we have

X_{\beta^{(q)}}=\sum_{(P,\Delta_{j}(q))\in\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(q)})}v^{w(P,\Delta_{j}(q))}X^{T}(P,\Delta_{j}(q)),

where
w:\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(q)})\to\mathbb{Z}
is given by Proposition
5.8
.

6.3.
Expansion formula for two ends tagged notched arcs.

Suppose that
p
and
q
are punctures. Assume that
T
contains no arc tagged notched at
p
or
q
.
If
\beta
is a pending arc incident to puncture
p
, let
X_{\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)}}=X_{\beta^{(p)}}
. Herein, we give an expansion formula for
\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)}
.

For any triangle
\Delta_{i}(p)
incident to
p
, recall that the three sides are
\tau_{i-1}(p),\tau_{i}(p)
and
\tau_{[i]}(p)
.

Definition 6.10
.

With the notation in Section
4.3
. Assume that
p
and
q
are punctures. For any
(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))\in\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)})
with
P\in\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}})
and
i\in\{1,\cdots,s\},j\in\{1,\cdots,t\}
,

(1)

The
weight

x^{T}(\Delta_{i}(p))
of
\Delta_{i}(p)
is defined to be

x^{T}(\Delta_{i}(p)):=x^{-1}_{\tau_{i-1}(p)}x_{\tau_{[i]}(p)}x^{-1}_{\tau_{i}(p)}.

(2)

The
weight

x^{T}(\Delta_{j}(q))
of
\Delta_{j}(q)
is defined to be

x^{T}(\Delta_{j}(q)):=x^{-1}_{\tau_{j-1}(q)}x_{\tau_{[j]}(q)}x^{-1}_{\tau_{j}(q)}.

(3)

The
weight

x^{T}\left(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q)\right)
of
(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))
is defined to be

(18)

x^{T}\left(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q)\right):=x^{-1}_{\tau_{i-1}(p)}x_{\tau_{[i]}(p)}x^{-1}_{\tau_{i}(p)}x^{T}(P)x^{-1}_{\tau_{j-1}(q)}x_{\tau_{[j]}(q)}x^{-1}_{\tau_{j}(q)}.

(4)

The
height monomial

h^{T}(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))
of
(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))
is defined as follows:

(a)

if
\widetilde{\beta}\notin T^{o}
then

h^{T}(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q)):=\begin{cases}h^{T}(P,\Delta_{j}(q)),&\mbox{if $E_{1}(p)\in P$ and $i=1$},\\
h^{T}(P,\Delta_{j}(q))h^{T}_{\tau_{1}(p)}h^{T}_{\tau_{2}(p)}\cdots h^{T}_{\tau_{s-1}(p)}h^{T}_{\tau_{s}(p)},&\mbox{if $E_{2}(p)\in P$ and $i=1$},\\
h^{T}(P,\Delta_{j}(q))h^{T}_{\tau_{1}(p)}h^{T}_{\tau_{2}(p)}\cdots h^{T}_{\tau_{i-1}(p)},&\mbox{otherwise},\end{cases}

where
E_{1}(p)
is the edge of
G_{1}
determined by (
8
) in Section
4
.

(b)

if
\widetilde{\beta}\in T^{o}
and
s,t\geq 2
then

(19)

h^{T}(\Delta_{i}(p),P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q)):=\begin{cases}1,&\mbox{if $i=1$ and $j=t$},\\
h^{T}_{\tau_{1}(p)}h^{T}_{\tau_{2}(p)}\cdots h^{T}_{\tau_{s-1}(p)}h^{T}_{\tau_{1}(q)}\cdots h^{T}_{\tau_{t-1}(q)}(h^{T}_{\beta})^{2},&\mbox{if $i=s$ and $j=1$},\\
h^{T}_{\tau_{1}(p)}h^{T}_{\tau_{2}(p)}\cdots h^{T}_{\tau_{i-1}(p)}h^{T}_{\tau_{1}(q)}\cdots h^{T}_{\tau_{j-1}(q)}h^{T}_{\beta},&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(5)

The
specialized height monomial

y^{T}(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))
of
(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))
is defined to be

y^{T}(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q)):=\Phi(h^{T}(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))),

where
\Phi
is given by (
14
) in Definition
6.1
.

(6)

The
quantum weight

X^{T}(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))
of
(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))
is defined to be the element
X^{a(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))}
such that

X^{a(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))}|_{v=1}=\frac{x^{T}(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))y^{T}(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))}{{\bigoplus_{(\Delta,R,\Delta^{\prime})\in\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)})}y^{T}(\Delta,R,\Delta^{\prime})}}.

From the definition of weight
x^{T}\left(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q)\right)
and
x^{T}\left(P,\Delta_{j}(q)\right)
, we have

x^{T}\left(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q)\right)=x^{T}(\Delta_{i}(p))x^{T}\left(P,\Delta_{j}(q)\right).

The following lemmas follow by Definition
6.10
(4) (5).

Lemma 6.11
.

Let
P,Q
be two perfect matchings such that
P>Q
are related by a twist on a tile
G
with diagonal labeled
\tau
. If
(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))
covers
(\Delta_{i}(p),Q,\Delta_{j}(q))
then

\frac{y^{T}(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))}{y^{T}(\Delta_{i}(p),Q,\Delta_{j}(q))}=y^{T^{o}}_{\tau}.

Lemma 6.12
.

If
(\Delta_{i+1}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))
covers
(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))
then

\frac{y^{T}(\Delta_{i+1}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))}{y^{T}(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))}=y^{T^{o}}_{\tau_{i}(p)}.

Lemma 6.13
.

If
(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j+1}(q))
covers
(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))
then

\frac{y^{T}(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j+1}(q))}{y^{T}(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))}=y^{T^{o}}_{\tau_{j}(q)}.

The following theorem is the third main result of this paper.

Theorem 6.14
.

Let
\Sigma
be an orbifold without orbifold points of weight
2
. Let
T^{o}
be an ideal triangulation of
\Sigma
and
\beta
be an arc. Let
T
be the corresponding tagged triangulation of
T^{o}
. Let
\widetilde{\beta}
be the curve associated with
\beta
given by (
4
). Assume that
\widetilde{\beta}
connects two punctures
p
and
q
.
If
T
contains no arc tagged notched at
p
or
q
, then in the quantum cluster algebra
\mathcal{A}_{v}(\Sigma)
we have

X_{\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)}}=\sum_{(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))\in\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)})}v^{w(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))}X^{T}(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q)),

where
w:\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)})\to\mathbb{Z}
is given by Proposition
5.15
.

As an immediate corollary of Theorem
6.5
, Theorem
6.9
, and Theorem
6.14
, we obtain the positivity for quantum cluster algebras from orbifolds.

Theorem 6.15
.

Positivity conjecture holds for quantum cluster algebras from orbifolds without orbifold points of weight
2
.

Remark 6.16
.

In case
\Sigma
be an orbifold with or without orbifold points of weight
2
, our method can be also applied to its associated orbifold
\hat{\Sigma}
, which is obtained from
\Sigma
by making all orbifold points of weight
2
to punctures.

7.
Partition bijection between perfect matchings

Let
T^{o}
be an ideal triangulation and
\gamma
be a curve connecting two marked points
p
and
q
. Choose an orientation of
\gamma
, assume that
\gamma
crosses
T^{o}
at
p_{1},\cdots,p_{c}
sequentially. Denote
p_{0}=p
and
p_{c+1}=q
. Suppose that
p_{j}\in\tau_{i_{j}}
for
j=1,\cdots,c
. Given a non-self-folded arc
\alpha\in T^{o}
, denote
T^{\prime o}=\mu_{\alpha}(T^{o})
and by
\alpha^{\prime}
the new arc obtained.

In this section we construct a partition bijection
\varphi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}:\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{o},\gamma})\to\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{\prime o},\gamma})
. To this end, we first construct a partition bijection
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}:\mathcal{CP}(T^{o},\gamma)\to\mathcal{CP}(T^{\prime o},\gamma)
between the complete
(T^{o},\gamma)
-paths.

We start with the polygon case.

7.1.
Polygon case

In this subsection, let
\Sigma
be the
n
-gon, i.e., the disk with
n
marked points labeled
1,\cdots,n
clockwise on the boundary.

7.1.1.
Partition bijection
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}

For any complete
(T^{o},\gamma)
-path
\overrightarrow{\xi}
and
\zeta\in T^{o}
, recall
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\zeta)
given in (
2
). In the case that
\Sigma
is a polygon, we have
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\zeta)\in\{1,0,-1\}
.

Assume that
\alpha=(a,c)
is the diagonal of the quadrilateral
(a,b,c,d)
in
T^{o}
, where
a,b,c,d
are in the clockwise order. Then
\alpha^{\prime}=(b,d)
.

Let
\overrightarrow{\xi}=(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt1},\cdots,\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2c+1})
be a complete
(T^{o},\gamma)
-path. We would like to construct a set of complete
(T^{\prime o},\gamma)
-paths as follows.

Case I:
\gamma
crosses with two parallel edges of the quadrilateral
(a,b,c,d)
. We may assume that
\gamma
crosses
\cdots,(a,b),(a,c),(c,d),\cdots
sequentially. Then
\xi_{2j-2}=(a,b),\xi_{2j}=(a,c)
and
\xi_{2j+2}=(c,d)
for some
j\in\{2,\cdots,c-1\}
.

(1) If
\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j}=(a,c)
then
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+2})=((a,b),(b,a),(a,c),(c,d),(d,c))
. We have
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha)=-1
.

In this case we replace
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+2})
by
((a,b),(b,d),(d,b),(b,d),(d,c))
in the sequence
\overrightarrow{\xi}
.

(2) If
\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j}=(c,a)
then there are four possibilities of
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+2})
:

((b,a),(a,c),(c,a),(a,c),(c,d))
,
((b,a),(a,c),(c,a),(a,d),(d,c))
,
((a,b),(b,c),(c,a),(a,c),(c,d))
and
((a,b),(b,c),(c,a),(a,d),(d,c))
.

(2.1) In case
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+2})=((b,a),(a,c),(c,a),(a,c),(c,d))
, we have
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha)=1
.

In this case we replace
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+2})
by
((b,a),(a,d),(d,b),(b,c),(c,d))
or
((b,a),(a,b),(b,d),(d,c),(c,d))
in the sequence
\overrightarrow{\xi}
.

(2.2) In case
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+2})=((b,a),(a,c),(c,a),(a,d),(d,c))
, we have
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha)=0
.

In this case we replace
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+2})
by
((b,a),(a,d),(d,b),(b,d),(d,c))
in the sequence
\overrightarrow{\xi}
.

(2.3) In case
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+2})=((a,b),(b,c),(c,a),(a,c),(c,d))
,
we have
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha)=0
.

In this case we replace
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+2})
by
((a,b),(b,d),(d,b),(b,c),(c,d))
in the sequence
\overrightarrow{\xi}
.

(2.4) In case
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+2})=((a,b),(b,c),(c,a),(a,d),(d,c))
, we have
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha)=-1
.

In this case we replace
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+2})
by
((a,b),(b,d),(d,b),(b,d),(d,c))
in the sequence
\overrightarrow{\xi}
.

In each case, one can see that the new sequences
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}
obtained are complete
(T^{\prime o},\gamma)
-paths and satisfy

m(\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})=-m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha).

Case II:
\gamma
crosses with
(a,c)
and two adjacent edges of the quadrilateral
(a,b,c,d)
. We may assume that
\gamma
crosses with
\cdots,(a,b),(a,c),(a,d),\cdots
sequentially. Then
\xi_{2j-2}=(a,b),\xi_{2j}=(a,c)
and
\xi_{2j+2}=(a,d)
for some
j\in\{2,\cdots,c-1\}
.

(1) If
\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j}=(a,c)
then
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+2})=((a,b),(b,a),(a,c),(c,a),(a,d))
or
((a,b),(b,a),(a,c),(c,d),(d,a))
.

(1.1) In case
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+2})=((a,b),(b,a),(a,c),(c,a),(a,d))
, we have
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha)=0
.

In this case we replace
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+2})
by
((a,b),(b,a),(a,d))
in the sequence
\overrightarrow{\xi}
.

(1.2) In case
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+2})=((a,b),(b,a),(a,c),(c,d),(d,a))
, we have
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha)=-1
.

In this case we replace
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+2})
by
((a,b),(b,d),(d,a))
in the sequence
\overrightarrow{\xi}
.

(2) If
\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j}=(c,a)
then
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+2})=((a,b),(b,c),(c,a),(a,d),(d,a))
or
((b,a),(a,c),(c,a),(a,d),(d,a))
.

(2.1) In case
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+2})=((a,b),(b,c),(c,a),(a,d),(d,a))
, we have
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha)=-1
.

In this case we replace
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+2})
by
((a,b),(b,d),(d,a))
in the sequence
\overrightarrow{\xi}
.

(2.2) In case
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+2})=((b,a),(a,c),(c,a),(a,d),(d,a))
, we have
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha)=0
.

In this case we replace
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j+2})
by
((b,a),(a,d),(d,a))
in the sequence
\overrightarrow{\xi}
.

In each case, one can see that the new sequences
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}
obtained are complete
(T^{\prime o},\gamma)
-paths and satisfy

m(\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})=-m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha).

Case III:
\gamma
crosses two adjacent edges of the quadrilateral
(a,b,c,d)
but not cross
(a,c)
. We may assume that
\gamma
crosses with
\cdots,(a,b),(b,c),\cdots
sequentially. Then
\xi_{2j-2}=(a,b),\xi_{2j}=(b,c)
for some
j\in\{2,\cdots,c\}
. Then we have
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j})=((b,a),(a,b),(b,c))
or
((a,b),(b,c),(c,b))
or
((b,a),(a,c),(c,b))
.

(1) If
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j})=((b,a),(a,b),(b,c))
, we have
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha)=0
. In this case we replace
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j})
by
((b,a)
,
(a,b),(b,d),(d,b),(b,c))
in the sequence
\overrightarrow{\xi}
.

(2) If
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j})=((a,b),(b,c),(c,b))
, we have
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha)=0
. In this case we replace
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j})
by
((a,b)
,
(b,d),(d,b),(b,c),(c,b))
in the sequence
\overrightarrow{\xi}
.

(3) If
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j})=((b,a),(a,c),(c,b))
, we have
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha)=1
. In this case we replace
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2j})
by
((b,a)
,
(a,d),(d,b),(b,c),(c,b))
or
((b,a),(a,b),(b,d),(d,c),(c,b))
in the sequence
\overrightarrow{\xi}
.

In each case, one can see that the new sequences
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}
obtained are complete
(T^{\prime o},\gamma)
-paths and satisfy

m(\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})=-m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha).

Case IV:
\gamma
crosses with
(a,c)
and exactly one edge of the quadrilateral
(a,b,c,d)
. We may assume that
\gamma
crosses with
(a,c),(a,d),\cdots
sequentially. Then
\xi_{2}=(a,c),\xi_{4}=(a,d)
. Thus we have
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt3},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt4})=((b,a),(a,c),(c,a),(a,d))
or
((b,a),(a,c),(c,d),(d,a))
or
((b,c),(c,a),(a,d),(d,a))
.

(1) If
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt3},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt4})=((b,a),(a,c),(c,a),(a,d))
, we have
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha)=0
. In this case we replace
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt3},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt4})
by
((b,a)
,
(a,d))
in the sequence
\overrightarrow{\xi}
.

(2) If
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt3},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt4})=((b,a),(a,c),(c,d),(d,a))
, we have
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha)=-1
. In this case we replace
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt3},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt4})
by
((b,d)
,
(d,a))
in the sequence
\overrightarrow{\xi}
.

(3) If
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt3},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt4})=((b,c),(c,a),(a,d),(d,a))
, we have
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha)=-1
. In this case we replace
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt3},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt4})
by
((b,d)
,
(d,a))
in the sequence
\overrightarrow{\xi}
.

In each case, one can see that the new sequences
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}
obtained are complete
(T^{\prime o},\gamma)
-paths and satisfy

m(\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})=-m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha).

Case V:
\gamma
crosses with exactly one edge of the quadrilateral
(a,b,c,d)
but does not cross with
(a,c)
. We may assume that
\gamma
crosses with
(b,c),\cdots
sequentially. Then
\xi_{2}=(b,c)
. Thus we have
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2})=((a,b),(b,c))
or
((a,c),(c,b))
.

(1) If
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2})=((a,b),(b,c))
, we have
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha)=0
. In this case we replace
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2})
by
((a,b),(b,d),(d,b),(b,c))
in the sequence
\overrightarrow{\xi}
.

(2) If
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2})=((a,c),(c,b))
, we have
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha)=1
. In this case we replace
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2})
by
((a,b),(b,d),(d,c),(c,b))
or
((a,d),(d,b),(b,c),(c,b))
in the sequence
\overrightarrow{\xi}
.

In each case, one can see that the new sequences
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}
obtained are complete
(T^{\prime o},\gamma)
-paths and satisfy

m(\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})=-m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha).

From the above discussion, we have the following observation.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
\overrightarrow{\xi}=(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt1},\cdots,\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2c+1})
be a complete
(T^{o},\gamma)
-path. Assume that
\xi_{j}=\alpha=(a,c)
for some even
j
such that
\xi_{j-1}\neq\alpha\neq\xi_{j+1}
.

(1)

If
\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj}=(a,c)
then
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj+1})=((b,a),(a,c),(c,d))
or
((d,a),(a,c),(c,b))
.

(2)

If
\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj}=(c,a)
then
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj+1})=((b,c),(c,a),(a,d))
or
((d,c),(c,a),(a,b))
.

Therefore, for any complete
(T^{o},\gamma)
-path
\overrightarrow{\xi}
, we obtain a set of complete
(T^{\prime o},\gamma)
-paths, denoted by
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(\overrightarrow{\xi})
, moreover, for any
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}\in\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(\overrightarrow{\xi})
, we have
m(\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})=-m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha).

The construction of
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}
can be summarized as follows:

Construction of
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}
:
given any complete
(T^{o},\gamma)
-path
\overrightarrow{\xi}=(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt1},\cdots,\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2c+1})
.

If
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha)=1
, then the set
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(\overrightarrow{\xi})
contains all sequences of oriented arcs
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}
, where
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}
is obtained via the following recipe:

(i)

First remove a pair
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj+1})
in case
\xi_{j}=\xi_{j+1}=\alpha
and
\xi_{j-1}\neq\alpha
;

(ii)

Next for any
\xi_{j}=\alpha
with odd
j
,

(a)

if
\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj}=(a,c)
, then replace
\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj}
by
(a,b),(b,d),(d,c)
or
(a,d),(d,b),(b,c)
;

(b)

if
\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj}=(c,a)
, then replace
\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj}
by
(c,b),(b,d),(d,a)
or
(c,d),(d,b),(b,a)
;

If
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha)=0
, then the set
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(\overrightarrow{\xi})
contains all sequences of oriented arcs
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}
, where
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}
can be obtained via the following recipe:

(i)

First remove a pair
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj+1})
in case
\xi_{j}=\xi_{j+1}=\alpha
and
\xi_{j-1}\neq\alpha
;

(ii)

Next, if
\gamma
crosses with
\alpha^{\prime}=(b,d)
, then add

(b,d),(d,b)
or
(d,b),(b,d)
to make the sequences to complete
(T^{\prime o},\gamma)
-paths;

If
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha)=-1
, then
\xi_{j}=\alpha
for some even
j
such that
\xi_{j-1}\neq\alpha\neq\xi_{j+1}
. The set
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(\overrightarrow{\xi})
contains all sequences of oriented arcs
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}
, where
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}
can be obtained via the following recipe:

(i)

First,
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj+1})=((b,a),(a,c),(c,d))
;
((b,c),(c,a),(a,d))
;
((d,a),(a,c),(c,b))
or
((d,c),(c,a),(a,b))
by Lemma
7.1
.

(a)

if
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj+1})=((b,a),(a,c),(c,d))
or
((b,c),(c,a),(a,d))
, then replace it by
(b,d)
;

(b)

if
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj+1})=((d,a),(a,c),(c,b))
or
((d,c),(c,a),(a,b))
, then replace it by
(d,b)
;

(ii)

Next, if
\gamma
crosses with
\alpha^{\prime}=(b,d)
, then add

(b,d),(d,b)
or
(d,b),(b,d)
to make the sequences to complete
(T^{\prime o},\gamma)
-paths.

Remark 7.2
.

In particular, if
\gamma
crosses neither
\alpha
nor
\alpha^{\prime}
then
\mathcal{CP}(T^{o},\gamma)=\mathcal{CP}(T^{\prime o},\gamma)
and
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}
is the identity map.

In summary, we obtain the following proposition.

Proposition 7.3
.

With the foregoing notation. For any complete
(T^{o},\gamma)
-path
\overrightarrow{\xi}
, we have

(1)

\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(\overrightarrow{\xi})\subset\mathcal{CP}(T^{\prime o},\gamma)
;

(2)

|\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(\overrightarrow{\xi})|=2^{[m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha)]_{+}}
,
moreover, if
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha)=1
, assume that
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(\overrightarrow{\xi})=\{\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime},\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime\prime}\}
, then
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}
and
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime\prime}
are related by a twist at
\alpha^{\prime}
;

(3)

\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(\overrightarrow{\xi})=\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(\overrightarrow{\zeta})
for some
\zeta\neq\xi
if and only if

m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha)=-1
,
and
\overrightarrow{\xi}
and
\overrightarrow{\zeta}
are related by a twist on
\alpha
;

(4)

for any
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}\in\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(\overrightarrow{\xi})
, we have

m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha)=-m(\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})
;

(5)

for any
(a,b),(b,c),(c,d),(a,d),(a,c)\neq\zeta\in T^{o}
and
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}\in\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(\overrightarrow{\xi})
, we have

m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\zeta)=m(\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime},\zeta)
.

Similarly, we can construct
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
.

Theorem 7.4
.

With the foregoing notation.
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}
and
\phi^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
are partition bijections between
\mathcal{CP}(T^{o},\gamma)
and
\mathcal{CP}(T^{\prime o},\gamma)
, moreover,
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}
and
\phi^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
are inverse to each other.

Proof.

We first show that
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}
and
\phi^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
are partition bijections. We shall only consider
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}
. By Proposition
7.3
(2), we have
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(\overrightarrow{\xi})\neq\emptyset
for any
\overrightarrow{\xi}\in\mathcal{CP}(T^{o},\gamma)
.

Suppose that
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(\overrightarrow{\xi})\cap\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(\overrightarrow{\zeta})\neq\emptyset
for some
\overrightarrow{\xi},\overrightarrow{\zeta}\in\mathcal{CP}(T^{o},\gamma)
. Assume that
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}\in\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(\overrightarrow{\xi})\cap\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(\overrightarrow{\zeta})
. Then
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha)=m(\overrightarrow{\zeta},\alpha)=-m(\overrightarrow{\xi^{\prime}},\alpha^{\prime})
by Proposition
7.3
(4). If
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha)=m(\overrightarrow{\zeta},\alpha)\leq 0
then
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(\overrightarrow{\xi})=\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(\overrightarrow{\zeta})=\{\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}\}
by Proposition
7.3
(2). If
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha)=m(\overrightarrow{\zeta},\alpha)=1
, then
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(\overrightarrow{\xi})=\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(\overrightarrow{\zeta})=\{\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime},\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime\prime}\}
by Proposition
7.3
(2), where
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime\prime}
is the complete
(T^{\prime o},\gamma)
-path related to
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}
by a twist on
\alpha^{\prime}
. Therefore we have either
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(\overrightarrow{\xi})\cap\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(\overrightarrow{\zeta})=\emptyset
or
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(\overrightarrow{\xi})=\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(\overrightarrow{\zeta})
for any
\overrightarrow{\xi},\overrightarrow{\zeta}\in\mathcal{CP}(T^{o},\gamma)
.

According to the construction of
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}
and
\phi^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
, we see that
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}\in\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(\overrightarrow{\xi})
if and only if
\overrightarrow{\xi}\in\phi^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}(\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime})
for any
\overrightarrow{\xi}\in\mathcal{CP}(T^{o},\gamma)
and
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{CP}(T^{\prime o},\gamma)
. Thus
\bigcup_{\overrightarrow{\xi}\in\mathcal{CP}(T^{o},\gamma)}\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(\overrightarrow{\xi})=\mathcal{CP}(T^{\prime o},\gamma)
. Therefore,
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}
is a partition bijection by Remark
2.5
. Similarly,
\phi^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha}
is a partition bijection. By Proposition
2.6
,
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}
and
\phi^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
are inverse to each other.

\square

7.1.2.
Partition bijection
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}

Let
\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}
be a set of arcs in
T^{o}
such that
\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j}
are not two sides of any triangle in
T^{o}
for any
i\neq j
. Denote
T^{\prime o}=\mu_{\alpha_{k}}\circ\cdots\circ\mu_{\alpha_{1}}(T^{o})
. Denote by
\alpha^{\prime}_{i}
the arc obtained from
T^{o}
by flip at
\alpha_{i}
. Assume that
\alpha_{i}=(a_{i},c_{i})
and
\alpha_{i}
is a diagonal of the quadrilateral
(a_{i},b_{i},c_{i},d_{i})
in
T^{o}
, where
a_{i},b_{i},c_{i},d_{i}
are in the clockwise order.

For any given complete
(T^{o},\gamma)
-path
\overrightarrow{\xi}=(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt1},\cdots,\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2c+1})
, we construct a subset
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\xi})
of complete
(T^{\prime o},\gamma)
-paths, which is obtained from
\overrightarrow{\xi}
via the following receipt:

(i)

First, remove all the pairs
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj+1})
such that
\xi_{j}=\xi_{j+1}=\alpha_{i}
for some
i\in\{1,\cdots,k\}
and
\xi_{j-1}\neq\alpha_{i}
;

(ii)

Next, for any
i\in\{1,\cdots,k\}
,

(a)

if
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha_{i})=1
, we have
\xi_{j_{i}}=\alpha_{i}
for some odd
j_{i}
, then replace
\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj_{i}}
by
(a_{i},b_{i}),(b_{i},d_{i}),(d_{i},c_{i})
or
(a_{i},d_{i}),(d_{i},b_{i}),(b_{i},c_{i})
in case
\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj_{i}}=(a,c)
and replace
\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj_{i}}
by
(c_{i},b_{i}),(b_{i},d_{i}),(d_{i},a_{i})
or
(c_{i},d_{i}),(d_{i},b_{i}),(b_{i},a_{i})
in case
\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj_{i}}=(c_{i},a_{i})
;

(b)

if
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha_{i})=-1
, we have
\xi_{j_{i}}=\alpha_{i}
for some even
j_{i}
, then replace
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj_{i}-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj_{i}},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj_{i}+1})
by
(b_{i},d_{i})
in case
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj_{i}-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj_{i}},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj_{i}+1})=((b_{i},a_{i}),(a_{i},c_{i}),(c_{i},d_{i}))
or
((b_{i},c_{i}),(c_{i},a_{i}),(a_{i},d_{i}))
, and replace
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj_{i}-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj_{i}},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj_{i}+1})
by
(d_{i},b_{i})
in case
(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj_{i}-1},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj_{i}},\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0ptj_{i}+1})=((d_{i},a_{i}),(a_{i},c_{i}),(c_{i},b_{i}))
or
((d_{i},c_{i}),(c_{i},a_{i}),(a_{i},b_{i}))
;

(iii)

Last, for all the
\alpha^{\prime}_{i}=(b_{i},d_{i})
such that
\gamma
crosses with
\alpha^{\prime}_{i}
, then add

(b_{i},d_{i}),(d_{i},b_{i})
or
(d_{i},b_{i}),(b_{i},d_{i})
to make the sequences to complete
(T^{\prime o},\gamma)
-paths.

As
\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j}
are not two sides of any triangle in
T^{o}
for any
i\neq j
, the above construction is well-defined and we have

(20)

\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\xi})=\phi^{\mu_{\alpha_{k-1}}\circ\cdots\circ\mu_{\alpha_{1}}T^{o}}_{\alpha_{k}}\left(\cdots\phi^{\mu_{\alpha_{1}}T^{o}}_{\alpha_{2}}(\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1}}(\overrightarrow{\xi}))\right),

moreover,
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}
does not depend on the order of
\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}
.

We can also construct a subset
\phi^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\alpha^{\prime}_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime})
for any complete
(T^{\prime o},\gamma)
-path
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}
.

We have the following proposition which generalizes Proposition
7.3
.

Proposition 7.5
.

Let
\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}
be a set of arcs in
T^{o}
such that
\alpha_{i}
and
\alpha_{j}
are not two sides of any triangle in
T^{o}
for any
i\neq j
. For any complete
(T^{o},\gamma)
-path
\overrightarrow{\xi}
, we have

(1)

\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\xi})\subset\mathcal{CP}(T^{\prime o},\gamma)
,

(2)

|\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\xi})|=2^{\sum_{i=1}^{k}[m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha_{i})]_{+}}
,
moreover, for any
\alpha_{i}
with
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha_{i})=1
and
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}\in\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\xi})
, there exists
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime\prime}\in\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\xi})
such that
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}
and
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime\prime}
are related by a twist on
\alpha^{\prime}_{i}
;

(3)

if
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha_{i})=-1
for some
\alpha_{i}
then we have
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\xi})=\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\zeta})
, where
\overrightarrow{\zeta}
is the complete
(\prod_{i=1}^{k}\mu_{\alpha_{i}}(T^{o}),\gamma)
-path which is related to
\overrightarrow{\xi}
by a twist at
\alpha_{i}
;

(4)

for any
i\in\{1,\cdots,k\}
and
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}\in\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\xi})
, we have

m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha_{i})=-m(\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}_{i}).

(5)

If
\zeta\in T^{o}
and
\zeta\neq(a_{i},b_{i}),(b_{i},c_{i}),(c_{i},d_{i}),(a_{i},d_{i}),(a_{i},c_{i})
for all
i
, then for any
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}\in\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\xi})
, we have

m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\zeta)=m(\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime},\zeta).

The following theorem generalizes Theorem
7.4
.

Theorem 7.6
.

With the foregoing notation.
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}
and
\phi^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\alpha^{\prime}_{k}}
are partition bijections between
\mathcal{CP}(T^{o},\gamma)
and
\mathcal{CP}(T^{\prime o},\gamma)
inverse to each other.

Proof.

It is clear that
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\xi})\neq\emptyset
for any
\overrightarrow{\xi}\in\mathcal{CP}(T^{o},\gamma)
.

Suppose that
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\xi})\cap\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\zeta})\neq\emptyset
for some
\overrightarrow{\xi}
and
\overrightarrow{\zeta}
. Assume that
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}\in\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\xi})\cap\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\zeta})
. Then
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha_{i})=m(\overrightarrow{\zeta},\alpha_{i})=-m(\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}_{i})
for any
i
by Proposition
7.5
(4). By Proposition
7.5
(2), we see that
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\xi})=\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\zeta})
contains all
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime\prime}
which are related to
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}
by a sequence of twists on some
\alpha^{\prime}_{i}
with
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha_{i})=1
. Therefore, we have

\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\xi})=\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\zeta})
.

For any
\overrightarrow{\xi}\in\mathcal{CP}(T^{o},\gamma)
and
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{CP}(T^{\prime o},\gamma)
, we show that
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}\in\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\xi})
if and only if
\overrightarrow{\xi}\in\phi^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\alpha^{\prime}_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime})
by induction on
k
. If
k=1
, it follows by Theorem
7.4
. Suppose that it is true for the cases less than
k
. If
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}\in\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\xi})
, then
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}\in\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\eta})
for some
\overrightarrow{\eta}\in\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k-1}}(\overrightarrow{\xi})
. By induction hypothesis, we have
\overrightarrow{\xi}\in\phi^{\prod_{i=1}^{k-1}\mu_{\alpha_{i}}T^{o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\alpha^{\prime}_{k-1}}(\overrightarrow{\eta})
and
\overrightarrow{\eta}\in\phi^{\prod_{i=1}^{k}\mu_{\alpha_{i}}T^{o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime})
. Thus,

\overrightarrow{\xi}\in\phi^{\prod_{i=1}^{k-1}\mu_{\alpha_{i}}T^{o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\alpha^{\prime}_{k-1}}(\phi^{\prod_{i=1}^{k}\mu_{\alpha_{i}}T^{o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}))=\phi^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\alpha^{\prime}_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}).
The statement is proved.

By Remark
2.5
,
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}
is a partition bijection. Similarly,
\phi^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\alpha^{\prime}_{k}}
is a partition bijection. By Proposition
2.6
,
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}
and
\phi^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\alpha^{\prime}_{k}}
are inverse to each other.

\square

Remark 7.7
.

\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\xi})\cap\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\zeta})\neq\emptyset
if and only if
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\xi})=\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}(\overrightarrow{\zeta})
if and only if
\overrightarrow{\xi}
and
\overrightarrow{\zeta}
are related by a sequence of twists on some
\alpha_{i}
such that
m(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha_{i})=-1
.

7.1.3.
Partition bijection
\varphi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}

Recall that there are natural bijective maps from
\mathcal{CP}(T^{o},\gamma)
to
\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{o},\gamma})
and from
\mathcal{CP}(T^{\prime o},\gamma)
to
\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{\prime o},\gamma})
, see Subsection
2.7
. Therefore, the partition bijection
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}
from
\mathcal{CP}(T^{o},\gamma)
to
\mathcal{CP}(T^{\prime o},\gamma)
induces a partition bijection
\varphi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}
from
\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{o},\gamma})
to
\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{\prime o},\gamma})
, that is, we have the following commutative diagram.

\textstyle{\mathcal{CP}(T^{o},\gamma)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{CP}(T^{\prime o},\gamma)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{o},\gamma})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{\prime o},\gamma}).}

We also have the inverse partition bijection
\varphi^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}:\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{\prime o},\gamma})\to\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{o},\gamma})
of
\varphi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}
.

Under the bijection between
\mathcal{CP}(T^{o},\gamma)
and
\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{o},\gamma})
, for any
P\in\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{o},\gamma})
, denote by
\overrightarrow{\xi}(P)
the perfect matching of
G_{T^{o},\gamma}
corresponding to
P
.
For any
\zeta\in T^{o}
, let

\hat{m}(P,\zeta)=m(\overrightarrow{\xi}(P),\zeta),

where
m(\overrightarrow{\xi}(P),\zeta)
is given by (
2
).

In view of (
2
) and (
9
) (
10
), by the bijection between
\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{o},\gamma})
and
\mathcal{CP}(T^{o},\gamma)
, we have

\hat{m}(P,\zeta)=m(P;\zeta)-n(G_{T^{o},\gamma};\zeta).

Similarly we can define
\hat{m}(P^{\prime},\zeta)
for any
P^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{\prime o},\gamma})
and
\zeta\in T^{\prime o}
.

Assume that
\alpha=(a,c)
and
\alpha
is the diagonal of the quadrilateral
(a,b,c,d)
in
T^{o}
. Suppose that
a,b,c,d
are in the clockwise order.

Proposition 7.8
.

With the foregoing notation. Let
P\in\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{o},\gamma})
.

(1)

We have

|\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P)|=2^{[\hat{m}(P,\alpha)]_{+}}
,
moreover,

(a)

if
\hat{m}(P,\alpha)=1
, then there is a tile
G^{\prime}
of
G_{T^{\prime o},\gamma}
with diagonal labeled
\alpha^{\prime}
such that any
P^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}
can twist on
G^{\prime}
and
\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}
is closed under the twist on
G^{\prime}
;

(b)

if
\hat{m}(P,\alpha)=-1
, then there is a tile
G
of
G_{T^{o},\gamma}
with diagonal labeled
\alpha
such that
P
can twist on
G
and
\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P)=\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(\mu_{G}P)
;

(2)

For any
P^{\prime}\in\varphi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(P)
, we have

\hat{m}(P,\alpha)=-\hat{m}(P^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}).

(3)

For any
\zeta\in T^{o}
such that
\zeta\neq(a,b),(b,c),(c,d),(a,d),(a,c)
and
P^{\prime}\in\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(P)
, we have

\hat{m}(P,\zeta)=\hat{m}(P^{\prime},\zeta).

Proof.

It follows immediately by Proposition
7.3
.

\square

Proposition 7.9
.

Assume
P
can twist on a tile
G_{l}
with diagonal labeled
\tau_{i_{l}}
and
P>\mu_{G_{l}}P
.

(1)

if
\tau_{i_{l}}\neq\alpha,(a,b),(b,c),(c,d),(a,d)

of

with diagonal labeled

,

, and

(2)

if

, then

and

\{P,\mu_{G_{l}}P\}=\varphi_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{T^{\prime o}}(P^{\prime}),

where

.

(3)

if

, then
\hat{m}(P,\alpha)=\hat{m}(\mu_{G_{l}}P,\alpha)-1
.
For
P^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P)
, there are tiles

and

of

with diagonal labeled

and
(b,d)

,

G^{\prime}_{[l^{\prime}]}
,

,

(d)

\mu_{G^{\prime}_{[l]}}P^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(\mu_{G_{l}}P)
.

(4)

if

, then
\hat{m}(P,\alpha)=\hat{m}(\mu_{G_{l}}P,\alpha)+1
.

, there are tiles
G^{\prime}_{[l]}
and

of
G_{T^{\prime o},\gamma}
with diagonal labeled
\tau_{i_{l}}
and
(b,d)
, respectively, such that

(a)

|[l]-[l^{\prime}]|=1
,

(b)

P^{\prime}
can twist on
G^{\prime}_{[l]}
and
\mu_{G^{\prime}_{[l]}}P^{\prime}
can twist on
G^{\prime}_{[l^{\prime}]}
,

(c)

P^{\prime}<\mu_{G^{\prime}_{[l]}}P^{\prime}<\mu_{G^{\prime}_{[l^{\prime}]}}\mu_{G^{\prime}_{[l]}}P^{\prime}
,

(d)

\mu_{G^{\prime}_{[l]}}P^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P)
.

Proof.

It follows by the construction of
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}
.

\square

7.1.4.
Partition bijection
\varphi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}

Let
\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}
be a set of arcs in
T^{o}
such that
\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j}
are not two sides of any triangle in
T^{o}
for any
i\neq j
. Denote by
\alpha^{\prime}_{i}
the arc obtained from
T^{o}
by flip at
\alpha_{i}
. Assume that
\alpha_{i}=(a_{i},c_{i})
and
\alpha_{i}
is the diagonal of the quadrilateral
(a_{i},b_{i},c_{i},d_{i})
in
T^{o}
, where
a_{i},b_{i},c_{i},d_{i}
are in the clockwise order. Denote
T^{\prime o}=\mu_{\alpha_{k}}\circ\cdots\circ\mu_{\alpha_{1}}(T^{o})
.

The partition bijection
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}:\mathcal{CP}(T^{o},\gamma)\to\mathcal{CP}(T^{\prime o},\gamma)
induces a partition bijection
\varphi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}:\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{o},\gamma})\to\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{\prime o},\gamma})
, i.e., we have the following commutative diagram.

\textstyle{\mathcal{CP}(T^{o},\gamma)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{CP}(T^{\prime o},\gamma)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{o},\gamma})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{\prime o},\gamma}).}

We have the inverse partition bijection
\varphi^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\alpha^{\prime}_{k}}\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{\prime o},\gamma})\to\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{o},\gamma})
of
\varphi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}
.

The following proposition generalizes Proposition
7.8
, follows by Proposition
7.5
.

Proposition 7.12
.

With the foregoing notation. Let
P\in\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{o},\gamma})
.

for some

-mutable edges labeled

in

.

The following proposition follows by Proposition

.
