7.2.
General case

In this subsection, we assume that
\Sigma
is an arbitrary orbifold without orbifold points of weight
2
. Let
T^{o}
be an ideal triangulation and
\alpha\in T^{o}
be a non-self-folded arc. Denote
T^{\prime o}=\mu_{\alpha}(T^{o})
and by
\alpha^{\prime}
the new edge obtained. Let
\gamma
be an arc in
\Sigma
. Let
P_{n}
be the canonical polygon of
\gamma
in
T^{o}
with morphism
f:P_{n}\to\Sigma
and triangle
\hat{T}^{o}
. Assume that
f(\hat{\gamma})=\gamma
for some diagonal
\hat{\gamma}
in
P_{n}
.

Assume that
\alpha
is a diagonal of the quadrilateral
(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4})
in
T^{o}
and
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{3}
are in the clockwise direction of
\alpha
.

Denote by
\eta_{\alpha}
the cardinality of
f^{-1}(\alpha)
. We may label
f^{-1}(\alpha)
as
\hat{\alpha}_{1},\cdots,\hat{\alpha}_{\eta_{\alpha}}
in order according to the orientation of
\hat{\gamma}
. We may further assume that
\hat{\alpha}_{i}
is not a boundary edge in
P_{n}
for all
i\in\{1,\cdots,\eta_{\alpha}\}
, otherwise we may extend
P_{n}
to a larger polygon. As
\alpha
is not a folded arc,
\hat{\alpha}_{i}
and
\hat{\alpha}_{j}
are not in a same triangle in
\hat{T}^{o}
for
i\neq j
. Denote
\hat{T}^{\prime o}=\mu_{\hat{\alpha}_{\eta_{\alpha}}}\cdots\mu_{\hat{\alpha}_{1}}\hat{T}^{o}
.

7.2.1.
Partition bijection
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}

Using Theorem
7.6
, as
\hat{\alpha}_{i}
and
\hat{\alpha}_{j}
are not in a same triangle in
\hat{T}^{o}
for
i\neq j
, we have a partition bijection

(21)

\phi^{\hat{T}^{o}}_{\hat{\alpha}_{1},\cdots,\hat{\alpha}_{\eta_{\alpha}}}:\mathcal{CP}(\hat{T}^{o},\hat{\gamma})\to\mathcal{CP}(\hat{T}^{\prime o},\hat{\gamma}).

Since
f
induces bijections between
\mathcal{CP}(\hat{T}^{o},\hat{\gamma})
and
\mathcal{CP}(T^{o},\gamma)
, and between
\mathcal{CP}(\hat{T}^{\prime o},\hat{\gamma})
and
\mathcal{CP}(T^{\prime o},\gamma)
, we have
\phi^{\hat{T}^{o}}_{\hat{\alpha}_{1},\cdots,\hat{\alpha}_{\eta_{\alpha}}}
induces a partition bijection

(22)

\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}:\mathcal{CP}(T^{o},\gamma)\to\mathcal{CP}(T^{\prime o},\gamma).

That is, we have the following commutative diagram.

\textstyle{\mathcal{CP}(\hat{T}^{o},\hat{\gamma})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{\hat{T}^{o}}_{\hat{\alpha}_{1},\cdots,\hat{\alpha}_{\eta_{\alpha}}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{CP}(\hat{T}^{\prime o},\hat{\gamma})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathcal{CP}(T^{o},\gamma)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{CP}(T^{\prime o},\gamma).}

For any complete
(T^{o},\gamma)
-path
\overrightarrow{\xi}=(\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt1},\cdots,\overrightarrow{\xi}_{\hskip-2.0pt2c+1})
, denote by
\overrightarrow{\hat{\xi}}=(\overrightarrow{\hat{\xi}}_{\hskip-2.0pt1},\cdots,\overrightarrow{\hat{\xi}}_{\hskip-2.0pt2c+1})
the corresponding complete
(\hat{T}^{o},\hat{\gamma})
-path under
f
. We may associate
\overrightarrow{\xi}
with an integer vector

(23)

{\bf m}(\overrightarrow{\xi},\alpha)=(m(\overrightarrow{\hat{\xi}},\hat{\alpha}_{1}),\cdots,m(\overrightarrow{\hat{\xi}},\hat{\alpha}_{\eta_{\alpha}})),

where each
m(\overrightarrow{\hat{\xi}},\hat{\alpha}_{i})
is given by (
2
).

For any
i\in\{1,\cdots,\eta_{\alpha}\}
, denote by
\hat{\alpha}^{\prime}_{i}
the new arc in
P_{n}
obtained from
\hat{T}^{o}
by flip at
\hat{\alpha}_{i}
. For any complete
(T^{\prime o},\gamma)
-path
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}=(\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}_{\hskip-2.0pt1},\cdots,\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}_{\hskip-2.0pt2c^{\prime}+1})
, denote by
\overrightarrow{\hat{\xi}^{\prime}}=(\overrightarrow{\hat{\xi}^{\prime}}_{\hskip-2.0pt1},\cdots,\overrightarrow{\hat{\xi}^{\prime}}_{\hskip-2.0pt2c^{\prime}+1})
the corresponding complete
(\mu_{\hat{\alpha}_{\eta_{\alpha}}}\cdots\mu_{\hat{\alpha}_{1}}\hat{T}^{o},\hat{\beta})
-path under
f
. Similarly, we may associate
\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime}
with an integer vector

{\bf m}(\overrightarrow{\xi}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})=(m(\overrightarrow{\hat{\xi}^{\prime}},\hat{\alpha}^{\prime}_{1}),\cdots,m(\overrightarrow{\hat{\xi}^{\prime}},\hat{\alpha}^{\prime}_{\eta_{\alpha}})).

Similarly, we define the partition bijection

(24)

\phi^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}:\mathcal{CP}(T^{\prime o},\gamma)\to\mathcal{CP}(T^{o},\gamma),

which is induced by the partition bijection
\phi^{\hat{T}^{\prime o}}_{\hat{\alpha}^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\hat{\alpha}^{\prime}_{\eta_{\alpha}}}
.

7.2.2.
Partition bijection
\varphi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}

Since
\mathcal{CP}(T^{o},\gamma)\cong\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{o},\gamma})
and
\mathcal{CP}(T^{\prime o},\gamma)\cong\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{\prime o},\gamma})
, we have the partition bijection
\phi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}:\mathcal{CP}(T^{o},\gamma)\to\mathcal{CP}(T^{\prime o},\gamma)
in (
22
) induces partition bijections
\varphi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}:\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{o},\gamma})\to\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{\prime o},\gamma})
. Similarly, we have the inverse partition bijection
\varphi^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}:\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{\prime o},\gamma})\to\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{o},\gamma})
.

In this subsection, we study the properties of
\varphi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}
and
\varphi^{T^{\prime o}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
.

For any
P\in\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{o},\gamma})
, denote by
\overrightarrow{\xi}(P)
the corresponding complete
(T^{o},\gamma)
-path and
\hat{P}
the corresponding perfect matching of
G_{\hat{T}^{o},\hat{\gamma}}
, let

{\bf m}(P,\alpha)={\bf m}(\overrightarrow{\xi}(P),\alpha),

where
{\bf m}(\overrightarrow{\xi}(P),\alpha)
is given by (
23
).

Assume that
{\bf m}(P,\alpha)=(m_{1}(P),\cdots,m_{\eta_{\alpha}}(P))
. In view of (
9
) and (
10
), we have

\sum_{i=1}^{\eta(\alpha)}m_{i}(P)=m(P;\alpha)-n(G_{T^{o},\gamma};\alpha).

Similarly we can define
{\bf m}(P^{\prime};\alpha^{\prime})
for any
P^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{\prime o},\alpha^{\prime}})
.

Among
f^{-1}(\alpha)=\{\hat{\alpha}_{1},\cdots,\hat{\alpha}_{\eta_{\alpha}}\}
, assume that
\hat{\gamma}
crosses
\hat{\alpha}_{i_{1}},\cdots,\hat{\alpha}_{i_{l}}
sequentially, among
\{\hat{\alpha}^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\hat{\alpha}^{\prime}_{\eta_{\alpha}}\}
, assume that
\hat{\gamma}
crosses
\hat{\alpha}^{\prime}_{j_{1}},\cdots,\hat{\alpha}^{\prime}_{j_{l^{\prime}}}
sequentially.

The following two Propositions follows by Proposition
7.12
(1) (2).

Proposition 7.14
.

Assume that
{\bf m}(P,\alpha)=(m_{1}(P),\cdots,m_{\eta_{\alpha}}(P))
.

(1)

If
m_{k}(P)=-1
for some
k\in\{1,\cdots,\eta_{\alpha}\}
, then there exists a tile
G(k)
of
G_{T^{o},\gamma}
with diagonal labeled
\alpha
such that
P
can twist on the tile
G(k)
.

(2)

If
m_{k}(P)=1
for some
k\in\{1,\cdots,\eta_{\alpha}\}
, then there exists a tile
G^{\prime}(k)
of
G_{T^{\prime o},\gamma}
with diagonal labeled
\alpha^{\prime}
such that any
P^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P)
can twist on
G^{\prime}(k)
.

Proposition 7.15
.

For any
P\in\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{o},\gamma})
with
{\bf m}(P,\alpha)=(m_{1}(P),\cdots,m_{\eta_{\alpha}}(P))
, we have

|\varphi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(P)|=2^{\sum_{i=1}^{\eta_{\alpha}}[m_{i}(P)]_{+}},

more precisely, for any
P^{\prime}\in\varphi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(P)
,

(1)

we have
{\bf m}(P^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})=-{\bf m}(P,\alpha)
,

(2)

for any
k
such that
m_{k}(P)=1
,
P^{\prime}
can twist on the tiles
G^{\prime}(k)
and
\mu_{G^{\prime}(k)}P^{\prime}\in\varphi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(P)
, where
G^{\prime}(k)
are the tiles given in Proposition
7.14
.

Lemma 7.16
.

For any
P\in\mathcal{\mathcal{}}P(G_{T^{o},\gamma})
and
P^{\prime}\in\varphi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(P)
, for any
(\alpha\neq)\tau\in T^{o}
,

(1)

if
\tau\neq\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4}
, then we have
\hat{m}(P;\tau)=\hat{m}(P^{\prime};\tau)
;

(2)

if
\tau\in\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4}\}
, then the number of edges in
P
labeled
\tau
which are not
\alpha
-mutable equals the number of edges in
P^{\prime}
labeled
\tau
which are not
\alpha^{\prime}
-mutable.

Proof.

It follows by Proposition
7.12
(3) (4).

\square

The following Proposition follows by Propositions
7.12
and
7.13
.

Proposition 7.17
.

Assume that
P
can twist on a tile
G_{l}
with diagonal labeled
\tau_{i_{l}}
and
P>\mu_{G_{l}}P
. Assume that
{\bf m}(P,\alpha)=(m_{1}(P),\cdots,m_{\eta_{\alpha}}(P))
,

(1)

if
\tau_{i_{l}}\neq\alpha,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4}
, then there is a tile
G^{\prime}_{[l]}
of
G_{T^{\prime o},\gamma}
with diagonal labeled
\tau_{i_{l}}
such that any
P^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P)
can twist on
G^{\prime}_{[l]}
,
P^{\prime}>\mu_{G^{\prime}_{[l]}}(P^{\prime})
, and

\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(\mu_{G_{l}}P)=\mu_{G^{\prime}_{[l]}}(\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P)),

moreover, the following equalities hold:

(a)
m^{\pm}(P,G_{l};\tau_{i_{l}})=m^{\pm}(P^{\prime},G^{\prime}_{[l]};\tau_{i_{l}})
;
(b)
n(G_{l}^{\pm};\tau_{i_{l}})=n(G^{\prime\pm}_{[l]};\tau_{i_{l}})
.

In fact
G^{\prime}_{[l]}
corresponds to the same crossing point of
\gamma
and
\tau_{i_{l}}
as
G_{l}
.

(2)

if
\tau_{i_{l}}=\alpha
, then
m_{k}(P)=-1
and
G_{l}=G(k)
for some
k
, where
G(k)
is the tile given in Proposition
7.14
, moreover,
\mu_{G_{l}}P\in\varphi_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{T^{\prime o}}(P^{\prime})

for any
P^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P)
.

(3)

if
\tau_{i_{l}}\in\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4}\}
and
\tau_{i_{l}}
is not the radius of any self-folded triangle in
T^{o}
or
T^{\prime o}
, without loss of generality, we may assume
\tau_{i_{l}}=\alpha_{1}
, then there exists a tile
G^{\prime}_{[l]}
of
G_{T^{\prime o},\gamma}
with diagonal labeled
\alpha_{1}
such that any
P^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(\mu_{G_{l}}P)
can twist on
G^{\prime}_{[l]}
and
P^{\prime}>\mu_{G^{\prime}_{[l]}}P^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P)
. Moreover, if the
\alpha^{\prime}
-mutable edges in
P^{\prime}
are labeled
\alpha_{2},\alpha_{4}
then

(a)
m^{\pm}(P,G_{l};\alpha_{1})=m^{\pm}(P^{\prime},G^{\prime}_{[l]};\alpha_{1})
,
(b)
n(G_{l}^{\pm};\alpha_{1})=n(G^{\prime\pm}_{[l]};\alpha_{1})
.

Proposition 7.18
.

Assume that
\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{2}
and the diagonal of
G_{l}
is labeled
\alpha_{1}
. We further assume that
rel(G_{l},T^{o})=1
and
N(G_{l}),W(G_{l})
are labeled
\alpha
. If
P
can twist on
G_{l}
and
P>Q:=\mu_{G_{l}}P
, denote
{\bf m}(P,\alpha)=(m_{1}(P),\cdots,m_{\eta_{\alpha}}(P)),{\bf m}(Q,\alpha)=(m_{1}(Q),\cdots,m_{\eta_{\alpha}}(Q))
, then
m_{k}(P)=m_{k}(Q)-1,m_{k+1}(P)=m_{k+1}(Q)+1
for some
k
and
m_{i}(P)=m_{i}(Q)
for any
i\neq k,k+1
. Consequently, we have
(m_{k}(P),m_{k+1}(P))=(-1,1)
,
(0,0)
or
(0,1)
.

(1)

In case
(m_{k}(P),m_{k+1}(P))=(-1,1)
, we have
(m_{k}(\mu_{G_{l}}P),m_{k+1}(\mu_{G_{l}}P))=(0,0)
. Assume the tile left to
G^{\prime}(k+1)
is
G^{\prime}
. Then the diagonal of
G^{\prime}
is labeled
\alpha_{1}
and
any
Q^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(Q)
can twist on
G^{\prime}
, see Figure
12
. Moreover,

(a)
\mu_{G^{\prime}}Q^{\prime},\mu_{G^{\prime}(k+1)}\mu_{G^{\prime}}Q^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P)
,
(b)
Q^{\prime}<\mu_{G^{\prime}}Q^{\prime}<\mu_{G^{\prime}(k+1)}\mu_{G^{\prime}}Q^{\prime}
,

(c),

(d)
\Omega(Q^{\prime};G^{\prime})=\Omega(Q;G_{l})+\Omega(P;G_{l+1})
.

In particular,
\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P)=\{\mu_{G^{\prime}}Q^{\prime},\mu_{G^{\prime}(k+1)}\mu_{G^{\prime}}Q^{\prime}\mid Q^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(Q)\}
.

Figure 12
.

(2)

In case
(m_{k}(P),m_{k+1}(P))=(0,0)
, we have
(m_{k}(Q),m_{k+1}(\mu_{G_{l}}P))=(1,-1)
. Assume the tile right to
G^{\prime}(k)
is
G^{\prime}
. Then the diagonal of
G^{\prime}
is labeled
\alpha_{1}
and
any
P^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P)
can twist on
G^{\prime}
, see Figure
13
. Moreover,

(a)
\mu_{G^{\prime}}P^{\prime},\mu_{G^{\prime}(k)}\mu_{G^{\prime}}P^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(Q)
, (b)
P^{\prime}>\mu_{G^{\prime}}P^{\prime}>\mu_{G^{\prime}(k)}\mu_{G^{\prime}}P^{\prime}
,

(c)
\Omega(Q;G_{l})=\Omega(\mu_{G^{\prime}(k)}\mu_{G^{\prime}}P^{\prime};G^{\prime}(k))+\Omega(\mu_{G^{\prime}}P^{\prime};G^{\prime})
,

In particular,
\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(Q)=\{\mu_{G^{\prime}}P^{\prime},\mu_{G^{\prime}(k)}\mu_{G^{\prime}}P^{\prime}\mid P^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P)\}
.

Figure 13
.

(3)

In case
(m_{k}(P),m_{k+1}(P))=(0,1)
, we have
(m_{k}(Q),m_{k+1}(Q))=(1,0)
. There is a unique tile
G^{\prime}
between
G^{\prime}(k)
and
G^{\prime}(k+1)
and its diagonal is labeled
\alpha_{1}
. Assume that
P^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P)
with
P^{\prime}<\mu_{G^{\prime}(k+1)}P^{\prime}
. Then
P^{\prime}
can twist on
G^{\prime}
, see Figure
14
. Moreover,

(a)
\mu_{G^{\prime}}P^{\prime},\mu_{G^{\prime}(k)}\mu_{G^{\prime}}P^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(Q)
, (b)
\mu_{G^{\prime}(k+1)}P^{\prime}>P^{\prime}>\mu_{G^{\prime}}P^{\prime}>\mu_{G^{\prime}(k)}\mu_{G^{\prime}}P^{\prime}
,

(c)
\Omega(Q;G_{l})=\Omega(\mu_{G^{\prime}(k)}\mu_{G^{\prime}}P^{\prime};G^{\prime}(k))+\Omega(\mu_{G^{\prime}}P^{\prime};G^{\prime})
.

Figure 14
.

(4)

In all cases, for any
Q^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(Q)
we have

m(Q;\alpha_{1})-n(G_{T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}};\alpha_{1})=m(Q^{\prime};\alpha_{1})-n(G_{T^{\prime o},\widetilde{\beta}};\alpha_{1})+m(Q^{\prime};\alpha^{\prime})-n(G_{T^{\prime o},\widetilde{\beta}};\alpha^{\prime}).

Proof.

Since
P>Q
, we have
N(G_{l})\in P,W(G_{l})\in Q
. Thus
m_{k}(P)=m_{k}(Q)-1,m_{k+1}(P)=m_{k+1}(Q)+1
for some
k
and
m_{i}(P)=m_{i}(Q)
for any
i\neq k,k+1
. It follows that
m_{k}(P)\in\{-1,0\}
and
m_{k+1}(P)\in\{0,1\}
. Since
\widetilde{\beta}
can not cross
\alpha,\alpha_{1},\alpha
consecutively, we have
(m_{k}(P),m_{k+1}(P))\neq(-1,0)
. Therefore,
(m_{k}(P),m_{k+1}(P))=(-1,1)
,
(0,0)
or
(0,1)
.

We only consider the case that
(m_{k}(P),m_{k+1}(P))=(-1,1)
, as the other cases can be proved similarly. We have the diagonal of
G_{l-1}
is labeled
\alpha
and
S(G_{l-1})\in P\cap Q
. We may assume
rel(G^{\prime}(k+1),T^{\prime o})=1
. As
E(G_{l})
is labeled
\alpha_{4}
, from the construction of snake groups, we have
E(G^{\prime}(k+1))
is labeled
\alpha_{4}
and the diagonal of
G^{\prime}
is labeled
\alpha_{1}
. For any
Q^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(Q)
, since
m_{k}(Q)=m_{k}(P)+1=0
, we have
m_{k}(Q^{\prime})=0
by Proposition
7.15
. Thus we have
N(G^{\prime})\in Q^{\prime}
. As
S(G_{l-1})
is labeled
\alpha_{1}
and
S(G_{l-1})\in Q
, we have
S(G^{\prime})\in Q^{\prime}
. Thus
Q^{\prime}
can twist on
G^{\prime}
. For any
P^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P)
, since
m_{k}(P)=-1
, we have
m_{k}(P^{\prime})=1
by Proposition
7.15
. Thus we have
W(G^{\prime})\in P^{\prime}
. Therefore,
\mu_{G^{\prime}}Q^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P)
. By Proposition
7.14
(2),
\mu_{G^{\prime}}Q^{\prime}
can twist on
G^{\prime}(k+1)
and
\mu_{G^{\prime}(k+1)}\mu_{G^{\prime}}Q^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P)
.

Q^{\prime}<\mu_{G^{\prime}}Q^{\prime}<\mu_{G^{\prime}(k+1)}\mu_{G^{\prime}}Q^{\prime}
is immediate. By Proposition
7.12
(3) (4), we have

m^{\pm}(Q,G_{l};\alpha_{1})=m^{\pm}(Q^{\prime},G^{\prime};\alpha_{1})+m^{\pm}(\mu_{G^{\prime}}Q^{\prime},G^{\prime}(k+1);\alpha^{\prime})-n((G^{\prime}(k+1))^{\pm};\alpha^{\prime}),

m^{+}(Q^{\prime},G^{\prime};\alpha_{1})=m^{+}(Q,G_{l};\alpha_{1})+m^{+}(P,G_{l-1};\alpha)-n(G_{l-1}^{+};\alpha)-1,

m^{-}(Q^{\prime},G^{\prime};\alpha_{1})=m^{-}(Q,G_{l};\alpha_{1})+m^{-}(P,G_{l-1};\alpha)-n(G_{l-1}^{-};\alpha)-1.

We have
n(G_{l}^{\pm};\alpha_{1})=n(G^{\prime\pm};\alpha_{1})
.
Therefore, we have

\Omega(Q;G_{l})=\Omega(Q^{\prime};G^{\prime})+\Omega(\mu_{G^{\prime}}Q^{\prime};G^{\prime}(k+1)),\;\;\;\Omega(Q^{\prime};G^{\prime})=\Omega(Q;G_{l})+\Omega(P;G_{l+1})

and

m(Q;\alpha_{1})-n(G_{T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}};\alpha_{1})=m(Q^{\prime};\alpha_{1})-n(G_{T^{\prime o},\widetilde{\beta}};\alpha_{1})+m(Q^{\prime};\alpha^{\prime})-n(G_{T^{\prime o},\widetilde{\beta}};\alpha^{\prime}).

\square

Lemma 7.19
.

We have
P^{\prime}_{-}\in\varphi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(P_{-})
.

Proof.

Assume
P^{\prime}
is the minimum element in
\varphi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}(P_{-})
. For any tile
G^{\prime}
that
P^{\prime}
can twist on, as
P_{-}
is the minimum perfect matching, we have
P^{\prime}<\mu_{G^{\prime}}P^{\prime}
by Proposition
7.17
and Proposition
7.18
. It follows that
P^{\prime}=P^{\prime}_{-}
.

\square

The following result follows by Lemma
7.11
.

Lemma 7.20
.

For
P_{-}
, we have
m_{i}(P_{-})m_{j}(P_{-})\geq 0
for any
i,j\in\{1,\cdots,\eta_{\alpha}\}
.

Proof.

Otherwise,
m_{i}(P_{-})m_{j}(P_{-})<0
for some
i,j\in\{1,\cdots,\eta_{\alpha}\}
. We see that
\gamma
intersects itself by Lemma
7.11
, a contradiction.

\square

8.
Partition bijection between triangles

With the notation in Section
4.2
. Let
q
be a puncture. Let
\Delta(T^{o},q)
(resp.
\Delta(T^{\prime o},q)
) be the set of triangles incident to
q
in
T^{o}
(resp.
T^{\prime o}=\mu_{\alpha}(T^{o})
). In this section, we give a partition bijection
\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}:\Delta(T^{o},q)\to\Delta(T^{\prime o},q)
.

8.1.
Once-puncture polygon case

We first consider the case that
\Sigma
is the once-punctured polygon
P_{n,1}
, the disk with
n
marked points labeled
1,\cdots,n
clockwise and one puncture labeled
0
. Let
T^{o}
be a triangulation of
P_{n,1}
. Assume that
\Delta(T^{o},0)=\{\Delta_{1},\cdots,\Delta_{t}\}
and for any
i\in\{1,\cdots,t\}
the three edges of
\Delta_{i}
are labeled
\tau_{i^{-}},\tau_{i},\tau_{[i]}
, where we denote

\begin{array}[]{ccl}i^{+}&=&\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}i+1,&\mbox{if $1\leq i\leq t-1$},\\
1,&\mbox{if $i=t$},\end{array}\right.\end{array},\begin{array}[]{ccl}i^{-}&=&\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}i-1,&\mbox{if $2\leq i\leq t$},\\
t,&\mbox{if $i=1$}.\end{array}\right.\end{array}

Fix an arc
\alpha\in T^{o}
, we have one of the following cases happens: (1)
\alpha=\tau_{i}
for some
i
; (2)
\alpha=\tau_{[i]}
for some
i
; (3) the rest cases.

For any
\Delta_{j}\in\Delta(T^{o},0)
, we associate with a subset
\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{j})\subset\Delta(T^{\prime o},0)
as follows.

Case I:
\alpha=\tau_{i}
for some
i
.
Then
\Delta(T^{\prime o},0)=\{(\tau_{i^{-}},\tau_{i^{+}},\alpha^{\prime}),\Delta_{j}\mid j\neq i,i^{+}\}
. In this case, let

\begin{array}[]{ccl}\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{j})&=&\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(\tau_{i^{-}},\tau_{i^{+}},\alpha^{\prime}),&\mbox{if $j=i$ or $i^{+}$},\\
\Delta_{j},&\mbox{if $j\neq i,i^{+}$},\end{array}\right.\end{array}

Case II:
\alpha=\tau_{[i]}
for some
i
.
Then
\tau_{i-1},\alpha^{\prime}
are the two sides of some triangle
\hat{\Delta}_{1}
in
T^{\prime o}
and
\tau_{i},\alpha^{\prime}
are the two sides of some triangle
\hat{\Delta}_{2}
in
T^{\prime o}
. We have
\Delta(T^{\prime o},0)=\{\hat{\Delta}_{1},\hat{\Delta}_{2},\Delta_{j}\mid j\neq i\}
.

In this case, let

\begin{array}[]{ccl}\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{j})&=&\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\{\hat{\Delta}_{1},\hat{\Delta}_{2}\},&\mbox{if $j=i$},\\
\Delta_{j},&\mbox{if $j\neq i$},\end{array}\right.\end{array}

Case III: the rest cases.
In this case we have
\Delta(T^{\prime o},0)=\Delta(T^{o},0)
. In this case let
\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{j})=\Delta_{j}
for all
j\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
.

Similarly we can associate a subset
\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{\prime o},q}(\Delta^{\prime})
of
\Delta(T^{o},0)
for any
\Delta^{\prime}\in\Delta(T^{\prime o},0)
.

Assume that
\Delta(T^{\prime o},0)=\{\Delta^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\Delta^{\prime}_{s^{\prime}}\}
and for any
i\in\{1,\cdots,s^{\prime}\}
the three edges of
\Delta^{\prime}_{i}
are labeled
\tau^{\prime}_{i^{-}},\tau^{\prime}_{i},\tau^{\prime}_{[i]}
.

Proposition 8.1
.

With the foregoing notation.
\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}
is a partition bijection from
\Delta(T^{o},0)
to
\Delta(T^{\prime o},0)
with inverse
\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{\prime o},q}
. For any
\Delta_{i}\in\Delta(T^{o},0)
we have

|\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{i})|=2^{[m(\Delta_{i};\alpha)]_{+}},

where
m(\Delta_{i};\alpha)
is given by (
11
), moreover, if
m(\Delta_{i};\alpha)=1
, then
\alpha=\tau_{[i]}
and
\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{i})=\{\Delta^{\prime}_{j},\Delta^{\prime}_{j+1}\}
for some
j
such that
\tau^{\prime}_{j}=\alpha^{\prime}
.

Proof.

The result follows easily by checking all the above three cases.

\square

The following result is clear.

Lemma 8.2
.

For any
\Delta_{i}\in\Delta(T^{o},0)
and
\Delta^{\prime}\in\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{i})
, we have
m(\Delta_{i};\alpha)=-m(\Delta^{\prime};\alpha^{\prime})
.

Next, we generalize the above construction for a sequence of non-self-folded arcs
\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}\in T^{o}
. Let
\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}\in T^{o}
be a sequence of arcs such that
\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j}
are not two sides of any triangulation in
T^{o}
for any
i\neq j
. Denote by
\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\alpha^{\prime}_{k}
the new arcs obtained from
T^{o}
by flip at
\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}
, respectively. For any triangle
\Delta_{i}\in\Delta(T^{o},0)
, recall that the three sides of
\Delta_{i}
are
\tau_{i-1},\tau_{i}
and
\tau_{[i]}
, one and exactly one of the following cases happens:

(i)

Case I:
\tau_{i-1}=\alpha_{a}
for some
a\in\{1,\cdots,k\}
;

(ii)

Case II:
\tau_{i}=\alpha_{a}
for some
a\in\{1,\cdots,k\}
;

(iii)

Case III:
\tau_{[i]}=\alpha_{a}
for some
a\in\{1,\cdots,k\}
;

(iv)

Case IV:
\tau_{i-1},\tau_{i},\tau_{[i]}\neq\alpha_{a}
for any
a\in\{1,\cdots,k\}
.

We associate a subset
\phi_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{i})\subset\Delta(\mu_{\alpha_{k}}\cdots\mu_{\alpha_{1}}T^{o},0)
as follows:

(i)

In case I, we have
\tau_{i-2},\tau_{i},\alpha^{\prime}_{a}
form a triangle in
\mu_{\alpha_{k}}\cdots\mu_{\alpha_{1}}T^{o}
, let

\phi_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{i})=(\tau_{i-2},\tau_{i},\alpha^{\prime}_{a});

(ii)

In case II, we have
\tau_{i-1},\tau_{i+1},\alpha^{\prime}_{a}
form a triangle in
\mu_{\alpha_{k}}\cdots\mu_{\alpha_{1}}T^{o}
, let

\phi_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{i})=(\tau_{i-1},\tau_{i+1},\alpha^{\prime}_{a});

(iii)

In case III, we have
\tau_{i-1},\alpha^{\prime}
are the two sides of some triangle
\hat{\Delta}_{1}
in
\mu_{\alpha_{k}}\cdots\mu_{\alpha_{1}}T^{o}
and
\tau_{i},\alpha^{\prime}
are the two sides of some triangle
\hat{\Delta}_{2}
in
\mu_{\alpha_{k}}\cdots\mu_{\alpha_{1}}T^{o}
, let

\phi_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{i})=\{\hat{\Delta}_{1},\hat{\Delta}_{2}\};

(iv)

In case IV, let

\phi_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{i})=\Delta_{i}.

Similarly, we can associate a subset
\phi_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\alpha^{\prime}_{k}}^{\mu_{\alpha_{k}}\cdots\mu_{\alpha_{1}}T^{o},q}(\Delta^{\prime}_{i})\subset\Delta(T^{o},0)
for any
\Delta^{\prime}_{i}\in\Delta(\mu_{\alpha_{k}}\cdots\mu_{\alpha_{1}}T^{o},0)
.

Proposition 8.3
.

With the foregoing notation. We have
\phi_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}^{T^{o},q}
and
\phi_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\alpha^{\prime}_{k}}^{\mu_{\alpha_{k}}\cdots\mu_{\alpha_{1}}T^{o},q}
are partition bijections inverse to each other, moreover, for any
\Delta_{i}\in\Delta(0)
we have

|\phi_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{i})|=2^{\sum_{j=1}^{k}[m(\Delta_{i};\alpha_{j})]_{+}},

where
m(\Delta_{i};\alpha_{j})
is given by (
11
), moreover, if
m(\Delta_{i};\alpha_{j})=1
for some
j
, then
\alpha_{j}=\tau_{[i]}
and
\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{i})=\{\Delta^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}},\Delta^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}+1}\}
for some
j^{\prime}
such that
\tau^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}}=\alpha^{\prime}_{i}
.

Proof.

The result follows easily by checking all the above three cases.

\square

Note that
\sum_{j=1}^{k}[m(\Delta_{i}(q);\alpha_{j})]_{+}=1,0
or
-1
.

The following result follows immediately by Lemma
8.2
.

Lemma 8.4
.

For any
\Delta_{i}\in\Delta(T^{o},0)
and
\Delta^{\prime}\in\phi_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{i})
, we have
m(\Delta_{i};\alpha_{j})=-m(\Delta^{\prime};\alpha^{\prime}_{j})
for any
j\in\{1,\cdots,k\}
.

8.2.
General case

The following is a generalization of “canonical polygon” in
[
2
]
to “canonical once - polygon”.

Theorem 8.5
.

Let
T^{o}
be an ideal triangulation of
\Sigma
. For any puncture
q
of
\Delta
, assume that
\Delta(T^{o},q)=\{\Delta_{1}(q),\cdots,\Delta_{s}(q)\}
with the three sides of
\Delta_{i}(q)
are
\tau_{i-1},\tau_{i}
and
\tau_{[i]}
. Then there is a morphism
\pi
from
P_{s,1}
to
\Sigma
such that
\pi(0i)=\tau_{i}
for all
i=1,\cdots,s
and
\pi(i,i^{+})=\tau_{[i^{+}]}
.

We call
P_{s,1}
together with the morphism
\pi
the
canonical-once punctured polygon
for
q
concerning
T^{o}
. It is clear that
\Delta_{i}(q)
corresponds to a unique triangle
\Delta_{i}
incident to
0
in
P_{s,1}
for any
i
.

Example 8.6
.

In the twice punctured digon with triangulation
T^{o}
, as shown in the right below figure. We have
\alpha_{2}=\alpha_{4}
,
\alpha_{[2]}=\alpha_{5}
,
\alpha_{[3]}=\alpha_{3}=\alpha_{[4]}
and
\alpha_{[5]}=\alpha_{1}
. Then the canonical once-punctured polygon for
0
is shown in the left below figure.

Figure 15.
Canonical once-punctured polygon for puncture
0

For any non-self-folded arc
\alpha\in T^{o}
, let
T^{\prime o}=\mu_{\alpha}(T^{o})
, we now construct partition bijection
\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}
from
\Delta(T^{o},q)
to
\Delta(T^{\prime o},0)
.

In case
\alpha\notin\{\alpha_{i},\alpha_{[i]}\mid i=1,\cdots,s\}
, we have
\Delta(T^{\prime o},0)=\Delta(T^{o},0)
. Then let
\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{i})=\Delta_{i}
for any
i=1,\cdots,s
.

In case
\alpha\in\{\alpha_{i},\alpha_{[i]}\mid i=1,\cdots,s\}
, we can extend the canonical once punctured polygon
(P_{s,1},\pi)
to a bigger once punctured polygon
P_{n,1}
for some
n>s
with an ideal triangulation
\hat{T}^{o}
and a morphism
\pi^{\prime}:P_{n,1}\to\Sigma
such that (1)
\pi^{\prime}|_{P_{s,1}}=\pi
, (2) the preimages of
\alpha
are inner arcs, and (3)
\pi^{\prime}(\Delta)
is a triangle in
T^{o}
for any triangle
\Delta
in
\hat{T}^{o}
.

Assume that
\pi^{-1}(\alpha)=\{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}\}\subset\hat{T}^{o}
. It is easy to see that
\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j}
are not two sides of any triangulation in
\hat{T}^{o}
for all
i\neq j
from the construction of the canonical once punctured polygon.

For any
\Delta_{i}(q)\in\Delta(T^{o},q)
, let
\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{i}(q))=\pi^{\prime}(\phi_{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{i}))
, where
\Delta_{i}
is the triangle incident to
0
in the canonical once punctured polygon. Similarly, we can define
\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{\prime o},q}
.

Proposition 8.7
.

With the foregoing notation.
\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}
is a partition bijection from
\Delta(T^{o},q)
to
\Delta(T^{\prime o},q)
with inverse
\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{\prime o},q}
, moreover, for any
\Delta_{i}(q)\in\Delta(T^{o},q)
we have

|\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{i}(q))|=2^{[m(\Delta_{i}(q);\alpha)]_{+}},

where
m(\Delta_{i}(q);\alpha)
is given by (
11
).

Proof.

It follows immediately by Proposition
8.3
.

\square

The following lemma is clear.

Lemma 8.8
.

We have
\Delta_{1}^{\prime}(q)\in\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{1}(q))
, where
\Delta^{\prime}_{1}(q)
is the first triangle incident to
q
in
T^{\prime o}
.

By Lemma
8.4
, we have

Lemma 8.9
.

For any
\Delta\in\Delta(T^{o},q)
and
\Delta^{\prime}\in\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta)
, we have
m(\Delta;\alpha)=-m(\Delta^{\prime};\alpha^{\prime})
.

Lemma 8.10
.

For any
\Delta\in\Delta(T^{o},q)
and
\Delta^{\prime}\in\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta)
, for any
\tau\in T^{o}
,

(1)

if
\tau\neq\alpha,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4}
, then
m(\Delta;\tau)=m(\Delta^{\prime};\tau)
;

(2)

if
\tau\in\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4}\}
and
m(\Delta;\alpha)=0
, then
m(\Delta;\tau)=m(\Delta^{\prime};\tau)
.

Notation

We fix some notation throughout the rest of this paper. Fix an arc
\widetilde{\beta}
, an ideal triangulation
T^{o}
and a non-self-folded
\alpha\in T^{o}
, let
T^{\prime o}=\mu_{\alpha}T^{o}
and
\alpha^{\prime}
be the new arc obtained. Suppose that
\alpha
is a diagonal of the quadrilateral
(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4})
in
T^{o}
and
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{3}
are in the clockwise direction of
\alpha
. As
\alpha
is not folded in
T^{o}
,
\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{3}\}\neq\{\alpha_{2},\alpha_{4}\}
.

Denote by
G_{T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}}
(resp.
G_{T^{\prime o},\widetilde{\beta}}
) the associated snake graph with tiles
G_{1},\cdots,G_{c}
(resp.
G^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,G^{\prime}_{c^{\prime}}
) in order and
\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}})
(resp.
\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{\prime o},\widetilde{\beta}})
) the set of all perfect matching. Denote by
P_{\pm}
(resp.
P^{\prime}_{\pm}
) the maximal/minimal perfect matching. Denote by
\tau_{i_{l}}
(resp.
\tau^{\prime}_{i_{l}}
) the label of the diagonal of
G_{l}
(resp.
G^{\prime}_{l}
).

If
t(\widetilde{\beta})=q
is a puncture, denote by
\Delta_{1}(q),\Delta_{2}(q),\cdots,\Delta_{t}(q)
(resp.
\Delta^{\prime}_{1}(q),\Delta^{\prime}_{2}(q),\cdots,\Delta^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}(q)
) the triangles incident to
q
in
T^{o}
(resp.
T^{\prime o}
) in clockwise order such that
\widetilde{\beta}
crosses
\Delta_{1}(q)
(resp.
\Delta^{\prime}_{1}(q)
) or
\widetilde{\beta}
is the common side of
\Delta_{1}(q)
and
\Delta_{t}(q)
(resp.
\Delta^{\prime}_{1}(q)
and
\Delta^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}(q)
). Denote the common side of
\Delta_{i}(q)
and
\Delta_{i+1}(q)
(resp.
\Delta^{\prime}_{i}(q)
and
\Delta^{\prime}_{i+1}(q)
) by
\tau_{i}(q)
(resp.
\tau^{\prime}_{i}(q)
). Denote the third side of
\Delta_{i}(q)
(resp.
\Delta^{\prime}_{i}(q)
) by
\tau_{[i]}(q)
(resp.
\tau^{\prime}_{[i]}(q)
).

If
s(\widetilde{\beta})=p
is a puncture, denote by
\Delta_{1}(p),\Delta_{2}(p),\cdots,\Delta_{s}(p)
(resp.
\Delta^{\prime}_{1}(p),\Delta^{\prime}_{2}(p),\cdots,\Delta^{\prime}_{s^{\prime}}(p)
) the triangles incident to
p
in
T^{o}
(resp.
T^{\prime o}
) in clockwise order such that
\widetilde{\beta}
crosses
\Delta_{1}(p)
(resp.
\Delta^{\prime}_{1}(p)
) or
\widetilde{\beta}
is the common side of
\Delta_{1}(p)
and
\Delta_{s}(p)
(resp.
\Delta^{\prime}_{1}(p)
and
\Delta^{\prime}_{s^{\prime}}(p)
). Denote the common side of
\Delta_{i}(p)
and
\Delta_{i+1}(p)
(resp.
\Delta^{\prime}_{i}(p)
and
\Delta^{\prime}_{i+1}(p)
) by
\tau_{i}(p)
(resp.
\tau^{\prime}_{i}(p)
). Denote the third side of
\Delta_{i}(p)
(resp.
\Delta^{\prime}_{i}(p)
) by
\tau_{[i]}(p)
(resp.
\tau^{\prime}_{[i]}(p)
).

If
\widetilde{\beta}\notin T^{o}
and
t(\widetilde{\beta})=q
, denote

E_{1}(q)=\begin{cases}E(G_{c}),&\mbox{if $rel(G_{c},T^{o})=1$},\\
N(G_{c}),&\mbox{if $rel(G_{c},T^{o})=-1$,}\end{cases}\;\;\;E_{2}(q)=\begin{cases}N(G_{c}),&\mbox{if $rel(G_{c},T^{o})=1$},\\
E(G_{c}),&\mbox{if $rel(G_{c},T^{o})=-1$.}\end{cases}

If
\widetilde{\beta}\notin T^{o}
and
s(\widetilde{\beta})=p
, denote

E_{1}(p)=\begin{cases}W(G_{1}),&\mbox{if $rel(G_{1},T^{o})=1$},\\
S(G_{1}),&\mbox{if $rel(G_{1},T^{o})=-1$,}\end{cases}\;\;\;E_{2}(p)=\begin{cases}S(G_{1}),&\mbox{if $rel(G_{1},T^{o})=1$},\\
W(G_{1}),&\mbox{if $rel(G_{1},T^{o})=-1$.}\end{cases}

If
\widetilde{\beta}\notin T^{\prime o}
and
t(\widetilde{\beta})=q
, denote

E^{\prime}_{1}(q)=\begin{cases}E(G^{\prime}_{c^{\prime}}),&\mbox{if $rel(G^{\prime}_{c^{\prime}},T^{\prime o})=1$},\\
N(G^{\prime}_{c^{\prime}}),&\mbox{if $rel(G^{\prime}_{c^{\prime}},T^{\prime o})=-1$,}\end{cases}\;\;\;E^{\prime}_{2}(q)=\begin{cases}N(G^{\prime}_{c^{\prime}}),&\mbox{if $rel(G^{\prime}_{c^{\prime}},T^{\prime o})=1$},\\
E(G^{\prime}_{c^{\prime}}),&\mbox{if $rel(G^{\prime}_{c^{\prime}},T^{\prime o})=-1$.}\end{cases}

If
\widetilde{\beta}\notin T^{o}
and
s(\widetilde{\beta})=p
, denote

E^{\prime}_{1}(p)=\begin{cases}W(G^{\prime}_{1}),&\mbox{if $rel(G^{\prime}_{1},T^{\prime o})=1$},\\
S(G^{\prime}_{1}),&\mbox{if $rel(G^{\prime}_{1},T^{\prime o})=-1$,}\end{cases}\;\;\;E^{\prime}_{2}(p)=\begin{cases}S(G^{\prime}_{1}),&\mbox{if $rel(G^{\prime}_{1},T^{\prime o})=1$},\\
W(G^{\prime}_{1}),&\mbox{if $rel(G^{\prime}_{1},T^{\prime o})=-1$.}\end{cases}

Let
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}),\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(q)})
or
\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)})
. Let
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}=\mathcal{L}(T^{\prime o},\widetilde{\beta}),\mathcal{L}(T^{\prime o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(q)})
or
\mathcal{L}(T^{\prime o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)})
correspondingly. Denote the minimal elements in
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
by
{\bf P}_{-}
and
{\bf P}^{\prime}_{-}
, respectively.

9.
Partition bijection between lattices
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}

For any pair
{\bf P},{\bf Q}\in\mathcal{L}
, assume that
{\bf P}
covers
{\bf Q}
, then we have that
{\bf P}
and
{\bf Q}
are either related by a twist on a tile
G_{l}
or related by two adjacent triangles incident to
p
or
q
, assume
\tau
is the label of the diagonal or the common side of the triangles. In both cases, we say that
{\bf P}
covers
{\bf Q}
and
related by
\tau
. We denote by

\Omega({\bf P},{\bf Q})=w({\bf P})-w({\bf Q}).

We say that
{\bf P},{\bf Q}\in\mathcal{L}
are
related by a twist at
\tau
if either
{\bf P}
covers
{\bf Q}
and related by
\tau
or
{\bf Q}
covers
{\bf P}
and related by
\tau
.

In this section, we construct a partition bijection from
\mathcal{L}
to
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
via the partition bijections
\varphi^{T^{o}}_{\alpha}:\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}})\to\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{\prime o},\widetilde{\beta}})
and
\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}:\Delta(T^{o},q)\to\Delta(T^{\prime o},q)
, which are given in Section
7
.

Partition bijection
\pi:\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta})\to\mathcal{L}(T^{\prime o},\widetilde{\beta})

Herein, we assume that
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta})
and
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}=\mathcal{L}(T^{\prime o},\widetilde{\beta})
.

Recall that for any
P\in\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}})
we have
{\bf m}(P,\alpha)=(m_{1}(P),\cdots,m_{\eta_{\alpha}}(P))\in\{-1,0,1\}^{\eta_{\alpha}}
.

Definition 9.1
.

[
22
,
23
]

For any
P\in\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}})
, we choose pairs of indices in
\{1,\cdots,\eta_{\alpha}\}
via the following algorithm:

(i)

If
m_{i}(P)\geq 0
for all
i=1,\cdots,\eta_{\alpha}
or
m_{i}(P)\leq 0
for all
i=1,\cdots,\eta_{\alpha}
, then nothing is chosen;

(ii)

If
m_{i}(P)m_{j}(P)=-1
for some
i,j
, choose the pair of indices
(a,b)
with
a<b
such that (1)
m_{a}(P)m_{b}(P)=-1
, (2)
m_{\ell}(P)=0
for all
a<\ell<b
, (3)
m_{a}(P)m_{\ell}(P)\geq 0
for all
\ell<a
.

(iii)

Delete
m_{a}(P)
and
m_{b}(P)
from
{\bf m}(P,\alpha)
. Then return to step (i).

We call any above chosen pair
(a,b)
of indices an
{\bf m}(P,\alpha)
-pair
.

Example 9.2
.

If
{\bf m}(P,\alpha)=(1,1,0,-1,0,1,-1,1)
. Then all of the
{\bf m}(P,\alpha)
-pairs are
(2,4)
and
(6,7)
. If
{\bf m}(P,\alpha)=(1,1,-1,0-1,1)
. Then all the the
{\bf m}(P,\alpha)
-pairs are
(2,3)
and
(1,5)
.

The following is immediate.

Lemma 9.3
.

For
{\bf m}(P,\alpha)
, we have

(1)

{\bf m}(P,\alpha)
-pairs and
-{\bf m}(P,\alpha)
-pairs coincide.

(2)

For any
{\bf m}(P,\alpha)
-pair
(a,b)
with
a<b
we have

\sum_{a<\ell<b}m_{\ell}(P)=0.

(3)

If
\sum m_{i}(P)\geq 0
then for any
a
with
m_{a}(P)<0
we have
a
is in some
{\bf m}(P,\alpha)
-pair.

(4)

If
\sum m_{i}(P)\leq 0
then for any
a
with
m_{a}(P)>0
we have
a
is in some
{\bf m}(P,\alpha)
-pair.

Let
(a,b)
be an
{\bf m}(P,\alpha)
-pair. Then either
m_{a}(P)=1,m_{b}(P)=-1
or
m_{a}(P)=-1,m_{b}(P)=1
. By Proposition
7.14
, in case
m_{a}(P)=1,m_{b}(P)=-1
, we have
P
can twist on the tile
G(b)
and any
P^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P)
can twist on
G^{\prime}(a)
; in case
m_{a}(P)=-1,m_{b}(P)=1
, we have
P
can twist on the tile
G(a)
and any
P^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P)
can twist on
G^{\prime}(b)
, where
G(a),G(b)
(resp.
G^{\prime}(a),G^{\prime}(b)
) are the tiles with diagonal labeled
\alpha
(resp.
\alpha^{\prime}
) given in Proposition
7.14
.

Construction of
\pi
.
For any
P\in\mathcal{L}
,

\pi(P)
contains all
P^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P)
satisfy the following condition: for any
{\bf m}(P,\alpha)
-pair
(a,b)
,

\bullet
if
m_{a}(P)=1,m_{b}(P)=-1
then the labels of
P^{\prime}\cap edge(G^{\prime}(a))
and
P\cap edge(G(b))
coincide;

\bullet
if
m_{a}(P)=-1,m_{b}(P)=1
then the labels of
P^{\prime}\cap edge(G^{\prime}(b))
and
P\cap edge(G(a))
coincide.

Similarly, we can construction a set
\pi^{\prime}(P^{\prime})\subset\mathcal{L}
for any
P^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}=\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{\prime o},\widetilde{\beta}})
.

Proposition 9.4
.

For any
P\in\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}})
we have

|\pi(P)|=2^{[\sum m_{i}(P)]_{+}},

to be precise, for any
P^{\prime}\in\pi(P)
, we have

(1)

{\bf m}(P^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})=-{\bf m}(P,\alpha)
,

(2)

if
m_{k}(P)=1
and
k
is not in any
{\bf m}(P,\alpha)
-pair then
P^{\prime}
can twist on the tiles
G^{\prime}(k)
and
\mu_{G^{\prime}(k)}P^{\prime}\in\pi(P)
.

where
G^{\prime}(k)
are the tiles given in Proposition
7.14
.

Proof.

It follows by Proposition
7.15
and the construction of
\pi(P)
.

\square

Remark 9.5
.

Let
P\in\mathcal{L}
.

(1)

Denote
r=[\sum m_{i}(P)]_{+}
. Denote by
\{k_{1},\cdots,k_{r}\}\subset\{1,\cdots,\eta_{\alpha}\}
the set of indices that are not in any
{\bf m}(P,\alpha)
-pair and
m_{k_{j}}=1
. Assume
k_{1}<k_{2}<\cdots<

k_{r}
. By Proposition
9.4
, we may write
\pi(P)
as

\{P(\vec{c})\mid\vec{c}=(c_{1},\cdots,c_{r})\in\{0,1\}^{r}\}.
To be precise, for any
j\in\{1,\cdots,r\}
the edges labeled
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{3}
of
G^{\prime}(k_{j})
are in
P(\vec{c})
if
c_{j}=1
and the the edges labeled
\alpha_{2},\alpha_{4}
of
G^{\prime}(k_{j})
are in
P(\vec{c})
if
c_{j}=0
.

(2)

Under the order of
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}=\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{\prime o},\widetilde{\beta}})
in Section
4
, we have
P(\vec{c})\leq P(\vec{c}\hskip 2.0pt^{\prime})
if and only if
c_{j}\leq c^{\prime}_{j}
for all
j\in\{1,\cdots,r\}
.

Partition bijection
\pi:\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(q)})\to\mathcal{L}(T^{\prime o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(q)})
.

Herein, we assume that
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(q)})
and
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}=\mathcal{L}(T^{\prime o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(q)})
.
Recall in (
11
) that

\begin{array}[]{rcl}m(\Delta_{j}(q);\alpha)&=&\hskip-5.69054pt\text{number of edges labeled }\alpha\text{ in }\{\tau_{[j]}(q)\}\\
&-&\hskip-5.69054pt\text{number of edges labeled }\alpha\text{ in }\{\tau_{j-1}(q),\tau_{j}(q)\}.\end{array}

Note that if
m(\Delta_{j}(q);\alpha)=1
then
\alpha=\tau_{[j]}(q)
and thus either
\tau_{j-1}(q)=\alpha_{1},\tau_{j}(q)=\alpha_{4}
or
\tau_{j-1}(q)=\alpha_{3},\tau_{j}(q)=\alpha_{2}
.

For
{\bf P}=(P,\Delta_{j}(q))\in\mathcal{L}
, denote
{\bf m}({\bf P};\alpha)=({\bf m}(P,\alpha),m(\Delta_{j}(q);\alpha))
. As
\alpha
is not a self-folded arc in
T^{o}
, we have
m(\Delta_{j}(q);\alpha)\in\{-1,0,1\}
and thus
{\bf m}({\bf P};\alpha)\in\{-1,0,1\}^{\eta_{\alpha}+1}
.

Definition 9.6
.

Assume that
{\bf m}({\bf P};\alpha)=(m_{1}({\bf P}),\cdots,m_{\eta_{\alpha}}({\bf P}),m_{\eta_{\alpha}+1}({\bf P}))
. We choose pairs of indices
(a,b)
via the following algorithm:

(i)

If
m_{\eta_{\alpha}}({\bf P})m_{\eta_{\alpha}+1}({\bf P})=-1
, then first choose
(\eta_{\alpha},\eta_{\alpha}+1)
and all of the
(m_{1}({\bf P}),\cdots,m_{\eta_{\alpha}-1}({\bf P}))
-pairs as in Definition
9.1
;

(ii)

If
m_{\eta_{\alpha}}({\bf P})m_{\eta_{\alpha}+1}({\bf P})\neq-1
, then we choose all of the
(m_{1}({\bf P}),\cdots,m_{\eta_{\alpha}+1}({\bf P})
-pairs as in Definition
9.1
.

The chosen pairs are called
{\bf m}({\bf P};\alpha)
-pairs
.

The following is immediate.

Lemma 9.7
.

For
{\bf m}(P,\alpha)
, we have

(1)

{\bf m}(P,\alpha)
-pairs and
-{\bf m}(P,\alpha)
-pairs coincide.

(2)

For any
{\bf m}(P,\alpha)
-pair
(a,b)
with
a<b
we have

\sum_{a<\ell<b}m_{\ell}(P)=0.

(3)

If
\sum m_{i}(P)\geq 0
then for any
a
with
m_{a}(P)<0
we have
a
is in some
{\bf m}(P,\alpha)
-pair.

(4)

If
\sum m_{i}(P)\leq 0
then for any
a
with
m_{a}(P)>0
we have
a
is in some
{\bf m}(P,\alpha)
-pair.

Construction of
\pi
.
For
{\bf P}=(P,\Delta_{j}(q))\in\mathcal{L}
,

\pi({\bf P})
contains all
(P^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P)\times\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{j}(q))
satisfy the following condition: for any
{\bf m}(P,\Delta_{j}(q);\alpha)
-pair
1
1

1

We use
{\bf m}(P,\Delta_{j}(q);\alpha)
-pair as in Definition
9.15
to ensure Proposition
9.12
holds

(a,b)
,

\bullet
if
m_{a}({\bf P})=1,m_{b}({\bf P})=-1
with

then the labels of
P^{\prime}\cap edge(G^{\prime}(a))
and

coincide;

\bullet
if
m_{a}({\bf P})=-1,m_{b}({\bf P})=1
with

then the labels of
P^{\prime}\cap edge(G^{\prime}(b))
and
P\cap edge(G(a))
coincide;

\bullet
if
m_{a}({\bf P})=1,m_{b}({\bf P})=-1
with
b=\eta_{\alpha}+1
then the edges labeled
\tau_{j-2}(q)
and
\tau_{[j]}(q)
of
edge(G^{\prime}(a))
are in
P^{\prime}
in case
\tau_{j-1}(q)=\alpha
, or the edges labeled
\tau_{j+1}(q)
and
\tau_{[j]}(q)
of
edge(G^{\prime}(a))
are in
P^{\prime}
in case
\tau_{j}(q)=\alpha
;

\bullet
if
m_{a}({\bf P})=-1,m_{b}({\bf P})=1
with

,

(i)

in case
\tau_{j-1}(q)=\alpha_{1},\tau_{j}(q)=\alpha_{4}
, then
\Delta^{\prime}=\{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4}\}
when the edges in
P\cap edge(G(a))
labeled
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{3}
, or
\Delta^{\prime}=\{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\}
when the edges in
P\cap edge(G(a))
labeled
\alpha_{2}
and
\alpha_{4}
.

(ii)

in case
\tau_{j-1}(q)=\alpha_{3},\tau_{j}(q)=\alpha_{2}
, then
\Delta^{\prime}=\{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\}
when the edges in
P\cap edge(G(a))
labeled
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{3}
, or
\Delta^{\prime}=\{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4}\}
when the edges in
P\cap edge(G(a))
labeled
\alpha_{2}
and
\alpha_{4}
.

Similarly, we can construction a set
\pi^{\prime}({\bf P}^{\prime})\subset\mathcal{L}
for any
{\bf P}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}=\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{\prime o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(q)}})
.

By Propositions
7.15
,
8.7
, from the construction, the following proposition follows.

Proposition 9.8
.

For any
{\bf P}=(P,\Delta_{j}(q))\in\mathcal{L}
we have

|\pi({\bf P})|=2^{[\sum m_{i}({\bf P})]_{+}},

to be precise, for any
{\bf P}^{\prime}=(P^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\in\pi({\bf P})
, we have

(1)

{\bf m}({\bf P}^{\prime};\alpha^{\prime})=-{\bf m}({\bf P};\alpha)
;

(2)

if
k\neq\eta_{\alpha}+1
is not in any
{\bf m}({\bf P};\alpha)
-pair and
m_{k}({\bf P})=1
, then
P^{\prime}
can twist on
G^{\prime}(k)
and
(\mu_{G^{\prime}(k)}P^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\in\pi({\bf P})
, where
G^{\prime}(k)
is the tile given in Proposition
7.14
;

(3)

if
\eta(\alpha)+1
is not in any
{\bf m}({\bf P};\alpha)
-pair and
m_{\eta_{\alpha}+1}({\bf P})=1
, then
(P^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime})\in\pi({\bf P})
, where
\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{j}(q))=\{\Delta^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}\}
.

Lemma 9.9
.

For any
{\bf P}=(P,\Delta_{j}(q))\in\mathcal{L}
, if
m(\Delta_{j}(q);\alpha)=1
, then
\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{j}(q))=\{\Delta^{\prime}_{1},\Delta^{\prime}_{2}\}
satisfies that
(P^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}_{1})<(P^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}_{2})
for any
P^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P)
.

Proof.

Otherwise, by Lemma
4.5
, we have
\Delta^{\prime}_{1}(q)\in\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{j}(q))
and there exist
P^{\prime},Q^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P)
such that
E^{\prime}_{1}(q)\in P^{\prime},E^{\prime}_{2}(q)\in Q^{\prime}
. Thus by Proposition
7.15
, the diagonal of
G^{\prime}_{c^{\prime}}
is labeled
\alpha^{\prime}
.
Thus
m(\Delta^{\prime}_{1}(q),\alpha^{\prime})=1
. It contradicts to
m(\Delta_{j}(q);\alpha)=1
and Lemma
8.9
.

\square

Remark 9.10
.

Denote
r=[\sum m_{i}({\bf P})]_{+}
. Denote by
\{k_{1},\cdots,k_{r}\}\subset\{1,\cdots,\eta_{\alpha}+1\}
the set of indices that are not in any
{\bf m}({\bf P};\alpha)
-pair and
m_{k_{\ell}}({\bf P})=1
for
\ell=1,\cdots,r
. Suppose that
k_{1}<k_{2}<\cdots<k_{r}
. By Proposition
9.8
, we may write
\pi({\bf P})
as

\{{\bf P}(\vec{c})\mid\vec{c}\in\{0,1\}^{r}\},
to be precise, assume that
{\bf P}(\vec{c})=(P^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}),
for any
\ell\in\{1,\cdots,r\}
,

(i)

if
k_{\ell}\neq\eta_{\alpha}+1
then the edges labeled
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{3}
of
G^{\prime}(k_{\ell})
are in
P
in case
c_{\ell}=1
and the the edges labeled
\alpha_{2},\alpha_{4}
of
G^{\prime}(k_{\ell})
are in
P
in case
c_{\ell}=0
;

(ii)

if
k_{\ell}=\eta_{\alpha}+1
, then
\Delta^{\prime}=\Delta^{\prime}_{1}
in case
c_{\ell}=0
and
\Delta^{\prime}=\Delta^{\prime}_{2}
in case
c_{\ell}=1
, where
\Delta^{\prime}_{1}
and
\Delta^{\prime}_{2}
are given by Lemma
9.9
.

Lemma 9.11
.

Suppose that
\widetilde{\beta}\notin T^{\prime o}
. For any
{\bf P}=(P,\Delta_{j}(q))\in\mathcal{L}
,

(1)

if
\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{j}(q))=\{\Delta^{\prime}_{1}(q),\Delta^{\prime}_{2}(q)\}
and
E^{\prime}_{2}(q)\in P^{\prime}
for some
P^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P)
, then
m_{\eta_{\alpha}}({\bf P})=-1,m_{\eta_{\alpha}+1}({\bf P})=1
;

(2)

If
\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{j}(q))=\{\Delta^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}(q),\Delta^{\prime}_{1}(q)\}
and
E^{\prime}_{1}(q)\in P^{\prime}
for some
P^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P)
, then
m_{\eta_{\alpha}}({\bf P})=-1,m_{\eta_{\alpha}+1}({\bf P})=1
.

Consequently, we have
(\eta_{\alpha},\eta_{\alpha}+1)
is an
{\bf m}({\bf P};\alpha)
-pair.

Proof.

We shall only prove the first statement, as the second one can be proved similarly. As
\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta)=\{\Delta^{\prime}_{1}(q),\Delta^{\prime}_{2}(q)\}
, we see that
\tau^{\prime}_{1}(q)=\alpha
and the diagonal of
G^{\prime}_{c^{\prime}}
is labeled
\alpha_{1}
or
\alpha_{3}
. It follows that
m(\Delta^{\prime}_{1}(q))=-1
and
E^{\prime}_{2}(q)
is labeled
\alpha^{\prime}
. Thus
m(\Delta_{j}(q))=1
and
m_{\eta_{\alpha}}(P^{\prime})=1
. It follows that
m_{\eta_{\alpha}}({\bf P})=-m_{\eta_{\alpha}}(P^{\prime})=-1
and
m_{\eta_{\alpha}+1}({\bf P})=m(\Delta_{j}(q))=1
.

\square

Proposition 9.12
.

Suppose that
r=\sum m_{i}({\bf P})\geq 0
. Given any
k
, for
\vec{c},\vec{c}\hskip 2.27621pt^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{r}
such that
c_{k}=1,c^{\prime}_{k}=0
and
c_{a}=c^{\prime}_{a}
for
a\neq k
, then
{\bf P}(\vec{c})
covers
{\bf P}(\vec{c}\hskip 2.27621pt^{\prime})
.

Proof.

Assume that
{\bf P}(\vec{c}\hskip 2.27621pt^{\prime})=(P^{\prime}_{1},\Delta^{\prime}_{1})
and
{\bf P}(\vec{c})=(P^{\prime}_{2},\Delta^{\prime}_{2})
. By Remark
9.10
, we have either
P^{\prime}_{1}=P^{\prime}_{2}
or
\Delta^{\prime}_{1}=\Delta^{\prime}_{2}
.

(Case I)
P^{\prime}_{1}=P^{\prime}_{2}
. We have
(P^{\prime}_{2},\Delta^{\prime}_{2})=(P^{\prime}_{1},\Delta^{\prime}_{2})>(P^{\prime}_{1},\Delta^{\prime}_{1})
by Remark
9.10
(2). By Lemma
4.5
,
(P^{\prime}_{1},\Delta^{\prime}_{2})
does not cover
(P^{\prime}_{1},\Delta^{\prime}_{1})
if and only if either
E^{\prime}_{2}(q)\in P^{\prime}_{1},\Delta^{\prime}_{2}=\Delta^{\prime}_{1}(q),\Delta^{\prime}_{1}=\Delta^{\prime}_{2}(q)
or
E^{\prime}_{1}(q)\in P^{\prime}_{1},\Delta^{\prime}_{2}=\Delta^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}(q),\Delta^{\prime}_{1}=\Delta^{\prime}_{1}(q)
.
We have
(\eta_{\alpha},\eta_{\alpha}+1)
is an
{\bf m}({\bf P};\alpha)
-pair by Lemma
9.11
. From the construction of
\pi
, we see that
(P^{\prime}_{1},\Delta^{\prime}_{1})
and
(P^{\prime}_{1},\Delta^{\prime}_{2})
can not belong to
\pi({\bf P})
, a contradiction.

(Case II)
\Delta^{\prime}_{1}=\Delta^{\prime}_{2}
. We have
P^{\prime}_{1}<P^{\prime}_{2}=\mu_{G^{\prime}_{l}}P^{\prime}_{1}
for some tile
G^{\prime}_{l}
with diagonal labeled
\alpha^{\prime}
by Remark
9.10
(1). If
(P^{\prime}_{2},\Delta^{\prime}_{2})=(\mu_{G^{\prime}_{l}}P^{\prime}_{1},\Delta^{\prime}_{1})
does not cover
(P^{\prime}_{1},\Delta^{\prime}_{1})
, by Lemma
4.4
, we have
\Delta^{\prime}_{1}=\Delta^{\prime}_{1}(q)
and
G^{\prime}_{l}=G^{\prime}_{c^{\prime}}
is the last tile of
G_{T^{\prime o},\widetilde{\beta}}
. It follows that
m_{\eta_{\alpha}+1}(P^{\prime}_{1},\Delta^{\prime}_{1})=1
and
m_{\eta_{\alpha}}(P^{\prime}_{1},\Delta^{\prime}_{1})=-1
. Thus
m_{\eta_{\alpha}+1}({\bf P})=-1
and
m_{\eta_{\alpha}}({\bf P})=1
. It follows that
(\eta_{\alpha},\eta_{\alpha}+1)
is an
{\bf m}({\bf P};\alpha)
-pair. From the construction of
\pi
, we see that
(P^{\prime}_{1},\Delta^{\prime}_{1})
and
(P^{\prime}_{2},\Delta^{\prime}_{1})
can not belong to
\pi({\bf P})
, a contradiction.

\square

Partition bijection
\pi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},p,q}:\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)})\to\mathcal{L}(T^{\prime o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)})

Herein we assume that
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)})
and
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}=\mathcal{L}(T^{\prime o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)})
.

Recall in (
12
) that

\begin{array}[]{rcl}m(\Delta_{i}(p);\alpha)&=&\hskip-5.69054pt\text{number of edges labeled }\alpha\text{ in }\{\tau_{[i]}(p)\}\\
&-&\hskip-5.69054pt\text{number of edges labeled }\alpha\text{ in }\{\tau_{i-1}(p),\tau_{i}(p)\}.\end{array}

Lemma 9.13
.

If
m(\Delta_{i}(p);\alpha)=1
and
m(\Delta_{j}(q);\alpha)=-1
, assume that
\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{j}(q))=\{\Delta^{\prime\prime}\}
, then there is a unique
\Delta^{\prime}\in\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},p}(\Delta_{i}(p))
such that

x^{T}(\Delta_{i}(p))x^{T}(\Delta_{j}(q))=x^{T^{\prime}}(\Delta^{\prime})x^{T^{\prime}}(\Delta^{\prime\prime}).

Proof.

Since
m(\Delta_{j}(q);\alpha)=-1
, we have the following cases:
\Delta_{j}(q)=\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{4},\alpha\}
,
\tau_{[j]}(q)=\alpha_{1}
;
\Delta_{j}(q)=\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{4},\alpha\}
,
\tau_{[j]}(q)=\alpha_{4}
;
\Delta_{j}(q)=\{\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha\}
,
\tau_{[j]}(q)=\alpha_{2}
;
\Delta_{j}(q)=\{\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha\}
,
\tau_{[j]}(q)=\alpha_{3}
. We shall only consider the case that
\Delta_{j}(q)=\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{4},\alpha\}
,
\tau_{[j]}(q)=\alpha_{1}
, as the remaining cases can be proved similarly. Then
x^{T}(\Delta_{j}(q))=x_{\alpha_{1}}x^{-1}_{\alpha_{4}}x^{-1}_{\alpha}
,
\Delta^{\prime\prime}=\{\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4},\alpha^{\prime}\}
with
m(\Delta^{\prime\prime};\alpha^{\prime})=1
and thus

x^{T^{\prime}}(\Delta^{\prime\prime})=x_{\alpha^{\prime}}x^{-1}_{\alpha_{3}}x^{-1}_{\alpha_{4}}

Since
m(\Delta_{i}(p);\alpha)=1
, we have
\tau_{[i]}(p)=\alpha
and
\Delta_{i}(p)=\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{4},\alpha\}
or
\{\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha\}
.

In case
\Delta_{i}(p)=\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{4},\alpha\}
, then
x^{T}(\Delta_{i}(p))=x_{\alpha}x^{-1}_{\alpha_{1}}x^{-1}_{\alpha_{4}}
. Thus
\Delta^{\prime}=\{\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4},\alpha^{\prime}\}
with
m(\Delta^{\prime};\alpha_{3})=1
. Therefore
x^{T}(\Delta_{i}(p))x^{T}(\Delta_{j}(q))=\frac{1}{x^{2}_{\alpha_{4}}}=x^{T^{\prime}}(\Delta^{\prime})x^{T^{\prime}}(\Delta^{\prime\prime}).
The uniqueness of
\Delta^{\prime}
follows as
\alpha_{2}=\alpha_{3}
and
\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{4}
can not hold simultaneous.

In case
\Delta_{i}(p)=\{\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha\}
, then
x^{T}(\Delta_{i}(p))=x_{\alpha}x^{-1}_{\alpha_{2}}x^{-1}_{\alpha_{3}}
. Thus
\Delta^{\prime}=\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha^{\prime}\}
with
m(\Delta^{\prime};\alpha_{1})=1
. Therefore
x^{T}(\Delta_{i}(p))x^{T}(\Delta_{j}(q))=\frac{x_{\alpha_{1}}}{x_{\alpha_{2}}x_{\alpha_{3}}x_{\alpha_{4}}}=x^{T^{\prime}}(\Delta^{\prime})x^{T^{\prime}}(\Delta^{\prime\prime}).
The uniqueness of
\Delta^{\prime}
follows as
\alpha_{2}=\alpha_{3}
and
\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{4}
can not hold simultaneous.

\square

Similarly, we have the following result.

Lemma 9.14
.

If
m(\Delta_{i}(p);\alpha)=-1
and
m(\Delta_{j}(q);\alpha)=1
, assume that
\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},p}(\Delta_{i}(p))=\{\Delta^{\prime}\}
, then there is a unique
\Delta^{\prime\prime}\in\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{j}(q))
such that

x^{T}(\Delta_{i}(p))x^{T}(\Delta_{j}(q))=x^{T^{\prime}}(\Delta^{\prime})x^{T^{\prime}}(\Delta^{\prime\prime}).

For
{\bf P}=(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))\in\mathcal{L}
, denote
{\bf m}({\bf P};\alpha)=(m(\Delta_{i}(p);\alpha),{\bf m}(P,\alpha),m(\Delta_{j}(q);\alpha))
. As
\alpha
is not a self-folded arc in
T^{o}
, we have
m(\Delta_{j}(q);\alpha)\in\{-1,0,1\}
and thus
{\bf m}({\bf P};\alpha)\in\{-1,0,1\}^{\eta_{\alpha}+1}
. Denote
{\bf m}({\bf P};\alpha)=(m_{0}({\bf P}),m_{1}({\bf P}),\cdots,m_{\eta_{\alpha}}({\bf P}),m_{\eta_{\alpha}+1}({\bf P}))
.

Definition 9.15
.

For
{\bf m}({\bf P};\alpha)
, we choose pairs of indices
(a,b)
via the following algorithm:

(i)

If
m_{0}({\bf P})m_{1}({\bf P})=-1
, then choose
(0,1)
and let
{\bf m}^{\prime}=(m_{2}({\bf P}),\cdots,m_{\eta_{\alpha}+1}({\bf P}))
, otherwise let
{\bf m}^{\prime}={\bf m}({\bf P};\alpha)
;

(ii)

In
{\bf m}^{\prime}
, if
m_{\eta_{\alpha}}({\bf P})m_{\eta_{\alpha}+1}({\bf P})=-1
, then choose
(\eta_{\alpha},\eta_{\alpha}+1)
and let
{\bf m}^{\prime\prime}
be obtained by delete
m_{\eta_{\alpha}}({\bf P}),m_{\eta_{\alpha}+1}({\bf P})
from
{\bf m}^{\prime}
, otherwise let
{\bf m}^{\prime\prime}={\bf m}^{\prime}
;

(iii)

Choose all of the
{\bf m}^{\prime\prime}
-pairs as in Definition
9.1
.

The chosen pairs are called
{\bf m}({\bf P};\alpha)
-pairs
.

The following is immediate.

Lemma 9.16
.

For
{\bf m}(P,\alpha)
, we have

(1)

{\bf m}(P,\alpha)
-pairs and
-{\bf m}(P,\alpha)
-pairs coincide.

(2)

(3)

If
\sum m_{i}(P)\geq 0

with
m_{a}(P)<0
we have

is in some

-pair.

(4)

If
\sum m_{i}(P)\leq 0

m_{a}(P)>0

is in some
{\bf m}(P,\alpha)
-pair.

Note that if
m_{0}({\bf P})=1

.

Construction of
\pi
.

and
\alpha_{4}
.

\bullet

and

then the edges labeled

and

of

, or the edges labeled

\bullet
if
m_{a}({\bf P})=-1,m_{b}({\bf P})=1
with
a\neq 0,b=\eta_{\alpha}+1
,

(i)

in case
\tau_{j-1}(q)=\alpha_{1},\tau_{j}(q)=\alpha_{4}
, then
\Delta^{\prime}=\{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4}\}
when the edges in
P\cap edge(G(a))
labeled
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{3}
, or
\Delta^{\prime}=\{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\}
when the edges in
P\cap edge(G(a))
labeled
\alpha_{2}
and
\alpha_{4}
.

(ii)

in case
\tau_{j-1}(q)=\alpha_{3},\tau_{j}(q)=\alpha_{2}
, then
\Delta^{\prime}=\{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\}
when the edges in
P\cap edge(G(a))
labeled
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{3}
, or
\Delta^{\prime}=\{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4}\}
when the edges in
P\cap edge(G(a))
labeled
\alpha_{2}
and
\alpha_{4}
.

\bullet
if
m_{a}({\bf P})=1,m_{b}({\bf P})=-1
with
a=0,b=\eta_{\alpha}+1
, then
\Delta^{\prime}
is given by Lemma
9.13
.

\bullet
if
m_{a}({\bf P})=-1,m_{b}({\bf P})=1
with
a=0,b=\eta_{\alpha}+1
, then
\Delta^{\prime\prime}
is given by Lemma
9.14
.

Similarly, we can construction a set
\pi^{\prime}({\bf P}^{\prime})\subset\mathcal{L}
for any
{\bf P}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}=\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{\prime o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)}})
.

By Propositions
7.15
,
8.7
, from the construction, the following proposition follows.

Proposition 9.17
.

For any
{\bf P}=(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))\in\mathcal{L}
we have

|\pi({\bf P})|=2^{[\sum m_{a}({\bf P})]_{+}},

more precisely, for any
{\bf P}^{\prime}=(\Delta^{\prime},P^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime})\in\pi({\bf P})
,

(1)

{\bf m}({\bf P}^{\prime})=-{\bf m}({\bf P})
;

(2)

if
k(\neq 0,\eta_{\alpha}+1)
is not in any
{\bf m}({\bf P};\alpha)
-pair and
m_{k}({\bf P})=1
, then
P^{\prime}
can twist on
G^{\prime}(k)
and
(\Delta^{\prime},\mu_{G^{\prime}(k)}P^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime})\in\pi({\bf P})
;

(3)

if
0
is not in any
{\bf m}({\bf P};\alpha)
-pair and
m_{0}({\bf P})=1
, then there is a triangle
\widetilde{\Delta}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{p}(T^{\prime o})
such that
\widetilde{\Delta}^{\prime}
and
\Delta^{\prime}
share the common side
\alpha^{\prime}
, and
(\widetilde{\Delta}^{\prime},P^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime})\in\pi({\bf P})
.

(4)

if
\eta(\alpha)+1
is not in any
{\bf m}({\bf P};\alpha)
-pair and
m_{\eta_{\alpha}+1}({\bf P})=1
, then there is a triangle
\widetilde{\Delta}^{\prime\prime}\in\Delta_{q}(T^{\prime o})
such that
\widetilde{\Delta}^{\prime\prime}
and
\Delta^{\prime\prime}
share the common side
\alpha^{\prime}
, and
(\Delta^{\prime},P^{\prime},\widetilde{\Delta}^{\prime\prime})\in\pi({\bf P})
.

where
G^{\prime}(k)
are the tiles given in Proposition
7.14
.

The following lemmas are similar to Lemma
9.9
, the proofs are also similar, we omit them.

Lemma 9.18
.

For any
{\bf P}=(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))\in\mathcal{L}
, if
m(\Delta_{j}(q);\alpha)=1
, then
\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{j}(q))=\{\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{1},\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{2}\}
satisfies that
(\Delta^{\prime},P^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{1})<(\Delta^{\prime},P^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{2})
for any
P^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P)
and
\Delta^{\prime}\in\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},p}(\Delta_{i}(p))
.

Lemma 9.19
.

For any
{\bf P}=(\Delta_{i}(p),P,\Delta_{j}(q))\in\mathcal{L}
, if
m(\Delta_{i}(p);\alpha)=1
, then
\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},p}(\Delta_{i}(p))=\{\Delta^{\prime}_{1},\Delta^{\prime}_{2}\}
satisfies that
(\Delta^{\prime}_{1},P^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})<(\Delta^{\prime}_{2},P^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
for any
P^{\prime}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P)
and
\Delta^{\prime}\in\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{j}(q))
.

Remark 9.20
.

Denote
r=[\sum m_{i}({\bf P})]_{+}
. Denote by
\{k_{1},\cdots,k_{r}\}\subset\{1,\cdots,\eta_{\alpha}+1\}
the set of indices that are not in any
{\bf m}({\bf P};\alpha)
-pair and
m_{k_{\ell}}({\bf P})=1
for
\ell=1,\cdots,r
. Suppose that
k_{1}<k_{2}<\cdots<k_{r}
. By Proposition
9.17
, we may write
\pi({\bf P})
as

\{{\bf P}(\vec{c})\mid\vec{c}\in\{0,1\}^{r}\},
to be precise, assume that
{\bf P}(\vec{c})=(\Delta^{\prime},P^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}),
for any
\ell\in\{1,\cdots,r\}
,

(i)

if
k_{\ell}\neq 0,\eta_{\alpha}+1
, then the edges labeled
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{3}
of
G^{\prime}(k_{j})
are in
P^{\prime}
in case
c_{j}=1
and the the edges labeled
\alpha_{2},\alpha_{4}
of
G^{\prime}(k_{j})
are in
P^{\prime}
in case
c_{j}=0
;

(ii)

if
k_{\ell}=0
, then
\Delta^{\prime}=\Delta^{\prime}_{1}
in case
c_{\ell}=0
and
\Delta^{\prime}=\Delta^{\prime}_{2}
in case
c_{\ell}=1
, where
\Delta^{\prime}_{1}
and
\Delta^{\prime}_{2}
are given by Lemma
9.19
;

(iii)

if
k_{\ell}=\eta_{\alpha}+1
, then
\Delta^{\prime\prime}=\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{1}
in case
c_{\ell}=0
and
\Delta^{\prime\prime}=\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{2}
in case
c_{\ell}=1
, where
\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{1}
and
\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{2}
are given by Lemma
9.18
.

Proposition 9.21
.

Suppose that
r=\sum m_{a}({\bf P})\geq 0
. Given any
k
, assume that
\vec{c},\vec{c}\hskip 2.27621pt^{\prime}
in
\{0,1\}^{r}
satisfy
c_{k}=1,c^{\prime}_{k}=0
and
c_{a}=c^{\prime}_{a}
for
a\neq k
, then
{\bf P}(\vec{c})
covers
{\bf P}(\vec{c}\hskip 2.27621pt^{\prime})
.

Proof.

Assume that
{\bf P}(\vec{c}\hskip 2.27621pt^{\prime})=(\Delta^{\prime}_{1},P^{\prime}_{1},\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{1})
and
{\bf P}(\vec{c})=(\Delta^{\prime}_{2},P^{\prime}_{2},\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{2})
. By Remark
9.20
, one of the following three cases happens: (1)
P^{\prime}_{1}=P^{\prime}_{2}
and
\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{1}=\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{2}
, (2)
\Delta^{\prime}_{1}=\Delta^{\prime}_{2}
and
\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{1}=\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{2}
, (3)
\Delta^{\prime}_{1}=\Delta^{\prime}_{2}
and
P^{\prime}_{1}=P^{\prime}_{2}
.

In case
P^{\prime}_{1}=P^{\prime}_{2}
and
\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{1}=\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{2}
, we have
(\Delta^{\prime}_{2},P^{\prime\prime}_{2},\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{2})=(\Delta^{\prime}_{2},P^{\prime\prime}_{1},\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{2})>(\Delta^{\prime}_{1},P^{\prime}_{1},\Delta^{\prime}_{1})
by Remark
9.20
(2). By Lemma
4.12
,
(\Delta^{\prime}_{2},P^{\prime\prime}_{2},\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{2})=(\Delta^{\prime}_{2},P^{\prime\prime}_{1},\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{2})
covers
(\Delta^{\prime}_{1},P^{\prime}_{1},\Delta^{\prime}_{1})
.

Similarly,
(\Delta^{\prime}_{2},P^{\prime\prime}_{2},\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{2})
covers
(\Delta^{\prime}_{1},P^{\prime}_{1},\Delta^{\prime}_{1})
in case
\Delta^{\prime}_{1}=\Delta^{\prime}_{2}
and
P^{\prime}_{1}=P^{\prime}_{2}
.

In case
\Delta^{\prime}_{1}=\Delta^{\prime}_{2}
and
\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{1}=\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{2}
, by Remark
9.20
(1),
P^{\prime}_{1}<P^{\prime}_{2}=\mu_{G^{\prime}_{l}}P^{\prime}_{1}
for some tile
G^{\prime}_{l}
with diagonal labeled
\alpha^{\prime}
. Otherwise, if
(\Delta^{\prime}_{1},\mu_{G^{\prime}_{l}}P^{\prime}_{1},\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{1})
does not cover
(\Delta^{\prime}_{1},P^{\prime}_{1},\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{1})
, by Lemma
4.11
, we have either
\Delta^{\prime}_{1}=\Delta^{\prime}_{1}(p)
is the first triangle in
\Delta(T^{\prime o},p)
and
G^{\prime}_{l}
is the first tile of
G_{T^{\prime o},\widetilde{\beta}}

\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{1}=\Delta^{\prime}_{1}(q)
is the first triangle in
\Delta(T^{\prime o},q)
and
G^{\prime}_{l}
is the last tile of
G_{T^{\prime o},\widetilde{\beta}}
.

Suppose that
\Delta^{\prime}_{1}=\Delta^{\prime}_{1}(p)
is the first triangle in
\Delta(T^{\prime o},p)
and
G^{\prime}_{l}
is the first tile of
G_{T^{\prime o},\widetilde{\beta}}
. It follows that the diagonal
G^{\prime}_{l}=G^{\prime}_{1}
is labeled
\alpha^{\prime}
and
m_{0}(\Delta^{\prime}_{1},P^{\prime}_{1},\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{1})=1
. Thus
m_{0}({\bf P})=-1
and
m_{1}({\bf P})=1
, thus
(0,1)
is an
{\bf m}({\bf P};\alpha)
-pair.
From the construction of
\pi
, we see that
(\Delta^{\prime}_{1},\mu_{G^{\prime}_{l}}P^{\prime}_{1},\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{1})
and
(\Delta^{\prime}_{1},P^{\prime}_{1},\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{1})
can not belong to
\pi({\bf P})
, a contradiction.

Suppose that
\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{1}=\Delta^{\prime}_{1}(q)
is the first triangle in
\Delta(T^{\prime o},q)
and
G^{\prime}_{l}
is the last tile of
G_{T^{\prime o},\widetilde{\beta}}
. Similarly,
m_{\eta_{\alpha}}({\bf P})=1
,
m_{\eta_{\alpha}+1}({\bf P})=-1
and
(\eta_{\alpha},\eta_{\alpha}+1)
can not be an
{\bf m}({\bf P};\alpha)
-pair. Thus,
\widetilde{\beta}=\alpha
,
\eta_{\alpha}=1
and
m_{0}({\bf P})=-1
with
(0,1=\eta_{\alpha})
is an
{\bf m}({\bf P};\alpha)
-pair. It follows that
r=\sum m_{a}({\bf P})=-1+1-1=-1
, contradicts to
r=\sum m_{a}({\bf P})\geq 0
.

The proof is complete.

\square

In summary of Propositions
9.4
,
9.8
,
9.17
,
9.12
,
9.21
and Remarks
9.5
,
9.10
,
9.20
, we obtain the following theorem.

Theorem 9.22
.

For any
{\bf P}\in\mathcal{L}
, we have

(1)

|\pi({\bf P})|=2^{[\sum m_{i}({\bf P})]_{+}}
and
{\bf m}(\bf P^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})=-{\bf m}(\bf P,\alpha)
for all
\bf P^{\prime}\in\pi(\bf P)
;

(2)

There is a bijective map
\{0,1\}^{[\sum m_{i}({\bf P})]_{+}}\to\pi({\bf P}),\vec{c}\mapsto{\bf P}(\vec{c})
such that
{\bf P}(\vec{c})
covers
{\bf P}(\vec{c}\hskip 2.27621pt^{\prime})
in
\mathcal{L}
if and only if
\vec{c}
covers
\vec{c}\hskip 2.27621pt^{\prime}
in the lattice
\{0,1\}^{[\sum m_{i}({\bf P})]_{+}}
.

We need the following lemma for later use.

Lemma 9.23
.

For any
{\bf P}\in\mathcal{L}
with
r=\sum m_{i}({\bf P};\alpha)>0
, for any
\ell\in\{1,\cdots,r\}
,
assume that
\vec{c},\vec{c}\hskip 2.27621pt^{\prime}
in
\{0,1\}^{r}
satisfy
c_{\ell}=1,c^{\prime}_{\ell}=0
and
c_{i}=c^{\prime}_{i}
for
i\neq\ell
,
we have

\Omega({\bf P}(\vec{c}\hskip 2.27621pt^{\prime}),{\bf P}(\vec{c}))=d(\alpha^{\prime})(2\ell-1-r).

Proof.

We have
\Omega({\bf P}(\vec{c}\hskip 2.27621pt^{\prime}),{\bf P}(\vec{c}))=d(\alpha^{\prime})\left(-(r-\ell)-(-\ell-1)\right)=d(\alpha^{\prime})(2\ell-1-r).

\square

Proof.

It follows by Lemma
7.16
and Lemma
8.10
.

\square

The main result in this section is the following.

Theorem 9.24
.

\pi
is a partition bijection with inverse
\pi^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By Theorem
9.22
,
\pi({\bf P})\neq\emptyset
for any
{\bf P}\in\mathcal{L}
.

If
\pi({\bf P})\cap\pi({\bf Q})\neq\emptyset
for some
{\bf P},{\bf Q}
, from the construction of
\pi
,
{\bf P}
and
{\bf Q}
are related by a sequence of twists at
\alpha
. By Theorem
9.22
, we have
{\bf m}(\bf P,\alpha)={\bf m}(\bf Q,\alpha)
. Thus
\pi(P)=\pi(Q)
by the construction of
\pi
.

For any
{\bf P}\in\mathcal{L},{\bf P}^{\prime}\in\pi({\bf P})
, we have
{\bf m}({\bf P},\alpha)
-pairs and
{\bf m}({\bf P}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})
-pairs coincide by Lemmas
9.3
,
9.7
,
9.16
and Theorem
9.22
. Thus
{\bf P}\in\pi^{\prime}({\bf P}^{\prime})
iff
{\bf P}^{\prime}\in\pi({\bf P})
. Therefore,
\bigcup_{{\bf P}\in\mathcal{L}}\pi({\bf P})=\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
.

Therefore, by Remark
2.5
,
\pi
is a partition bijection. Similarly,
\pi^{\prime}
is a partition bijection. Furthermore,
\pi^{\prime}
is the inverse of
\pi
by Proposition
2.6
.

\square

10.
Compatibility of the partition bijections and the lattice structures

For any pair
{\bf P},{\bf Q}\in\mathcal{L}
, assume that
{\bf P}
covers
{\bf Q}
, then we have that
{\bf P}
and
{\bf Q}
are either related by a twist on a tile
G_{l}
or related by two adjacent triangles incident to
p
or
q
, assume
\tau
is the label of the diagonal or the common side of the triangles. In both cases, we say that
{\bf P}
covers
{\bf Q}
and
related by
\tau
. We denote

\Omega({\bf P},{\bf Q})=w({\bf P})-w({\bf Q}).

We say that
{\bf P},{\bf Q}\in\mathcal{L}
are
related by a twist at
\alpha
if either
{\bf P}
covers
{\bf Q}
and related by
\tau
or
{\bf Q}
covers
{\bf P}
and related by
\tau
.

Assumption
: We always assume that
T
and
T^{\prime}
contain no arc tagged notched at
q
in case
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(q)})
,
T
and
T^{\prime}
contain no arc tagged notched at
p
or
q
in case
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)})
if there is no other state.

Proposition 10.1
.

We have
{\bf P}^{\prime}_{-}\in\pi({\bf P}_{-})
. In particular, we have
{\bf P}^{\prime}_{-}={\bf P}_{-}(0,\cdots,0)
.

Proof.

By Lemma
7.19
,
P^{\prime}_{-}\in\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P_{-})
. By Proposition
7.15
we may assume
\sum m_{i}(P_{-})\geq 0
. By Lemma
7.20
,
m_{i}(P_{-})\geq 0
for any
i
. We have
\Delta^{\prime}_{1}(q)\in\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{1}(q))
,
\Delta^{\prime}_{1}(p)\in\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},p}(\Delta_{1}(p))
from Lemma
8.8
.

The situation that
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta})
follows by Lemma
7.19
and Lemma
7.20
.

We then consider the situation that
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}(T^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(q)})
.

Case 1. If
m(\Delta_{1}(q);\alpha)\geq 0
, as
\Delta^{\prime}_{1}(q)\in\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{1}(q))
, we see that
(P^{\prime}_{-},\Delta^{\prime}_{1}(q))\in\pi(P_{-},\Delta_{1}(q))
.

Case 2. If
m(\Delta_{1}(q);\alpha)<0
, then
m(\Delta_{1}(q);\alpha)=-1
and
\tau_{1}(q)=\alpha
or
\tau_{t}(q)=\alpha
. Thus we have
\phi_{\alpha}^{T^{o},q}(\Delta_{1}(q))=\{\Delta^{\prime}_{1}(q)\}
.

Case 2.1.
\tau_{1}(q)=\alpha
. We may assume that
\tau_{t}(q)=\alpha_{4}
and
\tau_{[1]}(q)=\alpha_{1}
. We have either
\widetilde{\beta}=\tau_{t}(q)
or
\widetilde{\beta}
crosses
\alpha_{1}
and ending at
q
.

Case 2.1.1.
\widetilde{\beta}=\tau_{t}(q)
. Then
\widetilde{\beta}=\alpha_{4}\in T^{o}\cap T^{\prime o}
. Thus
P_{-}=P^{\prime}_{-}=P_{\widetilde{\beta}}
and
\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P_{-})=\{P^{\prime}_{-}\}
and thus
(P^{\prime}_{-},\Delta^{\prime}_{1}(q))\in\pi(P_{-},\Delta_{1}(q))
.

Case 2.1.2.
\widetilde{\beta}
crosses
\alpha_{1}
and ending at
q
.
If
\sum m_{i}(P_{-})=0
, then
\varphi_{\alpha}^{T^{o}}(P_{-})=\{P^{\prime}_{-}\}
and thus
(P^{\prime}_{-},\Delta^{\prime}_{1}(q))\in\pi(P_{-},\Delta_{1}(q))
. If
\sum m_{i}(P_{-})>0
, then
m_{i}(P_{-})=1
for some
i
. By Lemma
7.11
, one the following happens:
\widetilde{\beta}
crosses
\alpha_{2},\alpha,\alpha_{4}
consecutively; or
\widetilde{\beta}
starts from the common endpoint of
\alpha
and
\alpha_{1}
then crosses
\alpha_{4}
; or
\widetilde{\beta}
starts from
q
then crosses
\alpha_{2}
, see Figure
16
. We have either
\widetilde{\beta}
intersects itself or the two endpoints are
q
, a contradiction.

Figure 16.

\tau_{t}(q)=\alpha
, we may assume that

and

. We have either
\widetilde{\beta}=\alpha
or

\alpha_{4}

.

\widetilde{\beta}=\alpha
. Thus we have

,

. Thus we have

Case 2.1.
