.

12.2.
Proof of Theorem
6.14
in case
p\neq q

As
p\neq q
, we have
\widetilde{\beta}=\beta
,
\beta
and
\beta^{(p)}
are compatible. We may choose a tagged triangulation
T_{0}
such that
\beta,\beta^{(p)}\in T_{0}
and any arc
\gamma(\neq\beta^{(p)})\in T_{0}
does not tagged notched. Thus
T_{0}^{(p)}=T_{0}
. Let
T_{0}^{o}=l(T_{0})
be the corresponding ideal triangulation. Thus
l_{p}(\beta)\in T_{0}^{o}
and
\Delta=(\beta,\beta,l_{p}(\beta))
is a self-folded triangle in
T^{o}_{0}
. Denote
\Delta(T_{0}^{o},q)=\{\Delta_{1}(q),\cdots,\Delta_{t}(q)\}
. Then
\tau_{t-1}(q)=\tau_{1}(q)=l_{p}(\beta),\tau_{t}(q)=\tau_{[1]}(q)=\tau_{[t]}(q)=\beta
and
\tau_{j}[q]\neq\beta,l_{p}(\beta)
for any
j\in\{2,\cdots,t-1\}
.
Thus we have

\mathcal{L}:=\mathcal{L}(T^{o}_{0},\beta^{(p,q)})=\{\Delta\}\times\{P_{\beta}\}\times\{\Delta_{1}(q),\Delta_{3}(q),\cdots,\Delta_{t-3}(q),\Delta_{t}(q)\},

\mathcal{L}(T^{o}_{0},\beta^{(q)})=\{P_{\beta}\}\times\{\Delta_{1}(q),\Delta_{2}(q),\cdots,\Delta_{t-1}(q),\Delta_{t}(q)\}.

Figure 19.

For
j<t
, let

y^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))=y^{T_{0}}_{\beta}y^{T_{0}}_{\tau_{2}(q)}\cdots y^{T_{0}}_{\tau_{j-1}(q)},

and

y^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{t}(q))=y^{T_{0}}_{\beta}y^{T_{0}}_{\tau_{2}(q)}\cdots y^{T_{0}}_{\tau_{t-2}(q)}y^{T_{0}}_{\beta}.

We have the following lemma.

Lemma 12.12
.

For any
(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))\in\mathcal{L}
, we have

(1)

\sigma\mid_{v=1}\left(x^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))\right)=x^{T_{0},(p)}(P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))
,

(2)

\sigma\mid_{v=1}\left(y^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))\right)=y^{T_{0},(p)}(P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))
,

(3)

\sigma\left(X^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))\right)=X^{T_{0},(p)}(P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q)).

Proof.

(1) For
j=1,t
, we have
x^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))=(x_{l_{p}(\beta)}x_{\beta}^{-2})x_{\beta}(x_{\beta}x_{\beta}^{-1}x_{l_{p}(\beta)}^{-1})=x_{\beta}^{-1}
.
x^{T_{0},(p)}(P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))=x^{(p)}_{\beta}(x^{(p)}_{\beta}(x^{(p)}_{\beta})^{-1}(x^{(p)}_{l_{p}(\beta)})^{-1})=(x^{(p)}_{\beta^{(p)}})^{-1}
. Thus the result holds for
j=1,t
. For
j\neq 1,t
, we have
x^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))=(x_{l_{p}(\beta)}x_{\beta}^{-2})x_{\beta}x^{T_{0}}(\Delta_{j}(q))=x_{\beta^{(p)}}x^{T_{0}}(\Delta_{j}(q))
. Thus
\sigma\mid_{v=1}\left(x^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))\right)=x^{(p)}_{\beta}x^{T_{0},(p)}(\Delta_{j}(q))=x^{T_{0},(p)}(P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))
.

(2) Since
\tau_{j}[q]\neq\beta,l_{p}(\beta)
for any
j\in\{2,\cdots,t-1\}
, we have for
j<t

\sigma\mid_{v=1}\left(y^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q)\right)=y^{T_{0},(p)}_{\beta^{(p)}}y^{T_{0},(p)}_{\tau_{2}(q)}\cdots y^{T_{0},(p)}_{\tau_{j-1}(q)}=y^{T_{0},(p)}(P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q)),

\sigma\mid_{v=1}\left(y^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{t}(q)\right)=y^{T_{0},(p)}_{\beta^{(p)}}y^{T_{0},(p)}_{\tau_{2}(q)}\cdots y^{T_{0},(p)}_{\tau_{t-2}(q)}y^{T_{0},(p)}_{\beta^{(p)}}=y^{T_{0},(p)}(P_{\beta},\Delta_{t}(q)).

(3) It follows by (1) and (2).

\square

The following proposition follows immediately by Lemma
12.12
.

Proposition 12.13
.

With the foregoing notation. We have

\sum_{{\bf P}=(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))\in\mathcal{L}}v^{w(P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))}\sigma\left(X^{T_{0}}({\bf P})\right)=\sum_{{\bf P}\in\mathcal{L}(T_{0}^{o},\beta^{(q)})}v^{w({\bf P})}X^{T_{0},(p)}({\bf P}).

Proposition 12.14
.

Assume that
p\neq q
, for any ideal triangulation
T_{0}^{o}
such that
\beta,l_{q}(\beta)\in T_{0}^{o}
, let
\alpha=l_{q}(\beta)
and
T_{0}^{\prime o}=\mu_{\alpha}(T^{o}_{0})
. Then we have

\sum_{{\bf P}=(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))\in\mathcal{L}(T_{0}^{o},\beta^{(p,q)})}v^{w(P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))}X^{T_{0}}({\bf P})=\sum_{{\bf P}\in\mathcal{L}(T_{0}^{\prime o},\beta^{(p,q)})}v^{w({\bf P})}X^{T^{\prime}_{0}}({\bf P}).

Proof.

Assume
\alpha_{2}=\alpha_{3}=\beta
. Denote
\Delta^{\prime}_{1}=\{\beta,\alpha_{1},\alpha^{\prime}\}
and
\Delta^{\prime}_{2}=\{\beta,\alpha_{4},\alpha^{\prime}\}
. Assume that the endpoints of
\gamma
are
p
and
p^{\prime}
. We have the following two cases
p^{\prime}\neq q
and
p^{\prime}=q
.

Case I:
p^{\prime}\neq q
. Then we have
\Delta(T^{\prime o}_{0},q)=\{\Delta^{\prime}_{1},\Delta_{3}(q),\cdots,\Delta_{t-2}(q),\Delta^{\prime}_{2}\}
and
\Delta(T^{\prime o}_{0},p)=\{\Delta^{\prime}_{2},\Delta^{\prime}_{1}\}
. See Figure
19
. Then the following assignments

\pi(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{1}(q))=(\Delta^{\prime}_{2},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{2}),\;\;\;\pi(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{2}(q))=(\Delta^{\prime}_{2},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{1}),

\pi(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))=\{(\Delta^{\prime}_{2},P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q)),(\Delta^{\prime}_{1},P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))\}

for
3\leq j\leq t-2
,

\pi(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{t-1}(q))=(\Delta^{\prime}_{1},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{2}),\;\;\;\pi(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{t}(q))=(\Delta^{\prime}_{1},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{1})

give a partition bijection
\pi:\mathcal{L}(T_{0}^{o},\beta^{(p,q)})\to\mathcal{L}(T^{\prime o}_{0},\beta^{(p,q)})
.

We have

x^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{1}(q))=\frac{x_{\alpha}}{x_{\beta}^{2}}x_{\beta}\frac{x_{\beta}}{x_{\alpha}x_{\beta}}=\frac{1}{x_{\beta}},\;\;y^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{1}(q))=1,

x^{T^{\prime}_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime}_{2},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{2})=\frac{x_{\alpha_{4}}}{x_{\beta}x_{\alpha^{\prime}}}x_{\beta}\frac{x_{\alpha^{\prime}}}{x_{\alpha_{4}}x_{\beta}}=\frac{1}{x_{\beta}},\;\;y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime}_{2},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{2})=1.

Thus
x^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{1}(q))y^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{1}(q))=x^{T^{\prime}_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime}_{2},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{2})y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime}_{2},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{2}).

We have

x^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{2}(q))=\frac{x_{\alpha}}{x_{\beta}^{2}}x_{\beta}\frac{x_{\alpha_{4}}}{x_{\alpha}x_{\alpha_{1}}}=\frac{x_{\alpha_{4}}}{x_{\beta}x_{\alpha_{1}}},\;\;y^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{2}(q))=y^{T_{0}}_{\beta},

x^{T^{\prime}_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime}_{2},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{1})=\frac{x_{\alpha_{4}}}{x_{\beta}x_{\alpha^{\prime}}}x_{\beta}\frac{x_{\alpha^{\prime}}}{x_{\alpha_{1}}x_{\beta}}=\frac{x_{\alpha_{4}}}{x_{\beta}x_{\alpha_{1}}},\;\;y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime}_{2},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{1})=y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}_{\beta}.

Thus
x^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{2}(q))y^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{2}(q))=x^{T^{\prime}_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime}_{2},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{1})y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime}_{2},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{1}).

For
3\leq j\leq t-2
, we have

\begin{array}[]{rcl}x^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))y^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))&=&y^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))\frac{x_{\alpha}}{x_{\beta}^{2}}x_{\beta}\frac{x_{[j]}(q)}{x_{\tau_{j-1}(q)}x_{\tau_{j}(q)}}\\
&=&y^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))\frac{x_{\alpha}}{x_{\beta}}\frac{x_{[j]}(q)}{x_{\tau_{j-1}(q)}x_{\tau_{j}(q)}}.\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\sum\limits_{{\bf P^{\prime}}\in\pi(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))}x^{T^{\prime}_{0}}({\bf P^{\prime}})y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}({\bf P^{\prime}})&=&y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime}_{2},P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))(\frac{x_{\alpha_{4}}}{x_{\beta}x_{\alpha^{\prime}}}+y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}\frac{x_{\alpha_{1}}}{x_{\beta}x_{\alpha^{\prime}}})x_{\beta}\frac{x_{[j]}(q)}{x_{\tau_{j-1}(q)}x_{\tau_{j}(q)}}\\
&=&y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime}_{2},P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))\frac{x_{\alpha}(1+y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}_{\alpha^{\prime}})}{x_{\beta}^{2}}x_{\beta}\frac{x_{[j]}(q)}{x_{\tau_{j-1}(q)}x_{\tau_{j}(q)}}.\end{array}

By Lemma
6.3
(3)(a), we have
y^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))=y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime}_{2},P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))(1+y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}_{\alpha^{\prime}})
. Thus

x^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))y^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))=\sum\limits_{{\bf P^{\prime}}\in\pi(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))}x^{T^{\prime}_{0}}({\bf P^{\prime}})y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}({\bf P^{\prime}}).

We have

x^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{t-1}(q))=\frac{x_{\alpha}}{x_{\beta}^{2}}x_{\beta}\frac{x_{\alpha_{1}}}{x_{\beta}x_{\alpha_{4}}}=\frac{x_{\alpha_{1}}}{x_{\beta}x_{\alpha_{4}}},

y^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{t-1}(q))=y^{T_{0}}_{\beta}y^{T_{0}}_{\tau_{2}(q)}\cdots y^{T_{0}}_{\tau_{t-2}(q)},

x^{T^{\prime}_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime}_{1},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{2})=\frac{x_{\alpha_{1}}}{x_{\beta}x_{\alpha^{\prime}}}x_{\beta}\frac{x_{\alpha^{\prime}}}{x_{\alpha_{4}}x_{\beta}}=\frac{x_{\alpha_{1}}}{x_{\beta}x_{\alpha_{4}}},

y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime}_{1},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{2})=y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}_{\beta}y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}_{\tau_{2}(q)}\cdots y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}_{\tau_{t-2}(q)}y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}.

Since
\tau_{2}(q)=\alpha_{1},\tau_{t-2}(q)=\alpha_{4}
, we have
y^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{t-1}(q))=y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime}_{1},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{2})
by Lemma
6.3
(3)(a). Thus
x^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{t-1}(q))y^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{t-1}(q))=x^{T^{\prime}_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime}_{1},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{2})y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime}_{1},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{2}).

We have

x^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{t}(q))=\frac{x_{\alpha}}{x_{\beta}^{2}}x_{\beta}\frac{x_{\beta}}{x_{\beta}x_{\alpha}}=\frac{1}{x_{\beta}},

y^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{t}(q))=y^{T_{0}}_{\beta}y^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{t-1}(q)),

x^{T^{\prime}_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime}_{1},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{1})=\frac{x_{\alpha_{1}}}{x_{\beta}x_{\alpha^{\prime}}}x_{\beta}\frac{x_{\alpha^{\prime}}}{x_{\alpha_{1}}x_{\beta}}=\frac{1}{x_{\beta}},

y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime}_{1},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{1})=y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}_{\beta}y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime}_{2},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{1}).

Thus
y^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{t}(q))=y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime}_{2},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{1})
and

x^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{t}(q))y^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{t}(q))=x^{T^{\prime}_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime}_{1},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{1})y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime}_{1},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{1}).

From the above, we see that
\oplus_{{\bf P}\in\mathcal{L}(T_{0}^{o},\beta^{(p,q)})}y^{T_{0}}({\bf P})=\oplus_{{\bf P^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{L}(T^{\prime o}_{0},\beta^{(p,q)})}y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}({\bf P^{\prime}})
. Thus, we have
X^{T_{0}}({\bf P})=\sum_{{\bf P^{\prime}}\in\pi({\bf P})}X^{T^{\prime}_{0}}({\bf P^{\prime}})
for any
{\bf P}\in\mathcal{L}(T^{o}_{0},\beta^{(p,q)})
. To end the proof, it remains to prove that
w(P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))=w({\bf P^{\prime}})
for any
(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))\in\mathcal{L}(T^{o}_{0},\beta^{(p,q)})
and
{\bf P^{\prime}}\in\pi(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))
.

It is easy to see that
w(P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))=0
for all
j\in\{1,2,\cdots,t\}
and
w({\bf P}^{\prime})=0
for all
{\bf P}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}(T^{\prime o}_{0},\beta^{(p,q)})
.

Case II.
p^{\prime}=q
. Then there exists
k
such that
\tau_{k}(q)=\alpha_{1}
and
\tau_{k+1}(q)=\alpha_{4}
. See Figure
20
. Thus we have
\Delta(T^{\prime o}_{0},q)=\{\Delta^{\prime}_{1},\Delta_{3}(q),\cdots,\Delta_{k}(q),\Delta^{\prime}_{k+1},\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{k+1},\Delta_{k+2}(q),\cdots,\Delta_{t-2}(q),\Delta^{\prime}_{2}\}
and
\Delta(T^{\prime o}_{0},p)=\{\Delta^{\prime}_{2},\Delta^{\prime}_{1}\}
, where
\Delta^{\prime}_{k+1}=\Delta^{\prime}_{1}
with
m(\Delta^{\prime}_{k+1};\alpha^{\prime})=-1
and
\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{k+1}=\Delta_{2}^{\prime}
with
m(\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{k+1};\alpha^{\prime})=-1
.
Then the following assignments

\pi(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{1}(q))=(\Delta^{\prime}_{2},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{2}),\;\;\;\pi(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{2}(q))=(\Delta^{\prime}_{2},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{1}),

\pi(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{k+1}(q))=\{(\Delta^{\prime}_{1},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{k+1}),(\Delta^{\prime}_{1},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{k+1}),(\Delta^{\prime}_{2},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{k+1}),(\Delta^{\prime}_{2},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{k+1})\},

\pi(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))=\{(\Delta^{\prime}_{2},P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q)),(\Delta^{\prime}_{1},P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))\}

for
3\leq j\leq k
or
k+2\leq j\leq t-2
,

\pi(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{t-1}(q))=(\Delta^{\prime}_{1},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{2}),\;\;\;\pi(\Delta,P_{\beta},\Delta_{t}(q))=(\Delta^{\prime}_{1},P_{\beta},\Delta^{\prime}_{1})

give a partition bijection
\pi:\mathcal{L}(T_{0}^{o},\beta^{(p,q)})\to\mathcal{L}(T^{\prime o}_{0},\beta^{(p,q)})
.

Figure 20.

As Case I, we can similarly prove
X^{T_{0}}({\bf P})=\sum_{{\bf P^{\prime}}\in\pi({\bf P})}X^{T^{\prime}_{0}}({\bf P^{\prime}})
for any
{\bf P}\in\mathcal{L}(T^{o}_{0},\beta^{(p,q)})
and
w(P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))=0
for all
j\in\{1,2,\cdots,t\}
and
w({\bf P}^{\prime})=0
for all
{\bf P}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}(T^{\prime o}_{0},\beta^{(p,q)})
.

The proof is complete.

\square

Proof of Theorem
6.14
in case
p\neq q
Choose a tagged triangulation
T_{0}
contains
\beta,\beta^{(p)}
and any arc
\gamma(\neq\beta^{(p)})\in T_{0}
does not tagged notched. By Theorem
6.9
, we have

\sum_{(P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))\in\mathcal{L}(T_{0}^{o},\beta^{(q)})}v^{w(P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))}X^{(p)}(P_{\beta},\Delta_{j}(q))=X^{(p)}_{\beta^{(q)}}.

By Propositions
12.13
and
12.14
, we have Theorem
6.14
holds for
T^{\prime}_{0}
. For any ideal triangulation
T^{o}
such that the corresponding tagged triangulation
T
contains no arc tagged notched at
p
or
q
, we see Theorem
6.14
holds for
T
by Lemma
2.20
and Theorem
12.10
.

\square

12.3.
Proof of Theorem
6.14
in case
p=q

As
(S,M)
is not a once-punctured closed surface, we may choose an arc
\gamma
incident to
p
with another endpoint different from
p
and compatible with
\widetilde{\beta}
. Then we have
\gamma
and
\gamma^{(p)}
are compatible. We may choose a tagged triangulation
T_{0}
such that
\gamma,\gamma^{(p)}\in T_{0}
. Thus
T_{0}^{(p)}=T_{0}
. Let
T_{0}^{o}=l(T_{0})
be the corresponding ideal triangulation. Thus
l_{p}(\gamma)\in T_{0}^{o}
and
\Delta=(\gamma,\gamma,l_{p}(\gamma))
is a self-folded triangle in
T_{0}^{o}
.

Thus

\mathcal{L}(T^{o}_{0},\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)})=\{\Delta\}\times\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{o}_{0},\widetilde{\beta}})\times\{\Delta\},

\mathcal{L}(T^{o}_{0},\widetilde{\beta})=\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{o}_{0},\widetilde{\beta}}).

For any
(\Delta,P,\Delta)\in\mathcal{L}(T_{0}^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)})
, let
y^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P,\Delta)=\sigma\mid_{v=1}(y^{T_{0},(p)}(P))
.

The following result follows by Lemma
6.4
.

Lemma 12.15
.

Assume that
P>\mu_{G_{l}}P
. Then we have

\begin{array}[]{ccl}\frac{y^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P,\Delta)}{y^{T_{0}}(\Delta,Q,\Delta)}&=&\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}y^{T_{0}}_{\gamma},&\mbox{if $\tau_{i_{l}}=l_{q}(\gamma)$},\\
y^{T^{o}_{0}}_{\tau_{i_{1}}},&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{array}\right.\end{array}

Lemma 12.16
.

For any
(\Delta,P,\Delta)\in\mathcal{L}(T_{0}^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)})
, we have

(1)

\sigma\mid_{v=1}\left(x^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P,\Delta)\right)=x^{T_{0},(p)}(P)
,

(2)

\sigma\mid_{v=1}\left(y^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P,\Delta)\right)=y^{T_{0},(p)}(P)
,

(3)

\sigma\left(X^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P,\Delta)\right)=X^{T_{0},(p)}(P)
.

Proof.

(1) We have the west edge
E_{1}
and south edge
E_{2}
of the first tile of
G_{T_{0}^{o},\widetilde{\beta}}
are labeled
\gamma
. The east edge
F_{1}
and north edge
F_{2}
of the last tile of
G_{T_{0}^{o},\widetilde{\beta}}
are labeled
\gamma
. For any
P\in\mathcal{P}(G_{T_{0}^{o},\widetilde{\beta}})
, we have either
E_{1}\in P
or
E_{2}\in P
, either
F_{1}\in P
or
F_{2}\in P
. Thus
x^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P,\Delta)=\frac{x_{l_{p}(\gamma)}}{x_{\gamma}^{2}}x_{\gamma}Xx_{\gamma}\frac{x_{l_{p}(\gamma)}}{x_{\gamma}^{2}}=x_{\gamma^{(p)}}^{2}X
, where
X
is the cluster Laurent monomial corresponding to the edges except
E_{1},E_{2},F_{1},F_{2}
in
P
and the diagonals of
G_{T_{0}^{o},\widetilde{\beta}}
. Since
x_{\gamma}
is not a factor of
X
, we have

\sigma\mid_{v=1}\left(x^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P,\Delta)\right)=(x^{(p)}_{\gamma})^{2}X^{(p)}=x^{T_{0},(p)}(P)
.

(2) It follows by the definition of
y^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P,\Delta)
.

(3) It follows by (1) and (2).

\square

The following proposition follows immediately by Lemma
12.16
.

Proposition 12.17
.

With the foregoing notation. We have

\sum_{(\Delta,P,\Delta)\in\mathcal{L}(T_{0}^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)})}v^{w(P)}\sigma\left(X^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P,\Delta)\right)=\sum_{P\in\mathcal{L}(T_{0}^{o},\widetilde{\beta})}v^{w(P)}X^{T_{0},(p)}(P).

Proposition 12.18
.

Assume that
p=q
, let
\alpha=l_{q}(\gamma)
and
T^{\prime o}_{0}=\mu_{\alpha}(T^{o}_{0})
. Then we have

\sum_{(\Delta,P,\Delta)\in\mathcal{L}(T_{0}^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)})}v^{w(P)}X^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P,\Delta)=\sum_{{\bf P}\in\mathcal{L}(T_{0}^{\prime o},\beta^{(p,q)})}v^{w({\bf P})}X^{T^{\prime}_{0}}({\bf P}).

We will prove Proposition
12.18
in Section
12.4
.

Proof of Theorem
6.14
in case
p=q
.
By Theorem
6.5
, we have

\sum_{P\in\mathcal{L}(T^{o}_{0},\widetilde{\beta})}v^{w(P)}X^{T_{0},(p)}(P)=X^{(p)}_{\widetilde{\beta}}.

By Proposition
12.17
, we have
Theorem
6.14
holds for
T_{0}
. By Proposition
12.18
, Theorem
6.14
holds for
T^{\prime}_{0}
.
For any ideal triangulation
T^{o}
such that the corresponding tagged triangulation
T
contains no arc tagged notched at
p
, we see Theorem
6.14
holds for
T
by Lemma
2.20
and Theorem
12.10
.

The proof of Theorem
6.14
is complete.

\square

12.4.
Proof of Proposition
12.18
.

Assume that the tiles of
G_{T^{\prime o}_{0},\widetilde{\beta}}
are
G^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,G^{\prime}_{c}
with diagonals labeled
\tau^{\prime}_{i_{1}},\cdots,\tau^{\prime}_{i_{c}}
. Then
\tau^{\prime}_{i_{1}},\tau^{\prime}_{i_{c}}\in\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{4}\}
. Since
\gamma,\widetilde{\beta}
are compatible,
\widetilde{\beta}
crosses
T_{0}^{o}

c+2
times. Assume that the tiles of
G_{T_{0}^{o},\widetilde{\beta}}
are
G_{0},G_{1},\cdots,G_{c},G_{c+1}
with diagonals labeled
\tau_{i_{j}},j=0,\cdots,c+1
. Then
\tau_{i_{j}}=\tau^{\prime}_{i_{j}}
for
j=1,\cdots,c
and
\tau_{i_{0}},\tau_{i_{c+1}}=\alpha
. We may further assume that
rel(T_{0}^{o},G_{1})=rel(T_{0}^{o},G_{c})=rel(T^{\prime o}_{0},G^{\prime}_{1})=rel(T^{\prime o}_{0},G^{\prime}_{c})=1
. (Here the assumption implies
c
is odd, the result can be proved similarly in case
c
is even, the assumption fixes the labels of the edges of these tiles.)

Denote
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}(T_{0}^{o},\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)})
,
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}=\mathcal{L}(T^{\prime o}_{0},\widetilde{\beta}^{(p,q)})
.

By symmetry, we shall consider the following cases. Case I:
\tau^{\prime}_{i_{1}}=\alpha_{1},\tau^{\prime}_{i_{c}}=\alpha_{4}
; Case II:
\tau^{\prime}_{i_{1}}=\alpha_{1},\tau^{\prime}_{i_{c}}=\alpha_{1}
and Case III:
\tau^{\prime}_{i_{1}}=\alpha_{4},\tau^{\prime}_{i_{c}}=\alpha_{4}
.

We consider the Case I, as the other cases can be proved similarly.

In this case, the subgraph of
G_{T_{0}^{o},\widetilde{\beta}}
formed by
N(G_{1}),E(G_{1}),S(G_{c}),W(G_{c})
and
G_{2},\cdots,G_{c-1}
equals the subgraph of
G_{T^{\prime o}_{0},\widetilde{\beta}}
formed by
N(G^{\prime}_{1}),E(G^{\prime}_{1}),S(G^{\prime}_{c}),W(G^{\prime}_{c})
and
G^{\prime}_{2},\cdots,G^{\prime}_{c-1}
. See Figure
21
. Denote this common subgraph by
H
.

Figure 21.

The following lemma is immediate.

Lemma 12.19
.

For any
P\in\mathcal{P}(G_{T_{0}^{o},\widetilde{\beta}})
, there is a unique
P^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(G_{T^{\prime o}_{0},\widetilde{\beta}})
such that
P\cap edge(H)=P^{\prime}\cap edge(H)
. We denote
P\mid_{G_{T^{\prime o}_{0},\widetilde{\beta}}}=P^{\prime}
. In particular,
P_{-}\mid_{G_{T^{\prime o}_{0},\widetilde{\beta}}}=P^{\prime}_{-}
is the minimal perfect matching.

Denote
\Delta_{1}=\{\gamma,\alpha^{\prime},\alpha_{1}\}
and
\Delta_{2}=\{\gamma,\alpha^{\prime},\alpha_{4}\}
. Then the maximal/minimal element in
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
is
(\Delta_{1},P^{\prime}_{\pm},\Delta_{2})
. In this case the partition bijection
\pi:\mathcal{L}\to\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
has the following explicit construction: for any
{\bf P}=(\Delta,P,\Delta)\in\mathcal{L}
,

(i)

if
S(G_{1}),E(G_{c})\in P
, then
\pi({\bf P})=\{(\Delta_{1},P^{\prime},\Delta_{1}),(\Delta_{1},P^{\prime},\Delta_{2}),(\Delta_{2},P^{\prime},\Delta_{1}),(\Delta_{2},P^{\prime},\Delta_{2})\}
;

(ii)

If
S(G_{1})\in P,E(G_{c})\notin P
, then
\pi({\bf P})=\{(\Delta_{1},P^{\prime},\Delta_{1}),(\Delta_{2},P^{\prime},\Delta_{1})\}
in case
N(G_{c+1})\in P
and
\pi({\bf P})=\{(\Delta_{1},P^{\prime},\Delta_{2}),(\Delta_{2},P^{\prime},\Delta_{2})\}
in case
N(G_{c+1})\notin P
;

(iii)

If
S(G_{1})\notin P,E(G_{c})\in P
, then
\pi({\bf P})=\{(\Delta_{1},P^{\prime},\Delta_{1}),(\Delta_{1},P^{\prime},\Delta_{2})\}
in case
W(G_{0})\notin P
and
\pi({\bf P})=\{(\Delta_{2},P^{\prime},\Delta_{1}),(\Delta_{2},P^{\prime},\Delta_{2})\}
in case
W(G_{0})\in P
;

(iv)

If
S(G_{1}),E(G_{c})\notin P
, then
\pi({\bf P})=\{(\Delta_{1},P^{\prime},\Delta_{1})\}
in case
W(G_{0})\notin P,N(G_{c+1})\in P
,
\pi({\bf P})=\{(\Delta_{2},P^{\prime},\Delta_{1})\}
in case
W(G_{0}),N(G_{c+1})\in P
,
\pi({\bf P})=\{(\Delta_{1},P^{\prime},\Delta_{2})\}
in case
W(G_{0}),N(G_{c+1})\notin P
and
\pi({\bf P})=\{(\Delta_{2},P^{\prime},\Delta_{2})\}
in case
W(G_{0})\in P,N(G_{c+1})\notin P
,

where
P^{\prime}=P\mid_{G_{T^{\prime o}_{0},\widetilde{\beta}}}
.

The following is immediate.

Lemma 12.20
.

\pi(\Delta,P_{-},\Delta)
contains the minimal element
(\Delta_{1},P^{\prime}_{-},\Delta_{2})
.

Lemma 12.21
.

Assume that
{\bf P}=(\Delta,P,\Delta)\in\mathcal{L}
covers
{\bf Q}=(\Delta,\mu_{G_{l}}P,\Delta)\in\mathcal{L}
.

(1)

If
l=0
, then for any
(\Delta^{\prime},P^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime})\in\pi({\bf P})
we have
\Delta^{\prime}=\Delta_{2}
and

\pi({\bf Q})=\{(\Delta_{1},P^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime})\mid(\Delta^{\prime},P^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime})\in\pi({\bf P})\}.

Moreover,
{\bf P}(0,\cdots,0)
covers
{\bf Q}(0,\cdots,0)
and related by
\gamma
, and

\Omega(P,\mu_{G_{l}}P)=\Omega({\bf P}(0,\cdots,0),{\bf Q}(0,\cdots,0));

(2)

If
l=1
, then for any
(\Delta^{\prime},Q^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime})\in\pi({\bf Q})
we have
\Delta^{\prime}=\Delta_{2}
,
Q^{\prime}
can twist on
G^{\prime}_{1}
with
Q^{\prime}<\mu_{G^{\prime}_{1}}(Q^{\prime})
and

\pi({\bf P})=\{(\Delta_{1},\mu_{G^{\prime}_{1}}Q^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}),(\Delta_{2},\mu_{G^{\prime}_{1}}Q^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime})\mid(\Delta^{\prime},Q^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime})\in\pi({\bf Q})\}.

Moreover,
{\bf P}(0,\cdots,0)
covers
{\bf Q}(0,\cdots,0)
and related by
\alpha_{1}
, and

\Omega(P,\mu_{G_{l}}P)=\Omega({\bf P}(0,\cdots,0),{\bf Q}(0,\cdots,0));

(3)

If
2\leq l\leq c-1
, then for any
(\Delta^{\prime},P^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime})\in\pi({\bf P})
we have
P^{\prime}
can twist on
G^{\prime}_{l}
with
P^{\prime}>\mu_{G^{\prime}_{l}}(P^{\prime})
and

\pi({\bf Q})=\{(\Delta^{\prime},\mu_{G_{l}}P^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime})\mid(\Delta^{\prime},P^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime})\in\pi({\bf P})\}.

Moreover,
{\bf P}(0,\cdots,0)
covers
{\bf Q}(0,\cdots,0)
and related by
\tau_{i_{l}}
, and

\Omega(P,\mu_{G_{l}}P)=\Omega({\bf P}(0,\cdots,0),{\bf Q}(0,\cdots,0));

(4)

If
l=c
, then for any
(\Delta^{\prime},P^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime})\in\pi({\bf P})
we have
\Delta^{\prime\prime}=\Delta_{1}
,
P^{\prime}
can twist on
G^{\prime}_{c}
with
P^{\prime}>\mu_{G^{\prime}_{c}}(P^{\prime})
and

\pi({\bf Q})=\{(\Delta^{\prime},\mu_{G^{\prime}_{c}}P^{\prime},\Delta_{1}),(\Delta^{\prime},\mu_{G^{\prime}_{c}}P^{\prime},\Delta_{2})\mid(\Delta^{\prime},Q^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime})\in\pi({\bf P})\}.

Moreover,
{\bf P}(1,\cdots,1)
covers
{\bf Q}(1,\cdots,1)
and related by
\alpha_{1}
, and

\Omega(P,\mu_{G_{l}}P)=\Omega({\bf P}(1,\cdots,1),{\bf Q}(1,\cdots,1));

(5)

If
l=c+1
, then for any
(\Delta^{\prime},P^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime})\in\pi({\bf P})
we have
\Delta^{\prime\prime}=\Delta_{2}
and

\pi({\bf Q})=\{(\Delta^{\prime},P^{\prime},\Delta_{1})\mid(\Delta^{\prime},P^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime})\in\pi({\bf P})\}.

Moreover,
{\bf P}(0,\cdots,0)
covers
{\bf Q}(0,\cdots,0)
and related by
\gamma
, and

\Omega(P,\mu_{G_{l}}P)=\Omega({\bf P}(0,\cdots,0),{\bf Q}(0,\cdots,0)).

Proof.

The relation between
\pi({\bf P})
and
\pi({\bf Q})
follows immediately by the construction of
\pi
.

(1) Since
W(G^{\prime}_{1})\in P^{\prime}
, we have
{\bf P}(0,\cdots,0)
covers
{\bf Q}(0,\cdots,0)
and related by
\gamma
. If
E(G_{c})\in P
, then
\Omega(P,\mu_{G_{l}}P)=0
and
E(G^{\prime}_{c})\in P^{\prime}
. Thus

\Omega({\bf P}(0,\cdots,0),{\bf Q}(0,\cdots,0))=1+m(\Delta_{2};\gamma)=1-1=0.

If
E(G_{c})\notin P
, then
\Omega(P,\mu_{G_{l}}P)=-1
and
S(G^{\prime}_{c})\in P^{\prime}
. Thus

\Omega({\bf P}(0,\cdots,0),{\bf Q}(0,\cdots,0))=0+m(\Delta_{1};\gamma)=0-1=-1.

(2) Since
l=1
, we have
S(G^{\prime}_{1})\in P^{\prime}
. Thus
{\bf P}(0,\cdots,0)
covers
{\bf Q}(0,\cdots,0)
and related by
\alpha_{1}
. If
N(G_{c})\in P
, then
\Omega(P,\mu_{G_{l}}P)=1
and
N(G_{c+1})\in P,N(G^{\prime}_{c})\in P^{\prime}
. Thus
{\bf P}(0,\cdots,0)=(\Delta_{2},\mu_{G^{\prime}_{1}}Q^{\prime},\Delta_{1})
and

\Omega({\bf P}(0,\cdots,0),{\bf Q}(0,\cdots,0))=m(\Delta_{1};\alpha_{1})-m(\Delta_{2};\alpha_{1})=1-0=1.

If
N(G_{c})\notin P
, then
\Omega(P,\mu_{G_{l}}P)=0
and
E(G_{c})\in P
in case
N(G_{c+1})\in P
. Thus
{\bf P}(0,\cdots,0)=(\Delta_{2},\mu_{G^{\prime}_{1}}Q^{\prime},\Delta_{2})
and

\Omega({\bf P}(0,\cdots,0),{\bf Q}(0,\cdots,0))=m(\Delta_{2};\alpha_{1})-m(\Delta_{2};\alpha_{1})=0.

(3) Since
2\leq l\leq c-1
, we have
{\bf P}(0,\cdots,0)
covers
{\bf Q}(0,\cdots,0)
and related by
\tau_{i_{l}}
, and

\Omega(P,\mu_{G_{l}}P)=\Omega({\bf P}(0,\cdots,0),{\bf Q}(0,\cdots,0))=0.

(4) and (5) are similar to (2) and (1).

\square

Lemma 12.22
.

Assume that
{\bf P}=(\Delta,P,\Delta)\in\mathcal{L}
covers
{\bf Q}=(\Delta,\mu_{G_{l}}P,\Delta)\in\mathcal{L}
. Then

\frac{y^{T_{0}}({\bf P})}{y^{T_{0}}({\bf Q})}=\frac{\bigoplus_{{\bf P}^{\prime}\in\pi({\bf P})}y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}({\bf P}^{\prime})}{\bigoplus_{{\bf Q}^{\prime}\in\pi({\bf Q})}y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}({\bf Q}^{\prime})}.

Proof.

If
l=0
, then by Lemmas
12.15
,
6.11
and
12.21
we have

\frac{y^{T_{0}}({\bf P})}{y^{T_{0}}({\bf Q})}=y^{T_{0}}_{\gamma}=y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}_{\gamma}=\frac{\bigoplus_{{\bf P}^{\prime}\in\pi({\bf P})}y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}({\bf P}^{\prime})}{\bigoplus_{{\bf Q}^{\prime}\in\pi({\bf Q})}y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}({\bf Q}^{\prime})}.

If
l=1
, then by Lemmas
12.15
,
6.3
,
6.11
and
12.21
we have

\frac{y^{T_{0}}({\bf P})}{y^{T_{0}}({\bf Q})}=y^{T^{o}_{0}}_{\alpha_{1}}=y^{T^{\prime o}_{0}}_{\alpha_{1}}(1\oplus y^{T^{\prime o}_{0}}_{\alpha^{\prime}})=\frac{\bigoplus_{{\bf P}^{\prime}\in\pi({\bf P})}y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}({\bf P}^{\prime})}{\bigoplus_{{\bf Q}^{\prime}\in\pi({\bf Q})}y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}({\bf Q}^{\prime})}.

If
2\leq l\leq c-1
, then by Lemmas
6.11
and
12.21
we have

\frac{y^{T_{0}}({\bf P})}{y^{T_{0}}({\bf Q})}=y^{T^{o}_{0}}_{\tau_{i_{l}}}=y^{T^{\prime o}_{0}}_{\tau_{i_{l}}}=\frac{\bigoplus_{{\bf P}^{\prime}\in\pi({\bf P})}y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}({\bf P}^{\prime})}{\bigoplus_{{\bf Q}^{\prime}\in\pi({\bf Q})}y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}({\bf Q}^{\prime})}.

If
l=c
, then by Lemmas
12.15
,
6.3
,
6.11
and
12.21
we have

\frac{y^{T_{0}}({\bf P})}{y^{T_{0}}({\bf Q})}=y^{T^{o}_{0}}_{\alpha_{4}}=y^{T^{\prime o}_{0}}_{\alpha_{4}}\frac{1}{y^{T^{\prime o}_{0}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}(1\oplus y^{T^{\prime o}_{0}}_{\alpha^{\prime}})=\frac{\bigoplus_{{\bf P}^{\prime}\in\pi({\bf P})}y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}({\bf P}^{\prime})}{\bigoplus_{{\bf Q}^{\prime}\in\pi({\bf Q})}y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}({\bf Q}^{\prime})}.

If
l=c+1
, then by Lemmas
12.15
,
6.11
and
12.21
we have

\frac{y^{T_{0}}({\bf P})}{y^{T_{0}}({\bf Q})}=y^{T_{0}}_{\gamma}=y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}_{\gamma}=\frac{\bigoplus_{{\bf P}^{\prime}\in\pi({\bf P})}y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}({\bf P}^{\prime})}{\bigoplus_{{\bf Q}^{\prime}\in\pi({\bf Q})}y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}({\bf Q}^{\prime})}.

The proof is complete.

\square

Lemma 12.23
.

Assume that
{\bf P}=(\Delta,P,\Delta)\in\mathcal{L}
covers
{\bf Q}=(\Delta,\mu_{G_{l}}P,\Delta)\in\mathcal{L}
. Then we have

w(P)=w({\bf P}(0,\cdots,0))=w({\bf P}(1,\cdots,1))
.

Proof.

It follows by Lemmas
12.20
and
12.21
.

\square

Proposition 12.24
.

For any
{\bf P}=(\Delta,P,\Delta)\in\mathcal{L}
, we have

v^{w(P)}X^{T_{0}}({\bf P})=\sum\limits_{{\bf P^{\prime}}\in\pi({\bf P})}v^{w({\bf P^{\prime}})}X^{T^{\prime}_{0}}({\bf P^{\prime}}).

Proof.

For
{\bf P}=(\Delta,P_{-},\Delta)
, we have
W(G_{0}),S(G_{1})\notin P,E(G_{c}),N(G_{c+1})\in P
. Then

x^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P,\Delta)=\frac{x_{\alpha}}{x^{2}_{\gamma}}x_{\gamma}x^{-1}_{\alpha}x(P\mid_{H})x^{-1}_{\alpha_{4}}x_{\gamma}\frac{x_{\alpha}}{x^{2}_{\gamma}}=x(P\mid_{H})\frac{x_{\alpha}}{x^{2}_{\gamma}x_{\alpha_{4}}},\;\;y^{T_{0}}(\Delta,P,\Delta)=1,

x^{T^{\prime}_{0}}(\Delta_{1},P^{\prime}_{-},\Delta_{1})=\frac{x_{\alpha_{1}}}{x_{\gamma}x_{\alpha^{\prime}}}x_{\alpha^{\prime}}x^{-1}_{\alpha_{1}}x(P^{\prime}_{-}\mid_{H})x^{-1}_{\alpha_{4}}x_{\gamma}\frac{x_{\alpha_{1}}}{x_{\gamma}x_{\alpha^{\prime}}}=x(P^{\prime}_{-}\mid_{H})\frac{x_{\alpha_{1}}}{x_{\alpha_{4}}x_{\gamma}x_{\alpha^{\prime}}},

x^{T^{\prime}_{0}}(\Delta_{1},P^{\prime}_{-},\Delta_{2})=x^{T^{\prime}_{0}}(\Delta_{1},P^{\prime}_{-},\Delta_{1})\frac{x_{\alpha_{4}}}{x_{\alpha_{1}}}=x(P^{\prime}_{-}\mid_{H})\frac{1}{x_{\gamma}x_{\alpha^{\prime}}},

y^{T_{0}}(\Delta_{1},P^{\prime}_{-},\Delta_{1})=y^{T_{0}}_{\alpha^{\prime}},y^{T_{0}}(\Delta_{1},P^{\prime}_{-},\Delta_{2})=1.

where
x(P\mid_{H})
(resp.
x(P^{\prime}_{-}\mid_{H})
) is the Laurent monomial determined by the edges
P\cap edge(H)
(resp.
P^{\prime}_{-}\cap edge(H)
) and the diagonals of
H
.
Thus,

x^{T_{0}}({\bf P})y^{T_{0}}({\bf P})=\frac{1}{\oplus_{{\bf P^{\prime}}\in\pi({\bf P})}y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}({\bf P^{\prime}})}\sum_{{\bf P^{\prime}}\in\pi({\bf P})}x^{T^{\prime}_{0}}({\bf P^{\prime}})y^{T^{\prime}_{0}}({\bf P^{\prime}}).

Then the proof is similar to Proposition
12.9
by using Lemma
12.22
.

\square

Proof of Proposition
12.18
.
It follows immediately by Proposition
12.24
.
